Proven by `GlobalTokenRevocationServerTests.ARevocationWhoseAuthorizationFieldCarriesNoAccessTokenIsChallengedWithoutAnErrorCode` (an empty and a white-space line, a refusing seam: 401, an empty body, `Bearer realm="https://gtr.issuer.test/"`; without the endpoint's exception for such a line the answer is 400 `invalid_request`, `MalformedAuthorizationField` and `Bearer realm="https://gtr.issuer.test/", error="invalid_request"`), the pin `GlobalTokenRevocationServerTests.AnAuthorizationLineWithoutACredentialReachesTheSeamAsAReadingWithoutCredentials` (an accepting seam receives `HasCredentials` false, `IsAuthorizationMalformed` true and `ClientId` null), `DynamicRegistrationEndToEndTests.AClientConfigurationRequestWhoseAuthorizationFieldCarriesNoBearerTokenIsChallenged` (the client configuration endpoint: 401 with the challenge), `GlobalTokenRevocationServerTests.AnEmptyAuthorizationLineOfAMetadataDocumentClientIsChallengedWithoutAnErrorCode`, `ClientIdMetadataDocumentAdversarialFlowTests.AnEmptyAuthorizationLineAtUserInfoIsChallengedWithoutAnErrorCodeAsync` and `ClientIdMetadataDocumentAdversarialFlowTests.TwoAuthorizationLinesAtUserInfoAreAnsweredWithTheBearerChallengeAloneAsync` (a Client ID Metadata Document client: 401 with the challenge and no error code, and 400 with the `Bearer` challenge alone), and `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.AnEmptyAuthorizationLineBesideAValidSecretIsAnInvalidRequest` (the token endpoint: 400 `invalid_request`). Where the integration requires DPoP-bound access tokens (`IsDpopBoundAccessTokenRequiredAtResources`), UserInfo and the Credential, Deferred Credential and Notification endpoints answer a request without credentials, a malformed line and any `Bearer` presentation with the `DPoP` challenge alone and no `error`, and a repeated field with the `DPoP` challenge alone carrying `invalid_request`, before any token is read (the entry for `7ae07edc` below).
  - **Deviations.** Each is named with its reason.
    - [RFC 7662 Section 2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7662#section-2.3) says "If the protected resource uses OAuth 2.0 client credentials to authenticate to the introspection endpoint and its credentials are invalid, the authorization server responds with an HTTP 401 (Unauthorized) as described in Section 5.2 of OAuth 2.0 [RFC6749]." At a server that advertises no `client_secret_basic`, a failed form authentication at introspection is answered 400. The reason: such a server has no challenge to send, and a 401 without one breaks RFC 9110 Section 15.5.2. Proven by `TokenIntrospectionServerTests.IntrospectionEndpointRejectsUnauthenticatedCallerWithoutTouchingTheStore` (no credential: 400 `invalid_client`, no `WWW-Authenticate`) and the pin `TokenIntrospectionServerTests.AWrongFormSecretAtAServerWithoutBasicIsABadRequestWithoutAChallenge` (a wrong `client_secret` over the wire: 400 `invalid_client`, the literal body, no `WWW-Authenticate`).
    - A request whose issuer does not resolve has no realm, and its failed authentication is answered 400 with no challenge, also when it attempted the `Authorization` field, where [RFC 6749 Section 5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-5.2) says: "If the client attempted to authenticate via the "Authorization" request header field, the authorization server MUST respond with an HTTP 401 (Unauthorized) status code and include the "WWW-Authenticate" response header field matching the authentication scheme used by the client." The issuer does not resolve when no application resolver is wired and neither the registration nor the context names an issuer of the issuer shape, and when the application's resolver answers none, also while the context holds an issuer an earlier step placed there, which the token endpoint's resolution does not use. The reason: a challenge without a realm breaks RFC 7617 Section 2, and a 401 without a challenge breaks RFC 9110 Section 15.5.2. Proven by the pin `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.AFailedBasicAttemptWhereNoIssuerResolvesIsABadRequestWithoutAChallenge` (no issuer resolver, no issuer on the registration or the context, a wrong secret in `Authorization: Basic`: 400 `invalid_client`, no `WWW-Authenticate`), `ClientIdMetadataDocumentAdversarialFlowTests.AFailedDocumentFetchUnderAnIssuerOutsideTheIssuerShapeIsABadRequestWithoutAChallengeAsync` (the materialization's refusal of a failed fetch with `Authorization: Basic`, no resolver, a registration issuer of the `http` scheme: 400 `invalid_client`, no `WWW-Authenticate`) and `ClientIdMetadataDocumentAdversarialFlowTests.AFailedDocumentFetchWhereTheIssuerResolverAnswersNoneIsABadRequestWithoutAChallengeAsync` (the same where the resolver answers none and the context carries a preset issuer: 400 `invalid_client`, no `WWW-Authenticate`).
    - [OpenID Federation 1.0 Section 8.9](https://openid.net/specs/openid-federation-1_0.html#section-8.9) defines `invalid_client` as "The Client cannot be authorized or is not a valid participant of the federation." and continues "The HTTP response status code SHOULD be 401 (Unauthorized)." A failed client authentication at a federation endpoint that did not use the `Authorization` field, at a server that advertises no `client_secret_basic`, is answered 400. The reason: such a server has no challenge applicable to the endpoint, and a 401 without one breaks RFC 9110 Section 15.5.2. Proven by `FederationFetchEndpointTests.FetchEndpointRejectsWhenClientAuthenticationFails`.
  - Proven by, over the loopback listener: `AuthCodeClientAuthenticationTests.ARepeatedClientAssertionIsAnInvalidRequest` (400 `invalid_request`, the literal body), `ClientCredentialReadingTests`.
- **Authorization-code lifecycle: hash at rest, claims, replay and reuse.** Authorization redirects carry the raw code; `ServerCodeIssuedState` persists its SHA-256 base64url `CodeHash`. Token correlation hashes the presented code, so the stored hash is not accepted as the wire code. `BeforeCodeRedemptionCorrelationAsync`, the endpoint's pre-correlation step, runs identification, issuer resolution, the `code_verifier`'s presence and grammar, declared client authentication, the request-only `client_id` rule, the request-only `resource` and `authorization_details` decisions, and DPoP — all before the presented code is ever looked up. `VerifyCodeGrantPresentation` then runs the checks that need the loaded code state — the PKCE digest, the grant-binding comparison, and the `redirect_uri` comparison — before claiming the loaded flow step and minting. PAR authorization claims its request URI in the same way. A valid code replay re-verifies through `VerifyCodeGrantPresentation`, revokes the original issuance audit, then walks the refresh family rooted at `ServerTokenIssuedState.RefreshFlowId` through retired links to the live token. It records `RevokedAt` through the pure `ServerAuthorizationCodeReplayDetected` transition only after that work; sequential repeats skip revocation. Invalid code presentations revoke nothing (OAuth 2.1 draft-16 §7.5.3: "The authorization server SHOULD NOT revoke any issued tokens when receiving a replayed authorization code that contains invalid parameters"). The Pre-Authorized Code grant's own stepless `authorization_details` resolution no longer runs the OID4VCI `locations` decision (and with it issuer resolution and Credential Issuer metadata contribution) when the grant carries `authorization_details` but the token request does not — that decision was already unread on this path; no wire answer changes.

- **Refresh rotation and reuse.** `BeforeRefreshCorrelationAsync`, the endpoint's pre-correlation step, carries identification, issuer resolution, declared client authentication, and the request-only `client_id` rule — OAuth 2.1 draft-16 §4.3.1's stored-grant binding rule ("if client authentication is included in the request, ensure that the refresh token was issued to the authenticated client, OR if a client_id is included in the request, ensure the refresh token was issued to the matching client") read fail-closed on neither identity (`invalid_request`). `VerifyRefreshClient` then keeps only the record-dependent half, once the record is loaded: the bound-client comparison. Validated credentials identify the registration; without credentials the form must identify the client. Confidential clients must authenticate. Both the live and reuse paths call `DpopTokenEndpointValidation.BindValidatedProofAsync`, reading the outcome the step's own `ValidatePresentedProofAsync` call already carried, and require a matching proof for a bound token. Rotation claims the loaded step before minting, side-saves a fresh refresh state, and retires the presented record with `SuccessorRefreshFlowId`. Its `ExpiresAt` is the maximum of issued-token expiry and successor refresh expiry. `PredecessorFlowId` on each live refresh state names the flow that audited its paired access token: the code flow initially, the presented refresh flow at rotation. The retirement transition preserves that backlink.

- **Every token-issuing grant applies the registration profile's DPoP mandate.** The authorization-code and refresh grants no longer call `DpopTokenEndpointValidation.ValidateAsync` at all: each instead runs the request-only half of DPoP in its own pre-correlation step (`ValidatePresentedProofAsync`, with `proofRequiredByRegistration = ClientPolicyProfiles.RequiresDpop(registration.Profile)` — the refresh grant's request-only half derives from the REGISTRATION's profile here, exactly like every other grant, so a DPoP-mandating profile presenting any refresh record without a proof is challenged), and the record's own `cnf.jkt` binding is applied afterwards, in the handler, by `BindValidatedProofAsync`. `ValidateAsync` itself runs with `dpopRequired = ClientPolicyProfiles.RequiresDpop(registration.Profile)` at its four remaining callers — the stepless grants without a pre-correlation step: Pre-Authorized Code, `client_credentials`, token exchange, and JWT Bearer. `RequiresDpop` returns `true` under `PolicyProfile.Fapi20` — [FAPI 2.0 Security Profile §5.3.2.1](https://openid.net/specs/fapi-security-profile-2_0-final.html#section-5.3.2.1): the authorization server "shall only issue sender-constrained access tokens" — and under `PolicyProfile.Haip10`, whose [§4](https://openid.net/specs/openid4vc-high-assurance-interoperability-profile-1_0-final.html#section-4) requires DPoP support of Wallets and Credential Issuers and which the library treats as the same mandate. Under either, a request presenting no proof is answered with a `use_dpop_nonce` challenge carrying a `DPoP-Nonce` header, and a proof without the server's nonce is challenged once. Every grant runs the validation before its first step that can consume a single-use artifact — the authorization code's and the refresh token's claim, the pre-authorized code seam, the JWT Bearer assertion seam and the assertion's `jti` record, the token exchange's subject-token, actor-token and authorization seams — because the retry [RFC 9449 §8](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-8) prescribes presents the same request: a challenge or a rejected proof must not burn the artifact (`RedeemCompletesAfterOneNonceRetryOverRealWireUnderMandatingRegistration`, `RedeemWithInvalidDpopProofDoesNotConsumeTheAssertion`, `RegistrationRequiringDpopChallengeDoesNotConsultTheSubjectSeamBeforeTheRetry`; the replay defense after a complete redemption stays pinned by `RedeemRejectsReplayedJti`). A presented proof binds the token (`IssuanceContext.Confirmation`, `cnf.jkt`, `token_type=DPoP` per [RFC 9449 §5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-5)). On the token exchange and JWT Bearer grants, `IdJagDpopDecision.Evaluate` runs its proof-of-possession matrix where its inputs exist, after the seams, reading the outcome of the validation that ran before them and combining the presented proof's key thumbprint with the grant's own bound thumbprint and the Resource Server's sender-constraint requirement. Proven by `TokenExchangeGrantTests.RegistrationRequiringDpopWithoutAProofIsRefused`, `JwtBearerGrantTests.RegistrationRequiringDpopWithoutAProofIsRefused`, `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.RegistrationRequiringDpopWithoutAProofIsRefused`, `Oid4VciPreAuthorizedCodeGrantTests.RegistrationRequiringDpopWithoutAProofIsRejectedWithoutConsultingTheSeam`, `DpopEndToEndTests.TokenIssuanceAndResourceCallValidateUnderDpopProtocol`, and the refresh grant's own `DpopBoundRefreshTests.DpopSenderConstrainedRefreshTokenRefreshesThroughRealClientAsync` / `NonDpopClientStillRefreshesThroughRealClientAsync` / `DpopMandatingProfileChallengesAnUnboundRefreshRecordObtainedBeforeTheProfileMandatedItAsync` (a registration-profile alteration to a DPoP-mandating profile, after a Bearer refresh record already exists, is challenged on the record's own profile, not its stored binding).

- **DPoP delegate wiring is all-or-nothing, checked wherever a DPoP decision can be reached.** `DpopTokenEndpointValidation`'s three entry points — `ValidateAsync`, `ValidatePresentedProofAsync`, and `BindValidatedProofAsync`'s bound-record/no-proof branch — each check the SAME five delegates (`ValidateDpopProofAsync`, `IssueDpopNonceAsync`, `ValidateDpopNonceAsync`, `ResolveServerHmacKeyAsync`, `GetHmacKeySetAsync`) before issuing a nonce challenge or accepting a proof, answering `server_error` when any one is missing rather than issuing a nonce the server could never validate a retried proof against, or silently skipping a check a partial wiring left out. Proven by `DpopBoundRefreshTests.BoundRefreshWithoutAProofAndPartiallyWiredDpopFailsClosedAsync`.

- **The Pre-Authorized Code grant validates DPoP before the code seam.** `AuthCodeEndpoints.BuildPreAuthorizedCodeToken` runs `DpopTokenEndpointValidation.ValidateAsync` before `AuthorizationServerIntegration.ValidatePreAuthorizedCodeAsync`, because the pre-authorized code is single-use and the seam may consume it: a `use_dpop_nonce` challenge or a rejected proof must not consume it, so the Wallet retries the same code once it satisfies the challenge. A presented proof's confirmation rides the DPoP outcome's `Confirmation` into `IssuanceContext.Confirmation`, and the response `token_type` follows it. Proven by `NonceChallengeRunsBeforeTheSeamAndTheRetryWithTheSameCodeSucceeds`, `PresentedProofBindsTheAccessTokenAndAnswersDpopTokenType`, `LongLivedAccessTokenIsIssuedWithAProofAndRefusedWithoutOne`, `ProofWithWrongHtuIsRejectedWithoutConsultingTheSeam`, `ProofWithWrongHtmIsRejectedWithoutConsultingTheSeam`, and `ReplayedJtiIsRejectedWithoutConsultingTheSeamASecondTime`. This grant has no [RFC 8707 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2) `resource` parameter, so its access token's audience is the answer of `AuthorizationServerIntegration.ResolveAccessTokenAudienceAsync` when the application wires one, else the union of the `ScopeToAudience` entries of the scopes it grants (the grant keeps its credential scopes and drops only the identity scopes, having no End-User), and the producer's default, the issuer, only when neither the resolver nor the granted scopes' mappings name an audience. The Credential, Deferred Credential and Notification endpoints accept a token whose audience holds the issuer, so an application that audiences the token elsewhere, through its resolver or its scope map, names the issuer among the audiences of a token that is to reach those endpoints.

- **A Credential Request is held to the Credential Issuer catalogue and to what its Access Token granted.** The Credential Endpoint materializes only when `AuthorizationServerIntegration.ContributeCredentialIssuerMetadataAsync` is wired beside the request parser and the issuance seam, as the Credential Issuer Metadata endpoint does, so no request is served against an unknown catalogue ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 12.2.4](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-12.2.4): "credential_configurations_supported: REQUIRED. Object that describes specifics of the Credential that the Credential Issuer supports issuance of."). The catalogue is read once per request and that one answer is handed to every rule that reads it (the encryption requirements, the scope binding, the key attestation and the batch ceiling), so a catalogue the application alters while a request is served judges the request once. The Deferred Credential Endpoint requires the same seam beside `ResolveDeferredCredentialAsync`, and at both endpoints a request asking for an encrypted response from a catalogue advertising no `credential_response_encryption` is refused `invalid_encryption_parameters` with a fixed description ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 8.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-8.2): "The Credential Issuer indicates support for encrypted responses by including the credential_response_encryption parameter in the Credential Issuer Metadata." [Section 8.3.1.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-8.3.1.2): "invalid_encryption_parameters: This error occurs when the encryption parameters in the Credential Request are either invalid or missing."). Both endpoints judge a request only against a catalogue the Credential Issuer Metadata endpoint publishes: right after the one read, the catalogue passes `CredentialIssuerMetadataValidation.Validate`, the one body that decides what that endpoint publishes, and a catalogue it refuses (a configuration without `format`, a proof type without `proof_signing_alg_values_supported`, a `batch_size` below 2, an encryption object lacking a REQUIRED member or carrying `encryption_required` as anything but a boolean, a malformed `display` entry) is answered exactly as that endpoint answers it, 500 `server_error` with the same body, before any rule reads it and with neither the issuance nor the deferred seam reached: the request is the server's own fault ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 12.2.4](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-12.2.4), on `credential_response_encryption`: "encryption_required: REQUIRED. Boolean value specifying whether the Credential Issuer requires the additional encryption on top of TLS for the Credential Response." [RFC 9110 Section 15.6.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-15.6.1): "The 500 (Internal Server Error) status code indicates that the server encountered an unexpected condition that prevented it from fulfilling the request."). A `credential_response_encryption` object the catalogue carries therefore holds a non-empty `alg_values_supported` and a non-empty `enc_values_supported`, the two lists the object makes REQUIRED, and the requested `alg` and `enc` are always checked against both lists ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 12.2.4](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-12.2.4): "alg_values_supported: REQUIRED. A non-empty array containing a list of the JWE [RFC7516] encryption algorithms (alg values) [RFC7518] supported by the Credential Endpoint to encode the Credential Response in a JWT [RFC7519]." And: "enc_values_supported: REQUIRED. A non-empty array containing a list of the JWE [RFC7516] encryption algorithms (enc values) [RFC7518] supported by the Credential Endpoint to encode the Credential Response in a JWT [RFC7519]."). A catalogue whose object sets `encryption_required` and carries no lists issues no Credential: every request under it is answered 500, whether it asks for an encrypted response or not.
  - **The grant rules.** After the exclusivity check, `Oid4VciEndpoints.AuthorizeCredentialRequest` reads the `openid_credential` objects the token endpoint embedded in the token's `authorization_details` claim. With such objects, a request by `credential_configuration_id` is refused `invalid_credential_request`, and a `credential_identifier` no object lists is refused `unknown_credential_identifier`; without them, a request by `credential_identifier` is refused `invalid_credential_request`. An identifier the grant lists under more than one configuration names no one Credential Dataset; the grant is the application's inconsistency, so the request is answered 500 `server_error` with a fixed description and the issuance seam is not consulted; an identifier is ambiguous only across distinct configurations, so one listed in two objects naming the same configuration is served ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 6.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-6.2): "credential_identifiers: REQUIRED. A non-empty array of strings, each uniquely identifying a Credential Dataset that can be issued using the Access Token returned in this response."). [OID4VCI 1.0 Section 8.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-8.2): "credential_identifier: REQUIRED when an Authorization Details of type openid_credential was returned from the Token Response. It MUST NOT be used otherwise." And: "credential_configuration_id: REQUIRED if a credential_identifiers parameter was not returned from the Token Response as part of the authorization_details parameter. It MUST NOT be used otherwise."
  - **The catalogue rules.** The configuration the grant authorized (the request's own, or the configuration of the object listing the requested identifier) is looked up in the catalogue; one it does not list is refused `unknown_credential_configuration` ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 8.3.1.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-8.3.1.2): "unknown_credential_configuration: Requested Credential Configuration is unknown."). A request by `credential_configuration_id` whose configuration declares a `scope` presents a token granted that scope, and a token with no `scope` claim grants none ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 8.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-8.2): "The corresponding object in the credential_configurations_supported map MUST contain one of the value(s) used in the scope parameter in the Authorization Request."); the scope binding holds a request by configuration only, a request by a granted identifier being authorized by the object that lists it; the key-attestation requirement judges the authorized configuration whichever member named it. Each refusal carries a fixed description that names no value from the request.
  - **The narrowed clause.** The clause of a configuration that declares no `scope` holds for a token that carries granted details, which names its configurations; a configuration that declares no `scope` is served to every token that carries no granted details, a token that carries a `scope` included, although [OID4VCI 1.0 Section 12.2.4](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-12.2.4) says: "If scope is absent, the only way to request the Credential is using authorization_details [RFC9396] - in this case, the OAuth Authorization Server metadata for one of the Authorization Servers found from the Credential Issuer's Metadata must contain an authorization_details_types_supported that contains openid_credential." This is a limitation with its reason for each token: a Pre-Authorized Code token carries neither scope nor details, and its evidence, the Credential Offer, is seen by the application's seams alone, so the Credential Endpoint cannot tell such a token from one the clause refuses, and binding it to its offer's configurations is `IssueCredentialAsync`'s; a token that carries a `scope` and no granted details is served because the endpoint holds a request by configuration to the configuration's own declared `scope` (`Oid4VciEndpoints.ValidateScopeConfigurationBinding`) and has no rule for a configuration that declares none.
  - **The authorization-code path.** The authorization-code client's token result carries the granted `openid_credential` authorizations the library's Token Response parser read (`AuthCodeFlowEndpointResult.GrantedCredentialAuthorizations`), and the wallet chooses its target through `CredentialRequestTarget.FromGrantedAuthorizations`, as the Pre-Authorized Code entry points do; the entry "The granted authorizations reach the wallet typed, through one reader, on both paths." states the rule.
  - **The wallet's target.** `CredentialRequestTarget` names what a Credential Request asks for, `ForConfiguration` or `ForIdentifier`, exactly one, so a request naming both members or neither cannot be expressed ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 8.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-8.2): "When this parameter is used, the credential_configuration_id MUST NOT be present."). `Oid4VciWalletClient.IssueWithAccessTokenDetailedAsync` takes it, and the request body writes exactly the member it names, escaped, as it writes the `credential_response_encryption` object's members. The Pre-Authorized Code entry points read their Token Response's `authorization_details` with the span reader, every member found by the text its name spells with its escapes decoded and read at its value's own offset (`JwkJsonReader.IndexOfValue`), and request by the first `credential_identifiers` value granted for the requested configuration, by the configuration when the response returned no `openid_credential` object, and send no request, answering `MalformedResponse`, when the response granted identifiers for other configurations only or any entry of the array is malformed (not an object, or an `openid_credential` object without its configuration, with a configuration that is empty or white space, without identifiers, or listing an identifier that is empty or white space); the whole array is validated before a grant is answered ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 6.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-6.2): "The Wallet MUST use these identifiers together with an Access Token in subsequent Credential Requests."). An `authorization_details` member that is an empty array is malformed, and so is an entry whose `type` is absent or not a JSON string, wherever it stands; an entry whose `type` is a string other than `openid_credential` is skipped ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 6.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-6.2): "It is a non-empty array of objects, as defined in Section 7 of [RFC9396]." [RFC 9396 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9396#section-2): "type: An identifier for the authorization details type as a string. The value of the type field determines the allowable contents of the object that contains it. The value is unique for the described API in the context of the AS. This field is REQUIRED."). The Pre-Authorized Code entry points read the member through `GrantedCredentialAuthorizationReader` and choose through `CredentialRequestTarget.FromGrantedAuthorizations`; after an authorization-code exchange the wallet chooses through the same factory over the token result.
  - Proven over the listener by `Oid4VciConfigurationConstraintsTests.ARequestByConfigurationUnderGrantedIdentifiersIsAnInvalidCredentialRequest`, `ARequestByAnotherConfigurationUnderGrantedIdentifiersIsAnInvalidCredentialRequest`, `AnIdentifierTheTokenWasNotGrantedIsUnknown`, `ARequestByIdentifierUnderATokenWithoutDetailsIsAnInvalidCredentialRequest`, `ATokenCarryingNoScopeIsRefusedAScopedConfiguration`, `AConfigurationTheCatalogueDoesNotListIsUnknown`, `AnIdentifierForAConfigurationRequiringKeyAttestationIsRefusedAPlainProof`, `AnIdentifierGrantedForAConfigurationTheCatalogueDoesNotListIsUnknown`, `AnIdentifierGrantedUnderTwoConfigurationsIsAServerError`, `AnIdentifierGrantedTwiceUnderOneConfigurationIsServed`, `ARequestByIdentifierIsNotHeldToTheConfigurationScope`, `ACredentialEndpointWithoutTheCatalogueIsNotServed`, `ACredentialRequestIsJudgedAgainstOneCatalogue`, `ACredentialRequestUnderACatalogueTheMetadataEndpointRefusesIsAServerError`, `ScopeMatchingConfigurationIssues`, `ScopeNotMatchingConfigurationIsRefused` and `AnUnknownScopeValueIsIgnoredAsync` (a token granted the configuration's scope value is served; a token granted another scope value is refused; a scope value naming no configuration is ignored, at the pushed request and at the Credential Endpoint; the entry for `40f45edc` below); by `Oid4VciDeferredAndNotificationTests.ADeferredCredentialEndpointWithoutTheCatalogueIsNotServed`; by `Oid4VciEncryptionConformanceTests.ACredentialRequestAskingForResponseEncryptionTheCatalogueDoesNotAdvertiseIsRefused`, `ADeferredRequestAskingForResponseEncryptionTheCatalogueDoesNotAdvertiseIsRefused`, `ACredentialRequestAgainstAResponseEncryptionObjectWithoutBothListsIsRefused`, `ADeferredRequestAgainstAnEmptyResponseEncryptionObjectIsRefused`, `ARequiredResponseEncryptionWithoutItsListsIssuesNoCredential`, `ACredentialRequestUnderAnEncryptionObjectTheMetadataEndpointRefusesIsAServerError` and `ADeferredRequestUnderAStringEncryptionRequiredFlagIsAServerError`; by `Oid4VciAuthorizationCodeIssuanceTests.ParPkceAuthorizationCodeJourneyEndsInAnIssuedCredential`; and by `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.APreAuthorizedJourneyRequestsByTheGrantedIdentifier`, `APreAuthorizedTokenGrantingAnotherConfigurationIsNotSpentOnIt`, `AGrantedIdentifierThatNamesNoDatasetIsAMalformedTokenResponse`, `AConfigurationIdentifierIsWrittenAloneAndEscaped` and `AResponseEncryptionJwkMemberIsWrittenEscaped`. In process by `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.AMalformedEntryAfterAValidGrantIsAMalformedTokenResponse`, `AnEmptyAuthorizationDetailsArrayIsAMalformedTokenResponse`, `AnEntryWithoutAStringTypeIsAMalformedTokenResponse`, `AnEntryWithABlankConfigurationIdentifierIsAMalformedTokenResponse`, `AnAuthorizationDetailsNameSpelledWithAnEscapeIsReadAsTheMember`, `AnEntryTypeSpelledWithAnEscapeIsReadAsItsType` and `AnEntryOfAnotherTypeBesideAValidGrantIsSkipped` (the library's Authorization Server emits no such member or entry, so the Token Response is delivered through the wallet's transport delegate); and by `CredentialRequestTargetTests`, the target's shape.

- **The granted authorizations reach the wallet typed, through one reader, on both paths.** `GrantedCredentialAuthorizationReader` (internal, `Verifiable.OAuth.Oid4Vci`) reads a Token Response's `authorization_details` on UTF-8 bytes into `GrantedCredentialAuthorization` values, the record the Authorization Server writes the member from, so one type serves both ends of the wire member; the Pre-Authorized Code wallet and `OAuthResponseParsers.ParseTokenResponse` both call it. It finds every member, `authorization_details` itself included, by the text its name spells with its escapes decoded and reads the member at its value's own offset (`JwkJsonReader.IndexOfValue`), since an escaped spelling makes the raw name longer than the text it spells ([RFC 8259 Section 7](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8259#section-7): "Any character may be escaped."; [RFC 8259 Section 8.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8259#section-8.3): "Implementations that transform the textual representation into sequences of Unicode code units and then perform the comparison numerically, code unit by code unit, are interoperable in the sense that implementations will agree in all cases on equality or inequality of two strings."). `TokenResponse.GrantedCredentialAuthorizations` carries the list in the order the response lists it, and `AuthCodeFlowHandlers.HandleTokenAsync` and `RefreshAsync` surface it on their `Ok` result as `AuthCodeFlowEndpointResult.GrantedCredentialAuthorizations`; `TokenReceivedState` does not carry it, and an application keeps what it needs from the result. `CredentialRequestTarget.FromGrantedAuthorizations` chooses the target: the first identifier of the first entry naming the configuration ([RFC 9396 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9396#section-2): "An authorization_details array MAY contain multiple entries of the same type."), the configuration when the list is empty, and no target when the list names other configurations only ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 8.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-8.2): "credential_identifier: REQUIRED when an Authorization Details of type openid_credential was returned from the Token Response. It MUST NOT be used otherwise." And: "credential_configuration_id: REQUIRED if a credential_identifiers parameter was not returned from the Token Response as part of the authorization_details parameter. It MUST NOT be used otherwise."). For every grant it parses (the authorization code, the refresh, the client credentials and the ID-JAG), `ParseTokenResponse` reads a body that is not an RFC 9457 problem as one JSON document before it reads any member: a body longer than `JwkJsonReader.MaximumDocumentLength`, counted in chars and then in UTF-8 bytes, is refused before any buffer is rented ([RFC 8259 Section 9](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8259#section-9): "An implementation may set limits on the size of texts that it accepts."); the body is then converted to UTF-8 once, into a buffer rented from `ArrayPool<byte>.Shared` and cleared on its return, and held to `JwkJsonReader.IsWellFormedJsonDocument`, so a body that is not well-formed JSON ([RFC 8259 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8259#section-2)), a member name repeated in any object included, a string holding an unpaired surrogate or a noncharacter, escaped or not, included ([RFC 7493 Section 2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7493#section-2.1): "Object member names, and string values in arrays and object members, MUST NOT include code points that identify Surrogates or Noncharacters as defined by [UNICODE]."), and a string holding an escaped U+0000 included (this library's own rule, since a store of text cannot hold U+0000 and the JSON grammar admits the escape; [RFC 8259 Section 9](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8259#section-9): "An implementation may set limits on the length and character contents of strings."), is refused before any member is read ([RFC 8259 Section 4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8259#section-4): "When the names within an object are not unique, the behavior of software that receives such an object is unpredictable."). Both refusals are an `OAuthMalformedResponse`, which the authorization-code client answers `server_error` with no token surfaced and no state saved; an error response is held to the same gate, so an error body that is not well-formed JSON or is over the limit is answered `server_error`, not the error code it names. Every member, `error`, `error_description`, `error_uri`, `access_token`, `token_type`, `expires_in`, `refresh_token`, `scope`, `id_token` and `authorization_details`, is then read from that copy at the top level of the response object only, so a member of the same name inside a nested object, an `authorization_details` entry among them, is never read in its place ([RFC 6749 Section 5.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-5.1): "The parameters are serialized into a JavaScript Object Notation (JSON) structure by adding each parameter at the highest structure level."). A string value is read with its JSON escapes decoded, and the members `error`, `error_description`, `error_uri`, `access_token`, `token_type`, `refresh_token`, `scope` and `id_token` are then held to their grammars: an `access_token` holding a char outside `%x20-7E`, a present `refresh_token` that is empty or holds such a char, a `token_type` that is neither a type-name nor an RFC 3986 URI-reference, a present `scope` outside Appendix A.4, or a present `id_token` that is empty or holds a char outside the JWS Compact Serialization's base64url chars and periods, is refused as `OAuthInvalidFieldValue` naming the member with a fixed reason and an empty received value, so no token holding a control char is surfaced or composed into a header and none is echoed ([RFC 6749 Appendix A.12](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#appendix-A.12): "access-token = 1*VSCHAR"; [Appendix A.13](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#appendix-A.13): "token-type = type-name / URI-reference", "type-name  = 1*name-char" and "name-char  = "-" / "." / "_" / DIGIT / ALPHA"; [RFC 3986 Section 4.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-4.1): "URI-reference = URI / relative-ref"; the URI-reference is read by `IsUriReference`, a private span recognizer of that grammar in `OAuthResponseParsers`, which holds (a) every char unreserved, a gen-delim, a sub-delim or a `%` followed by two hex digits, (b) at most one `#`, (c) a URI whose first segment's colon closes a scheme of `ALPHA *( ALPHA / DIGIT / "+" / "-" / "." )`, or a relative reference whose first segment holds no colon ([RFC 3986 Section 4.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-4.2): "A path segment that contains a colon character (e.g., "this:that") cannot be used as the first segment of a relative-path reference, as it would be mistaken for a scheme name."), (d) `[` and `]` only inside an authority, between `//` and the authority's end at the next `/`, `?`, `#` or the value's end, and (e) a non-empty value, the authority's inner structure not read further: brackets need not form an IP-literal and the port need not be digits; [RFC 7515 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7515#section-7.1): "The JWS Compact Serialization represents digitally signed or MACed content as a compact, URL-safe string."; [Appendix A.17](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#appendix-A.17): "refresh-token = 1*VSCHAR"; [Appendix A.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#appendix-A.4): "scope       = scope-token *( SP scope-token )" and "scope-token = 1*NQCHAR"; [Appendix A](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#appendix-A): "VSCHAR     = %x20-7E" and "NQCHAR     = %x21 / %x23-5B / %x5D-7E"); an empty `access_token` or `token_type` is an `OAuthMalformedResponse`. An `expires_in` of zero or below is refused, and one that is not an integer within `int` reads as absent. `id_token` is held to that alphabet only and is otherwise surfaced unvalidated by the parser: it is the input of the ID Token validator, `Oidc10IdTokenValidator.ValidateAsync`, which refuses a value that is not a compact JWS as `JwsAccessTokenValidationFailureReason.Malformed`. The parser holds `access_token` to Appendix A.12 alone, since it serves clients that carry the token in places other than an `Authorization` header. The Pre-Authorized Code wallet holds its own `access_token` to one or more chars in `%x20-7E` (an empty one refused) and to `token68`, the syntax of the `Authorization` header's credential it presents the token as under the Bearer or the DPoP scheme ([RFC 9110 Section 11.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-11.2): "token68        = 1*( ALPHA / DIGIT / "-" / "." / "_" / "~" / "+" / "/" ) *"=""; [RFC 9449 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.1): "The syntax of the Authorization header field for the DPoP scheme uses the token68 syntax defined in Section 11.2 of [RFC9110] for credentials and is repeated below for ease of reference."), so a token of white space or one holding a space or a quotation mark is refused at the read, before any later request. It holds a present `token_type` to the same type-name or URI-reference (an absent one refused as `MalformedResponse`, [RFC 6749 Section 5.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-5.1): "token_type REQUIRED."; a present empty one refused), and uses the token only under `Bearer` or `DPoP` ([RFC 6749 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-7.1): "The client MUST NOT use an access token if it does not understand the token type."; the entry for `9214b8eb` below). Both reads go through the same internal predicates `OAuthResponseParsers.IsVisibleCharacterText` and `OAuthResponseParsers.IsTokenType`, answering `MalformedResponse` with a fixed rule text that echoes no value. Its own error read, for every endpoint it calls, holds the error members to the parser's set through `OAuthResponseParsers.IsErrorCharacterText`: an `error` that is empty or outside the set is `MalformedResponse` with a fixed rule text, and an `error_description` outside it is read as absent (the wallet reads no `error_uri`). At the credential endpoint, and at the deferred credential endpoint, whose error response OpenID4VCI 1.0 Section 9.3 constructs as Section 8.3.1 defines it, Section 8.3.1.2 holds `error_description` to that set and asks of `error` only that it be a single ASCII code; at the nonce endpoint (Section 7 defines no error response) and the notification endpoint (Section 11.3 defines the `error` member only for its 400 JSON body, which SHOULD be one of its codes) the set is this library's reading, as is the set for the `error` of every endpoint but the token endpoint ([OpenID4VCI 1.0 Section 8.3.1.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-8.3.1.2): "error: REQUIRED. The error parameter SHOULD be a single ASCII [USASCII] error code from the following:" And: "The values for the error_description parameter MUST NOT include characters outside the set %x20-21 / %x23-5B / %x5D-7E."; [Section 9.3](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-9.3): "When the Deferred Credential Request is invalid, the Credential Issuer constructs the error response as defined in Section 8.3.1."; [Section 11.3](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-11.3): "error: REQUIRED. The value of the error parameter SHOULD be one of the following ASCII [USASCII] error codes:"). An `error` that is empty ([Appendix A.7](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#appendix-A.7): "error             = 1*NQSCHAR") or holds a char outside `%x20-21 / %x23-5B / %x5D-7E` makes the response an `OAuthMalformedResponse`, and an `error_description` holding one, an `error_uri` holding a char outside `%x21 / %x23-5B / %x5D-7E`, or an `error_uri` that is not a URI-reference that is an `https` or `http` URI with a host and no userinfo, is read as absent while the error stands with its code. `IsWebPageUri` holds the rule by itself and surfaces an `error_uri` only when `IsUriReference` holds, its scheme is `https` or `http`, compared without case, a query and a fragment allowed, and (a) its hier-part starts with `//`, (b) its authority, to the next `/`, `?`, `#` or the end, is not empty, (c) the authority holds no `@`, and (d) the authority is a non-empty host with an optional port: a host that starts with `[` runs to the first `]`, holds an IPv6 address between the brackets, and its `]` is the authority's last char or is followed by `:` and digits only, and any other host is not empty and holds no bracket and no `:`, so the authority holds at most one `:`, which is followed by digits only; a `javascript:`, `file:` or `data:` URI, a relative reference, a value holding a char or a delimiter no URI-reference holds, an `https:` or `http:` value without `//` or with an empty authority, an authority behind a userinfo, and a host or port outside that shape are read as absent ([RFC 9110 Section 4.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-4.2.2): "https-URI = "https" "://" authority path-abempty [ "?" query ]" and "A sender MUST NOT generate an "https" URI with an empty host identifier.  A recipient that processes such a URI reference MUST reject it as invalid."; [Section 4.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-4.2.1) the same for `http-URI`; [RFC 3986 Section 3.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-3.2): "authority   = [ userinfo "@" ] host [ ":" port ]"; [Section 3.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-3.2.2): "reg-name    = *( unreserved / pct-encoded / sub-delims )"; [Section 3.2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-3.2.3): "port        = *DIGIT"; [RFC 6749 Section 5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-5.2): "error_uri OPTIONAL. A URI identifying a human-readable web page with information about the error, used to provide the client developer with additional information about the error." And: "Values for the "error" parameter MUST NOT include characters outside the set %x20-21 / %x23-5B / %x5D-7E." And: "Values for the "error_description" parameter MUST NOT include characters outside the set %x20-21 / %x23-5B / %x5D-7E." And: "Values for the "error_uri" parameter MUST conform to the URI-reference syntax and thus MUST NOT include characters outside the set %x21 / %x23-5B / %x5D-7E."; [Appendix A.9](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#appendix-A.9): "error-uri         = URI-reference"; [RFC 3986 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-3): "URI         = scheme ":" hier-part [ "?" query ] [ "#" fragment ]"; [RFC 9110 Section 4.2.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-4.2.4): "Before making use of an "http" or "https" URI reference received from an untrusted source, a recipient SHOULD parse for userinfo and treat its presence as an error; it is likely being used to obscure the authority for the sake of phishing attacks."); the PAR parser's error path is unchanged. A refusal past the problem+json branch carries the body as its `OAuthMalformedResponse.Body` only when the body is an HTML or XML document: with a leading byte order mark and white space stripped, its first char is `<`; every other refused body, a JSON text, a form-encoded body, plain text, a JSON scalar or anything else, carries an empty `Body`, and the `authorization_details` refusal an empty `OAuthInvalidFieldValue.ReceivedValue`; each summary and reason name the refusal ([RFC 6749 Section 10.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-10.3): "Access token credentials (as well as any confidential access token attributes) MUST be kept confidential in transit and storage, and only shared among the authorization server, the resource servers the access token is valid for, and the client to whom the access token is issued."; [Section 10.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-10.4): "Refresh tokens MUST be kept confidential in transit and storage, and shared only among the authorization server and the client to whom the refresh tokens were issued."; [RFC 8259 Section 8.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8259#section-8.1): "Implementations MUST NOT add a byte order mark (U+FEFF) to the beginning of a networked-transmitted JSON text."). An HTML or XML document, such as a gateway's error page, keeps its `Body` for diagnostics, whatever it holds. A malformed member is answered as `OAuthInvalidFieldValue` naming `authorization_details`, which the authorization-code client answers `invalid_response`, with no token surfaced and no state saved; an `openid_credential` entry whose `credential_configuration_id` is empty or white space is malformed, as an empty identifier is, and an identifier listed twice in one entry is read as listed ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 6.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-6.2): "It is a non-empty array of objects, as defined in Section 7 of [RFC9396]." And: "credential_identifiers: REQUIRED. A non-empty array of strings, each uniquely identifying a Credential Dataset that can be issued using the Access Token returned in this response."). An entry whose `type` is a string other than `openid_credential` is skipped, and the data fields of an `openid_credential` entry other than its configuration and identifiers are ignored ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 6.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-6.2): "The Wallet MUST ignore any unrecognized data fields in the authorization_details present in the Token Response."). An application that wires its own `ParseTokenResponseDelegate` and leaves the member empty makes the wallet request by the configuration. Proven over the listener by `Oid4VciAuthorizationCodeIssuanceTests.ParPkceAuthorizationCodeJourneyEndsInAnIssuedCredential`, `ATokenResponseGrantingTwoIdentifiersIsSpentOnTheFirst`, `ATokenResponseWithoutGrantedDetailsIsSpentOnTheConfiguration` and `ARefreshedTokenResultCarriesTheGrantedAuthorizations`, and, with the Token Response rewritten after it crossed the wire and before the client reads it (the library's Authorization Server emits no such answer), by `AnAuthorizationDetailsNameSpelledWithAnEscapeIsReadAsTheMemberOnTheAuthorizationCodePath`, `AMalformedAuthorizationDetailsMemberFailsTheTokenExchange`, `AGrantedEntryNamingNoConfigurationFailsTheTokenExchange`, `ATokenResponseRepeatingItsAccessTokenFailsTheTokenExchange`, `ANestedAccessTokenIsNotSurfacedAsTheToken`, `AnAccessTokenOutsideItsGrammarFailsTheTokenExchange`, `AScopeOutsideItsGrammarFailsTheTokenExchange`, `APresentEmptyRefreshTokenFailsTheTokenExchange`, `AnEmptyErrorCodeFailsTheTokenExchange` and `AnIdTokenOutsideTheCompactSerializationFailsTheTokenExchange`, by `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.APreAuthorizedTokenAnswerHoldingALineBreakIsNotSpent`, `APreAuthorizedTokenErrorOutsideItsCharacterSetIsMalformed`, `APreAuthorizedAccessTokenOutsideToken68IsMalformed` and `APreAuthorizedTokenTypeOutsideItsGrammarIsMalformed` (the Pre-Authorized Code Token Response rewritten the same way), by `APreAuthorizedUriReferenceTokenTypeIsReadButNotUsed` (the URI-reference emitted by the listener through the test host's answer rewrite and read by the wallet's own transport), and by `IdJagGrantTests.ARefusedMintAnswerReachesTheApplicationWithoutItsBody`, `AFormEncodedMintAnswerReachesTheApplicationWithoutItsBody`, `AMintErrorUriNamingNoWebPageReachesTheApplicationAsAbsent`, `AMintErrorUriWithUserinfoReachesTheApplicationAsAbsent`, `AMintErrorUriOutsideTheUriReferenceSyntaxReachesTheApplicationAsAbsent` and `AMintErrorWithAnEmptyCodeReachesTheApplicationAsMalformed` (the ID-JAG mint answer rewritten the same way, the parse error reaching the application); in process by `OAuthResponseParsersTests.ParseTokenResponseReadsTheGrantedIdentifiersInOrder`, `ParseTokenResponseReadsAnAuthorizationDetailsNameSpelledWithAnEscape`, `ParseTokenResponseReadsEveryGrantedEntryInOrder`, `ParseTokenResponseLeadsALaterConfigurationToItsEntrysFirstIdentifier`, `ParseTokenResponseWithoutAuthorizationDetailsGrantsNoIdentifiers`, `ParseTokenResponseSkipsAnEntryOfAnotherType`, `ParseTokenResponseIgnoresUnrecognizedFieldsOfAGrantedEntry`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesAuthorizationDetailsThatAreNotANonEmptyArrayOfObjects`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesAnEntryWithoutAStringType`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesAGrantedEntryWithoutItsRequiredMembers`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesABlankConfigurationIdentifier`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesABodyThatRepeatsAMember`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesABodyThatRepeatsExpiresIn`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesABodyOverTheDocumentLimit`, `ParseTokenResponseReadsTheTopLevelAccessTokenOverANestedOne`, `ParseTokenResponseReadsANestedErrorAsDataOfItsEntry`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusalOfANotWellFormedBodyCarriesNoCredential`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusalOfAMissingTokenTypeCarriesNoCredential`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusalOfAnEmptyAccessTokenCarriesNoCredential`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesAnAccessTokenOutsideItsGrammar`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesARefreshTokenOutsideItsGrammar`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesATokenTypeOutsideItsGrammar`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusalOfARefreshTokenOnlyBodyCarriesNoCredential`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusalOfAnIdTokenOnlyBodyCarriesNoCredential`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusalOfATruncatedBodyHoldingARefreshTokenCarriesNoCredential`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusalOfAnEscapedAccessTokenNameCarriesNoCredential`, `ParseTokenResponseReadsAnAccessTokenNameSpelledWithAnEscape`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesATokenStringOutsideIJsonAsNotWellFormed`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesATokenStringHoldingAnEscapedControlCharacterAsNotWellFormed`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusalOfAuthorizationDetailsCarriesNoCredential`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusalOfAnHtmlBodyKeepsItsBody`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesAnErrorCodeOutsideItsCharacterSet`, `ParseTokenResponseReadsAnErrorDescriptionOutsideItsCharacterSetAsAbsent`, `ParseTokenResponseReadsAnErrorUriOutsideItsCharacterSetAsAbsent`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesAnErrorBodyThatRepeatsAMember`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusalOfABodyThatIsNotHtmlOrXmlCarriesNoBody`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusalOfAByteOrderMarkedJsonObjectCarriesNoBody`, `ParseTokenResponseAcceptsAUriReferenceTokenType`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesAPresentEmptyRefreshToken`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesAScopeOutsideItsGrammar`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesAnErrorCodeHoldingAQuotationMark`, `ParseTokenResponseReadsAnErrorDescriptionHoldingAReverseSolidusAsAbsent`, `ParseTokenResponseReadsAnErrorUriHoldingASpaceAsAbsent`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesAnEmptyErrorCode`, `ParseTokenResponseReadsAnErrorUriThatNamesNoWebPageAsAbsent`, `ParseTokenResponseReadsAnErrorUriOutsideTheUriReferenceSyntaxAsAbsent`, `ParseTokenResponseReadsAnErrorUriWithUserinfoAsAbsent`, `ParseTokenResponseReadsAnErrorUriWithoutAnAuthorityAsAbsent`, `ParseTokenResponseReadsAnErrorUriWithAnEmptyHostAsAbsent`, `ParseTokenResponseReadsAnErrorUriWhoseAuthorityIsNoHostAndPortAsAbsent`, `ParseTokenResponseSurfacesAnErrorUriWithAHostAndPortAsWritten`, `ParseTokenResponseSurfacesAnErrorUriWithAnAtSignAfterItsAuthorityAsWritten`, `ParseTokenResponseSurfacesAWebPageErrorUriAsWritten`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesAnIdTokenOutsideTheCompactSerialization`, `ParseTokenResponseSurfacesACompactSerializationIdTokenAsWritten`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesATokenTypeOutsideTheUriReferenceGrammar`, `ParseTokenResponseRefusesAScopeOutsideAppendixA4`, `ParseTokenResponseSurfacesAScopeOfTwoScopeTokens` and `ParseTokenResponseRefusalOfAByteOrderMarkedHtmlBodyKeepsItsBody`; by `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.AnAccessTokenOutsideItsGrammarIsAMalformedTokenResponse`, `ATokenTypeOutsideItsGrammarIsAMalformedTokenResponse`, `AUriReferenceTokenTypeIsReadButNotUsed`, `ATokenTypeHoldingASpaceIsAMalformedTokenResponse`, `AnEmptyTokenTypeIsAMalformedTokenResponse`, `AnEmptyAccessTokenIsAMalformedTokenResponse`, `ARefusedAccessTokenIsNotEchoedInTheFailure`, `ATokenTypeOutsideTheUriReferenceGrammarIsAMalformedTokenResponse`, `AnAccessTokenOutsideToken68IsAMalformedTokenResponse`, `AToken68AccessTokenIsSpent`, `ATokenErrorCodeOutsideItsCharacterSetIsAMalformedResponse` and `ATokenErrorDescriptionOutsideItsCharacterSetIsAbsent`; and by `CredentialRequestTargetTests.AGrantForTheConfigurationTargetsItsFirstIdentifier`, `TwoGrantsForTheConfigurationTargetTheFirstEntrysFirstIdentifier`, `NoGrantTargetsTheConfiguration` and `AGrantForOtherConfigurationsOnlyHasNoTarget`. The Pre-Authorized Code rows of `Oid4VciWalletClientTests` named in the entry before hold the wallet's answers through the shared reader and the factory.

- **Key-attestation assurance constraints.** `KeyAttestationVerifier.CheckAssuranceConstraints` checks the attested `key_storage` and `user_authentication` arrays against the issuer's accepted-value sets as OID4VCI 1.0 Appendix D.1/D.2 define them: a MEMBERSHIP SET, satisfied when at least one attested value is a member of the accepted set by ordinal string equality, never an ordering between values; an absent or empty constraint constrains nothing, and an attested array that does not read as a JSON array of strings answers `KeyAttestationVerificationFailureReason.AssuranceConstraintValuesMalformed` rather than a silent unsatisfied result. Proven by `KeyAttestationVerifierTests.SatisfiedConstraintsValidate`, `KeyStorageConstraintUnsatisfiedWhenNoAttestedValueIsAccepted`, `UserAuthenticationConstraintUnsatisfiedWhenNoAttestedValueIsAccepted`, `MalformedAttestedArrayAnswersAssuranceConstraintValuesMalformed`, `AbsentConstraintIsSatisfiedByAnyAttestation`, and `EcosystemDefinedUrlValueMatchesByOrdinalEquality`.

- **Trace facts.** A request that matches no endpoint is categorized by a fixed precedence (`NoMatchCategories`): `NoRegistrationForTenant` when the tenant has no registration; otherwise `MatcherDeclined` when any candidate reached its matcher, else `CapabilityFiltered`, else `EndpointNameUnresolved` when `EndpointChain.UnresolvedUriCandidateNames` lists an endpoint name the application's URI resolver did not map, else `NoCandidateForPathAndMethod`. The unresolved names ride the `NoMatch` event under `ServerEventNames.NoMatchEndpointNameUnresolvedTagName`, and an unmapped name never stops a mapped endpoint from dispatching. `OAuthTagNames` tags the dispatch span from `IssueTokensAsync` — the one function every token-issuing grant calls — with `GrantType`, `ClientId`, `GrantedScope`, and the issued access token's `AccessTokenJti`; the code grant additionally tags `PkceMethod` from its own redeemed `codeState`, since PKCE is specific to that one grant. `ServerHttpResponse.ErrorCode` and `ServerTagNames.ErrorCode` carry the same wire error code onto the response and the span. No tag carries a token, a code, or a subject. Proven by `OAuthDiagnosticsTests.MappedEndpointStillDispatchesWhileAnotherNameIsUnresolved`, `CodeGrantTokenActivityCarriesTheOAuthWireFacts`, `ClientCredentialsTokenActivityCarriesItsOwnGrantType`, `RefusedTokenRequestActivityAndResponseCarryTheSameErrorCode`, and `NoActivityTagCarriesTheAccessTokenAuthorizationCodeOrSubject`.

- **Family revocation and persistence.** Valid reuse first reads its predecessor's audit, then revokes its own audit and walks `SuccessorRefreshFlowId` through retained records. Each audited token reaches the optional `RevokeIssuedTokenAsync`; the live refresh record is claimed with its loaded step before the required `DeleteFlowStateAsync` deletes it. A lost claim reloads that same flow once and follows the retired successor. A second failed live claim stops without deleting unclaimed state. Missing or unexpected records stop traversal. There is no arbitrary hop cap: retention by `ExpiresAt` supplies the natural bound, and a visited set stops corruption-induced cycles while keeping completed revocations. After the walk, `ServerRefreshTokenReuseDetected` sets `RevokedAt` through a pure record-copy transition and the runner saves it. Sequential presentations skip the walk; concurrent reuses may repeat idempotent audit calls. Code replay uses the same successor walk. Every reuse refusal, including issuer-resolution failure, has the constant `invalid_grant` body; only response bytes are constant, not work or timing. A legitimate client's concurrent refresh can lose its grant when one request loads the retired record after another rotates it: OAuth 2.1 §4.3.1 states "The authorization server cannot determine which party submitted the invalid refresh token, but it will revoke the active refresh token as well as the access authorization grant associated with it."

- **PKCE policy.** Plain is refused everywhere, including `PolicyProfile.Rfc6749WithPkce`. [OAuth 2.1 draft-16 §4.1.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-v2-1-16#section-4.1.1) prohibits the plain transformation; [§7.5.2](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-v2-1-16#section-7.5.2) states "The plain code challenge method, defined in [RFC7636], is explicitly forbidden in OAuth 2.1." [RFC 9700 §2.1.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9700#section-2.1.1) calls for a method that does not expose the verifier; S256 is the only such method. PAR, direct authorize, and both JAR entry points refuse unsupported methods with `invalid_request` per [RFC 7636 §4.4.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7636#section-4.4.1). The RFC 6749 profile retains its other relaxations. [RFC 8414 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8414#section-2) discovery advertises `["S256"]` under every profile. `WellKnownCodeChallengeMethods.Plain` and `IsPlain` identify the refused wire name and values in remote client metadata; they do not enable server acceptance. The persisted `ServerCodeIssuedState.CodeChallengeMethod` travels from authorization to token verification: only S256 hashes and compares, and every other persisted value fails `invalid_grant`. [RFC 7636 §4.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7636#section-4.1) verifier grammar is checked before [§4.6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7636#section-4.6) digest verification. Absent methods request plain under [RFC 7636 §4.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7636#section-4.3) and receive the same refusal. Both JAR paths check the method immediately after signature verification, before projection and loopback redirect matching; parameter failures use the direct JSON response specified by [RFC 9101 §6.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9101#section-6.3). Direct authorization validates the client and redirect URI first, then redirects `invalid_request` with the exact request state under [RFC 6749 §4.1.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-4.1.2.1). Invalid destinations receive a direct refusal. PAR retains its JSON 400 under [RFC 9126 §2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9126#section-2.3).
  - `AbsentPkceMethodRefusedAtParUnderRfc6749WithPkce` and `AbsentPkceMethodRefusedAtDirectAuthorizeUnderRfc6749WithPkce` prove the absent-method refusal.
  - `JarAuthorizeByValueTests` and `JarParTests` each prove plain and absent methods with an exact registered redirect and with a native loopback redirect on an ephemeral port.
  - `LoopbackFallbackRefusesPlainPkceIndependently` directly proves the fallback's independent S256 condition; `PlainPkceWithInvalidRedirectUriDoesNotRedirect` proves destination validation precedes the redirect error.
  - `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.PlainPkceRefusedAtParUnderS256OnlyDefault`, `PlainPkceRefusedAtParUnderRfc6749WithPkce`, and `PlainPkceRefusedAtDirectAuthorizeUnderRfc6749WithPkce` prove the authorization refusals over the listener.
  - `JarAuthorizeByValueTests.RejectsJarWithCodeChallengeMethodPlain` and `JarParTests.RejectsJarWithCodeChallengeMethodPlain` prove signed-request refusals under the RFC 6749 profile over the listener.
  - `DiscoveryEndpointTests.DiscoveryEmitsCodeChallengeMethodsAsS256OnlyUnderFapi20` and `DiscoveryEmitsCodeChallengeMethodsAsS256OnlyUnderRfc6749WithPkce` prove the listener advertisement.
  - `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.TooShortCodeVerifierAtTokenExchangeIsInvalidGrant` and `CodeVerifierWithDisallowedCharacterAtTokenExchangeIsInvalidGrant` use matching S256 digests to isolate the grammar rules.
  - `PersistedPlainPkceMethodAtTokenExchangeIsInvalidGrant` and `PersistedUnknownPkceMethodAtTokenExchangeIsInvalidGrant` corrupt the host's stored method and prove refusal; `S256IssuedCodeVerifiedAgainstPersistedMethodNotRequest` proves the persisted S256 transformation, and `FullJourneyReachesTokenRefreshAndRevocation` proves successful S256 issuance.

- **CAEP `credential-change` emit + credential status lists (the credential-side signal).** The signal/command spectrum extends from the *session* to the *credential*: where `session-revoked` (Slice 7) signals a revoked session, a CAEP `credential-change` SET (CAEP 1.0 §3.3, `change_type: revoke`, `credential_type: verifiable-credential`) signals a revoked credential — the same best-effort "signal" end, the same `SecurityEventTokenIssuance` / reception subsystem, the same **no library orchestrator** rule. The status runtime itself lives *outside* the AS, in `Verifiable.Core/StatusLists`: one shared bit core (`StatusList` — packed N-bit entries over a pooled buffer, parameterized by a **required** `BitOrder`: least-significant-first for the IETF Token Status List, most-significant-first for the W3C Bitstring Status List) carrying two presentations — `StatusListToken` (IETF: ZLIB + base64url token) and `BitstringStatusListCodec` / `BitstringStatusListEntry` / `BitstringStatusListValidation` (W3C: GZIP + Multibase + the `BitstringStatusListCredential`, issued/verified over the existing JWS or Data Integrity surface). Revocation is the `UpdateCredentialStatusesDelegate` seam — the credential-side counterpart of `RevokeSubjectTokensDelegate`: the application flips the bit(s) and republishes each affected list once (the pull channel a verifier polls) and emits the `credential-change` SET to its Shared Signals receivers (the push channel). The seam is **batch-shaped** because a single credential may carry several entries across one or more lists (W3C §A.3/§A.4), grouped so a batch re-encodes each affected list exactly once with no half-applied intermediate. Proven by `BitstringStatusListRevocationDualChannelHttpTests` (one trigger → republished list + pushed SET, each verified firewalled) and `BitstringStatusListBatchRevocationTests`. Wire shapes verified against the verbatim specifications: Token Status List `draft-ietf-oauth-status-list-20` §4.1, CAEP 1.0 §3.3, RFC 8417 (SET), RFC 9493 (`sub_id`), and RFC 8935 (push delivery).

- **The federation resolve endpoint names its error.** `AuthorizationServerIntegration.ResolveSubjectTrustChainAsync` (`ResolveSubjectTrustChainDelegate`) answers a `FederationResolveOutcome` — `FederationResolveOutcome.Resolved(contribution)` or `FederationResolveOutcome.Failed(error)` naming a `FederationResolveError`, or `null` — which `FederationEndpoints.BuildFederationResolveErrorResponse` maps to the [Federation §8.9](https://openid.net/specs/openid-federation-1_0.html#section-8.9) error vocabulary the specification assigns each reason (`invalid_trust_anchor` 404, `invalid_trust_chain` 400, `invalid_metadata` 400, `not_found` 404, and `invalid_subject` 404 for `null` or an unnamed error). `FederationEffectiveMetadataResolver.ReadDeclaredEntityTypes` enumerates every Entity Type the resolved subject declares, in ordinal order, for the request's optional `entity_type` filter. Proven by `FederationResolveEndpointTests` (`ResolveEndpointServesSignedResolveResponse`, `ResolveEndpointReturns404WhenSubjectUnresolvable`, `ResolveEndpointReturns400WithInvalidTrustChainWhenResolverNamesIt`, `ResolveEndpointReturns400WithInvalidMetadataWhenResolverNamesIt`, `ResolveEndpointReturns404WithInvalidTrustAnchorWhenResolverNamesIt`, `ResolveEndpointReturns404WithNotFoundWhenResolverNamesIt`) and `FederationEffectiveMetadataResolverTests.ReadDeclaredEntityTypesReturnsEveryDeclaredTypeInOrdinalOrder` / `ReadDeclaredEntityTypesReturnsEmptyWhenSubjectDeclaresNone`.

- **Remote documents: the library performs the attempt, the application owns the cache.** Caching is an application-layer concern. `ClientIdMetadataDocuments.ResolveAsync` and `JwksUriResolver.ResolveAsync` are stateless: the policed fetch through `OutboundFetch.FetchAsync`, the document's validation, and the RFC 9111 §5.2 freshness the headers imply, reported on the result as `HttpCacheFreshness`. The application's implementations of `ResolveClientMetadataDelegate` and `ResolveJwksUriDelegate` are the cache — the store, the lifetime bounds, what follows a failure, and RFC 9111 §4.2.4's rule against serving stale without permission are theirs — and one key-set delegate instance is wired into both `ClientIdMetadataDocumentResolverOptions.ResolveJwksUri` and `PrivateKeyJwtClientAuthentication.BuildValidator`, so a document and a registration share one cache. A cached document whose `HasJwksUriKeySet` is set is refreshed on a hit through `ClientIdMetadataDocuments.RefreshJwksAsync`, so a rotated key set follows its own lifetime and not the document's. The library ships no in-process dictionary, no lifetime options and no retry floor; `ClientMetadataResolutionCache` in `test/Verifiable.Tests/TestInfrastructure` is the reference an application starts from. See [§4.2](#42-remote-documents-the-attempt-is-the-librarys-the-cache-is-the-applications) and `documents/ADRs/remote-documents-attempt-and-application-cache.md`.

- **The SD-JWT VC issuer key is resolved through one delegate.** `ResolveIssuerKeyDelegate` (`issuerId`, `keyId`, `x5c`, `ExchangeContext`) is the application's single seam for both of [SD-JWT VC draft-19 §2.5](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-sd-jwt-vc-19#section-2.5)'s key-discovery mechanisms — JWT VC Issuer Metadata (`JwtVcIssuerMetadataDocuments.ResolveAsync` / `SelectKeyAsync`, `JwtVcIssuerMetadataResolution` and its outcome enum, `JwtVcIssuerMetadataDocumentResolverOptions`, `WellKnownPaths.JwtVcIssuer`) or Inline X.509 Certificates (`SdJwtX5cTrustResolver.ResolveAsync`, evaluated per call against a caller-supplied instant, never a captured `TimeProvider`) — or an application's own trust framework entirely; `SdJwtVpTokenVerification.VerifyAsync` requires the calling context so the delegate can police its own outbound request. The CBOR mdoc sibling, `MdocCborIacaTrustResolver.Create`, takes a `TimeProvider` instead because its returned delegate is built once and reused across many resolutions. Proven by `SdJwtVpTokenVerificationIssuerKeySeamTests` (`VerifyAsyncPassesTheHeaderKidToTheDelegateAndNullX5cWhenAbsent`, `VerifyAsyncPassesTheHeaderX5cToTheDelegate`, `VerifyAsyncVerifiesCredentialSignatureThroughX5cTrustResolverWithConfiguredAnchor`, `VerifyAsyncReportsCredentialSignatureUnverifiedWithNoMatchingAnchor`, `VerifyAsyncPassesTheCallersContextToTheDelegate`) and `JwtVcIssuerMetadataDocumentsResolvingTests` (`SelectKeyAsyncSelectsByKeyIdFromInlineJwks`, `SelectKeyAsyncSelectsThroughJwksUriResolver`, `SelectKeyAsyncRefusesADuplicateKeyId`, `SelectKeyAsyncSelectsTheSoleKeyWhenNoKeyIdIsPresented`, `SelectKeyAsyncRefusesWhenNoKeyIdIsPresentedAndTwoKeysExist`); the real-wire round trip is `Oid4VpFlowIntegrationTests.IssuerKeyResolvesFromJwtVcIssuerMetadataOverRealWireThenViaJwksUri`.

- **Every client-side dial is evaluated against the outbound policy first.** The endpoints the client flows dial — PAR, token, refresh, revocation, the ID-JAG mint and redeem, the OID4VCI wallet's metadata, nonce, credential, deferred and notification calls, the OID4VP wallet's response and request-object posts — are named by discovered metadata or by an offer, and `OutboundRequest`'s remarks say a POST to such an endpoint is as much an SSRF vector as a fetched URL. Each flow evaluates `OutboundFetchPolicy` before invoking the application's transport, from the call's `ExchangeContext` when it carries one and otherwise from the client's deployment configuration (`OAuthClientInfrastructure.OutboundFetchPolicy`, `Oid4VciWalletConfiguration.OutboundFetchPolicy`, `Oid4VpWalletConfiguration.OutboundFetchPolicy`, default `SecureDefault`), and a denied target answers with the flow's own typed failure without the transport ever being called. The three OID4VCI transport delegates carry the `ExchangeContext` for the same reason `SendFormPostDelegate` does. The browser cookie concern is the application's transport factory's to close (`credentials: omit` on every request built for these seams); the seam docs say so. The OID4VCI wallet's OPTIONAL §4.1.3 by-reference Credential Offer GET (`Oid4VciWalletConfiguration.FetchCredentialOffer`) is the one client dial that does not follow this evaluate-then-invoke shape: it runs through the library's own `OutboundFetch.FetchAsync` chokepoint — the same one server-side document resolution uses — rather than a single-hop transport delegate, so it carries that chokepoint's own redirect re-validation and per-hop policy evaluation, bounded by `MaximumCredentialOfferBytes`. See [§4.2](#42-remote-documents-the-attempt-is-the-librarys-the-cache-is-the-applications).

- **The client starts an authorization request directly, and asks for a token as itself.** `AuthCodeClient.StartAsync` composes the [RFC 6749 §4.1.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-4.1.1) authorization redirect with PKCE `S256` ([RFC 7636 §4.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7636#section-4.3)) and persists the same flow state the pushed road (`StartParAsync`) persists, so the callback and token legs serve both roads. It is refused before any state, PKCE material or redirect is built when the authorization server's metadata sets `require_pushed_authorization_requests` ([RFC 9126 §5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9126#section-5)), and a caller field named after a flow-owned parameter (`client_id`, `response_type`, `redirect_uri`, `scope`, `state`, `code_challenge`, `code_challenge_method`, `resource`, `dpop_jkt`, `request`, `request_uri`) never overrides the flow's value, at the two request-object starts as at the plain ones (the entry for `9214b8eb` below). `ClientCredentialsClient.RequestTokenAsync`, behind the `ClientCredentials` extension property on `OAuthClient`, composes the [RFC 6749 §4.4.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-4.4.2) request, authenticates by `ClientRegistration.AuthenticationMethod`, and sends through the DPoP-retrying token request the authorization-code and refresh legs use (`TokenEndpointClientOperations`), so a wired infrastructure presents the proof and answers the one `use_dpop_nonce` challenge. The ID-JAG mint and redeem roads (`IdJagFlowHandlers.MintAsync`, `RedeemAsync`) send through the same request while keeping their own `OAuthOutboundFetchPolicyDenied` answer. Client authentication is attached per attempt, so a `private_key_jwt` retry presents a fresh assertion (a new `jti` and `iat`) and an authorization server that enforces client-assertion replay (`CheckClientAssertionJtiReplayDelegate`) serves it (`RequestCompletesAfterOneNonceRetryWithFreshClientAssertionUnderReplayCheckedRegistration`, `RedeemCompletesAfterOneNonceRetryWithFreshClientAssertionUnderReplayCheckedRegistration`, `MintCompletesAfterOneNonceRetryOverRealWireUnderMandatingRegistration`). Proven by `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.PlainStartReachesTokenOverRealWire`, `StartAsyncRefusedBeforeAnyDialWhenMetadataRequiresPar`, `AdditionalFieldsCannotOverrideStateOrCodeChallengeAtPlainStart`, and `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientComposedRequestIssuesTokenUnderClientSecretBasicOverRealWire`, `ClientComposedRequestIssuesTokenUnderPrivateKeyJwtOverRealWire`, `WrongSecretAnswersTypedInvalidClientFailureNeverAnException`, `TokenEndpointDeniedByOutboundPolicyIsNeverDialed`, and `ClientRequestCompletesAfterOneNonceRetryOverRealWire`.

- **The OID4VCI wallet's transports carry headers, and its requests carry DPoP.** `Oid4VciFormPostDelegate` and `Oid4VciJsonPostDelegate` take an `OutgoingHeaders` parameter and answer an `HttpResponseData`, so the client — not the transport — composes the `DPoP` proof header; `Oid4VciWalletConfiguration.ConstructDpopProofAsync`, `DpopKey`, and `GenerateIdentifierAsync` are wired together or not at all (`Oid4VciWalletClient`'s constructor refuses a partial set), and when wired the §6 Pre-Authorized Code Token Request carries a proof and every subsequent §7/§8/§9/§11 request does too once the Token Response returns `token_type=DPoP`. `LookupDpopNonce` / `StoreDpopNonce` keep the nonce each endpoint supplies under that endpoint, its scheme, authority and path, the text a proof sent there names as `htu`, from every answer that carries one `DPoP-Nonce` field within the `1*NQCHAR` syntax, a 200, a refusal and a nonce challenge alike ([RFC 9449 §8.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-8.2), [§9](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-9)), so a nonce is used only at the endpoint that supplied it and a co-located authorization server and resource keep their own; `DpopNonceRetry.SendWithNonceRetryAsync` sends once and retries exactly one further time with a fresh proof on a `use_dpop_nonce` challenge ([RFC 9449 §8](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-8), §9), never a second time, the bound being the library's own rule. `CredentialIssuanceResult.ExpiresAt` is the Token Request instant plus `expires_in`, or `null` when the response carried none. Proven by `ConstructWithDpopProofConstructorButNoDpopKeyThrows`, `ConstructWithDpopKeyButNoProofConstructorThrows`, `ConstructWithDpopKeyAndProofConstructorButNoGeneratorThrows`, `TokenRequestCarriesDpopHeaderWhenKeyConfiguredAndNoneWhenAbsent`, `TokenRequestRetriesOnceOnUseDpopNonceChallengeAndUsesTheSecondAnswer`, `TokenRequestFailsOnSecondUseDpopNonceChallengeRatherThanRetryingAgain`, `CredentialRequestRetriesOnceOnUseDpopNonceChallengeAndUsesTheSecondAnswer`, `CredentialRequestFailsOnSecondUseDpopNonceChallengeRatherThanRetryingAgain`, `ExpiresAtEqualsRequestedAtPlusExpiresInWhenPresentAndIsNullWhenAbsent`, and, for the nonce kept per endpoint from every answer, `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientUsesANonceSuppliedOnASuccessfulTokenResponseAsync`, `ClientUsesANonceSuppliedOnATokenErrorResponseAsync`, `ClientStoresNoNonceFromAnAnswerOutsideTheOneFieldNonceSyntaxAsync`, `ClientKeepsTheNonceOfEachTokenEndpointOnOneOriginApartAsync`, `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.WalletUsesANonceSuppliedOnASuccessfulCredentialResponseAsync` and `WalletKeepsTheTokenEndpointNonceApartFromTheResourceNoncesOnOneOriginAsync`.

- **The OID4VCI wallet answers remote failures as values.** `Oid4VciRequestFailure` (`Kind`, `Endpoint`, nullable `StatusCode`, `ErrorCode`, `ErrorDescription`) and `Oid4VciRequestFailureKind` (`ErrorResponse`, `MalformedResponse`, `OutboundPolicyDenied`) carry every `Oid4VciWalletClient` request's failure on input the Wallet does not control; every such request answers `Result<T, Oid4VciRequestFailure>` and `SendCredentialNotificationAsync` answers a nullable `Oid4VciRequestFailure?` — `null` on the §11.2 2xx acknowledgment. What the remote party sent never throws (`CredentialOfferSerializer.TryParse` is the non-throwing parse `FromJson` shares); composition errors, caller errors, and an application delegate's own exception stay exceptions. Proven by `TokenRequestRefusedAnswersErrorResponseWithStatusAndCode`, `TokenResponseRepeatingAccessTokenAnswersMalformedResponse`, `NonceRequestRefusedAnswersErrorResponseWithStatusAndCode`, `NonceResponseWithoutCNonceAnswersMalformedResponse`, `CredentialRequestRefusedAnswersErrorResponseWithStatusAndCode`, `CredentialResponseWithEmptyCredentialsArrayAnswersMalformedResponse`, and `NotificationRefusedAnswersErrorResponseWithStatusAndCode`.

- **Capability catalogs enumerate themselves.** `WellKnownCapabilityIdentifiers.All`, `WellKnownFederationCapabilityIdentifiers.All`, `WellKnownVcalmCapabilities.All`, and `WellKnownWebFingerCapabilityIdentifiers.All` are hand-kept lists, one per catalog file, that name every `CapabilityIdentifier` property the same file declares — an operator-typed capability URN is then checked against a complete set rather than one that drifts. `SourceHygieneTests.CapabilityIdentifierCatalogsEnumerateEveryDeclaredIdentifier` scans all four files and fails naming the file and line of any property a catalog's list omits, or any list entry no property declares.

- **The tenant key never leaves the process; the tenant handle is what the library shows.** `TenantId` scopes storage, correlation and the application's own delegates, renders nothing of its value, and reaches no span, body, claim or document. `TenantHandle`, set by the application on `ClientRecord`, is the only tenant-shaped value the library emits: the dispatch span's `server.tenant.handle` tag, fail closed when no handle is set, and the display half of the registration projection, whose key half stays for observer correlation. The library derives no handle. See [§4.3](#43-the-tenant-key-and-the-tenant-handle).

- **The client assertion names the issuer as its audience, its type is a per-registration choice, and the server's audience rule is a policy.** [rfc7523bis-11 §4](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-rfc7523bis-11#section-4) item b replaces RFC 7523 §3 item 3 for client authentication: "the aud (audience) claim value MUST use the issuer identifier [RFC8414] of the authorization server as its sole value. The authorization server MUST have an issuer identifier to be used with this specification. The aud claim value MUST indicate only the issuer identifier of the intended authorization server as the audience of the JWT; this includes that the token endpoint URL of the authorization server MUST NOT be used as an audience value.
