`Indeterminate` once the entry is removed after a first redemption it decided) and `IdJagGrantTests.TheAssociationDictionaryComparesMemberNamesOrdinallyAsync` (a `sub_id` holding one listed member name under two spellings refused `invalid_grant` as `Fails` under a listed member map that ignores letter case, and one holding one of two values a listed member map holds under one name refused the same way). `DidCommEqualityTests.JsonEqualComparesMemberNamesOrdinallyWhateverComparerAMapCarries` (with the equal hashes of its equal pair) and `DidCommEqualityTests.JsonEqualRefusesAMapHoldingOneMemberNameTwice`, the shared comparer's member names.

- **A blank subject is no subject, at every seam that supplies one.** [RFC 7523 §3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7523#section-3) item 2: "The JWT MUST contain a "sub" (subject) claim identifying the principal that is the subject of the JWT." [RFC 6749 §3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.1): "The authorization server MUST first verify the identity of the resource owner." A subject that is null, empty or white space only identifies no principal and no resource owner. The seams named below supply the subject a token is issued for, the actor its `act` claim names, or the `sub` of an ID Token, an access token or a UserInfo response. Before a token factory, a signature, a stored record or a sent response receives it, the tree refuses: a subject that is null, empty or white space only where a seam types it as a string (the JWT bearer grant's, both validated tokens of the token exchange, the authorization's, the pre-authorized decision's); a shaped actor's `sub` member that is present and is not a string with a character that is not white space; a resolver's `sub` that is a string which is empty or white space only, and for an ID Token, an access token or a UserInfo response a `sub` that is absent or is not a string; and an authenticated subject at the authorize step that is empty or white space only. The prior actors nested in an `act` chain are outside it (**Open**, below). The rule, in one sentence: a value that restates the client's request (the JWT bearer assertion, the subject token, the actor token) is answered with the error its clause gives for a request that is not valid; a value the application chose (the authorization and its shaped actor, the pre-authorized decision, the resolver's value, a claim contributor's value) is the server's own defect, `server_error`; the authorize step's absent End-User is `login_required`.
  - **The validators.** `Rfc7523AssertionValidation.Validate` and `IdJagAssertionValidation` refuse a `sub` of white space only as `MissingSubject` (`Rfc7523AssertionValidationFailureReason.MissingSubject`, `IdJagValidationFailureReason.MissingSubject`), the reading of item 2 above.
  - **The JWT bearer grant.** A `JwtBearerGrant` that the application's `ValidateJwtBearerAssertionAsync` returns with a `Subject` that is null, empty or white space only is answered 400 `invalid_grant` with the description `The JWT assertion is not valid.`, before the replay guard records the grant's `jti`. The delegate's grant restates the assertion's claims, and the library cannot tell an assertion whose `sub` is blank from a delegate that lost the subject, so it answers the case a clause governs, an assertion that is not valid: [RFC 7523 §3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7523#section-3.1), "The value of the "error" parameter MUST be the "invalid_grant" error code."
  - **The token exchange.** An authorization that the application's `AuthorizeTokenExchangeAsync` returns with a `TokenExchangeAuthorization.Subject` that is null, empty or white space only is answered 500 `server_error` with `WellKnownOAuthErrorDescriptions.ServerNotReady` right after the seam returns, before the issued token type is read: no access token, ID-JAG or refresh token is issued, no refresh token is stored, and no grant `jti` is generated. The authorization is the application's decision and names the subject of the issued token, so one that names no subject is the seam's own defect, as a `sub_id` that is not well formed is at the mint. A subject token whose validated subject is blank never reaches the seam (the next bullet), so a blank subject here is one the seam chose itself.
  - **The token exchange's subject token.** A validated subject token that the application's `ValidateTokenExchangeTokenAsync` returns with a `ValidatedSecurityToken.Subject` that is null, empty or white space only is answered as that seam's null result is: 400 `invalid_request` whatever token type the request asks for, an ID-JAG included, with the description `The subject_token is not valid.`, before the actor token is validated and before the authorization seam is called, so an authorization that copies the subject, as an impersonation policy does, never receives it; no token is issued and no `jti` is generated. The validated token restates the subject token's claims, and the library cannot tell a subject token whose `sub` is blank from a validation that lost it, so it answers the case a clause governs, a subject token that is not valid: [RFC 8693 §2.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8693#section-2.2.2), "The value of the "error" parameter MUST be the "invalid_request" error code." An ID-JAG request is no exception: [draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04 §4.3.4.3](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-4.3.4.3) reads "On an error condition, the IdP returns an OAuth 2.0 Token Error response as defined in Section 5.2 of [RFC6749], e.g:", and the `invalid_grant` it shows is the code of that example.
  - **The pre-authorized code grant.** A `PreAuthorizedCodeDecision` that the application's `ValidatePreAuthorizedCodeAsync` grants with a `Subject` that is null, empty or white space only is answered 500 `server_error` with `WellKnownOAuthErrorDescriptions.ServerNotReady`, the fixed description every defect of an application seam is answered with (`AuthCodeEndpoints.BuildPreAuthorizedCodeToken`), and nothing is issued. The subject is the application's choice, so a blank one is the server's own defect, as at the token exchange.
  - **The authorization endpoint.** An authenticated subject that is empty or white space only is no established subject: the `SubjectId` accessor of `ExchangeContextServerExtensions` reads such a value as `null` and `SetSubjectId` throws for one, so `AuthCodeEndpoints.EvaluateAuthenticationRequirementsAsync` answers it as no subject, with the `login_required` redirect of [OpenID Connect Core §3.1.2.6](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-core-1_0.html#AuthError) ("The Authorization Server requires End-User authentication.") and no authorization code. The value identifies no End-User, and the state before an End-User is authenticated is the one that redirect answers.
  - **The other grants.** The refresh grant and the client credentials grant take no subject from a seam at redemption: the refresh grant issues for the subject its stored record holds, and the client credentials grant for the registration's client identifier. The authorization code grant issues for the subject the authorize step stored, which the accessor above keeps from being blank.
  - **The token exchange's actor token.** A validated actor token that the application's `ValidateTokenExchangeTokenAsync` returns with a `ValidatedSecurityToken.Subject` that is null, empty or white space only is answered as that seam's null result is: 400 `invalid_request` with the description `The actor_token is not valid.`, before the `may_act` check and before the authorization seam is called, so no `act` claim names a blank actor. The validated token restates the actor token's claims, and the library cannot tell an actor token whose `sub` is blank from a validation that lost it, so it answers the case a clause governs, an actor token that is not valid: [RFC 8693 §2.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8693#section-2.2.2), "The value of the "error" parameter MUST be the "invalid_request" error code."
  - **The authorization's actor.** A `TokenExchangeAuthorization.Actor` whose `sub` member is present and is not a string with a character that is not white space (null, a value that is not a string, the empty string, or white space only) is answered by the check that answers a blank `TokenExchangeAuthorization.Subject`, right after the seam returns: 500 `server_error` with `WellKnownOAuthErrorDescriptions.ServerNotReady`, before the issued token type is read, so nothing is issued. The check runs whatever the issued token type, so an access or refresh token exchange whose authorization shapes such an actor is refused too, though only the ID-JAG writes the actor. The shaped actor is the application's decision and names the acting party of the ID-JAG's `act` claim, so one that names no actor is the seam's own defect, as a blank subject from the same seam is. An `Actor` without a `sub` member is written into the `act` claim as the seam shaped it.
  - **The resolver's value at the token endpoint.** After the claim contributors are merged into a producer's payload and before that payload is signed, a `sub` that is a string which is empty or white space only ends the issuance walk (`AuthCodeEndpoints.IssueTokensAsync`), and for the ID Token's and the access token's payloads so does a `sub` that is absent or is not a string: the token endpoint answers 500 `server_error` with `WellKnownOAuthErrorDescriptions.ServerNotReady` and returns no token of the walk, an access token signed before the ID Token included. The contributed `sub` is the value `ResolveSubjectIdentifierAsync` returns (`SubjectIdentifierContributor`), which [OpenID Connect Core §2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-core-1_0.html#IDToken) describes for an ID Token as "REQUIRED. Subject Identifier."; a blank one identifies no End-User and is the resolver's own defect. The ID Token and the access token are held to presence. The ID Token's producer composes no `sub` of its own, so a resolver that throws leaves a failed contribution the merge drops and the payload without a `sub`, and a resolver that returns null leaves a `sub` that is null. The access token's producer composes its `sub`, which [RFC 9068 §2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9068#section-2.2) lists as "sub REQUIRED - as defined in Section 4.1.2 of [RFC7519]."; the merge writes every successful contribution over the payload, so an application's claim contributor for the access token target can replace it with null or a value that is not a string, the contributor's own defect. The presence rule binds any producer whose `ResponseField` is `id_token` or `access_token`, an application-defined one included; a producer of another response field may carry no `sub`, and only its blank string is refused. The authorization code grant and the refresh grant claim the presented code or refresh token before the walk, so a walk that ends this way leaves it claimed, with no token issued and no refresh token stored, and a resolver fault, a transient one included, spends the presented code or refresh token.
  - **The resolver's value at the UserInfo endpoint.** A composed UserInfo response whose `sub` is absent (a claim issuer without the subject contribution, or a resolver that throws), is not a string (a resolver that returns null), or is a string that is empty or white space only, is not sent: the endpoint answers 500 `server_error` with `WellKnownOAuthErrorDescriptions.ServerNotReady` and no claim of the End-User. [OpenID Connect Core §5.3.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-core-1_0.html#UserInfoResponse): "The sub (subject) Claim MUST always be returned in the UserInfo Response." A blank value is the resolver's own defect, as at the token endpoint.
  - **The Logout Token builder.** `BackChannelLogout.BuildLogoutTokenAsync` takes its subject and its session identifier as the caller's arguments, not from a seam, and throws `ArgumentException` for a `subject` or a `sessionId` that is given and is empty or white space only, each for its own parameter, as it throws for its other arguments, so no Logout Token carries a blank `sub` or a blank `sid`. The session identifier's rule holds beside a subject too: a blank `sessionId` given with a `subject` throws for `sessionId`, and no token is built with the `sub` alone or with a blank `sid`. The rule is this library's.
  - **The end-session endpoint.** `EndSessionEndpoints` reads the verified `id_token_hint`'s `sub` and `sid` in `VerifyIdTokenHintAsync`. A `sub` that is empty or white space only identifies no End-User, so the hint is answered as one without a `sub`: 400 `invalid_request` with the description `id_token_hint is missing the sub claim.`, no redirect, no session terminated and nothing delivered to the back channel. A `sid` that is empty or white space only identifies no session and is read as absent, so the logout proceeds as for a hint without a `sid`, and neither `TerminateSessionAsync` nor `DeliverBackChannelLogoutAsync` receives it. [RP-Initiated Logout 1.0 §4](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-rpinitiated-1_0.html#ValidationAndErrorHandling): "If any of the validation procedures defined in this specification fail, any operations requiring the information that failed to correctly validate MUST be aborted and the information that failed to validate MUST NOT be used." The ID Token such a hint restates carries no blank `sid`: `SidClaimContributor.GenerateSidClaim` contributes none for an `IssuanceContext.SessionId` that is empty or white space only, the Claim [Back-Channel Logout 1.0 §2.1](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-backchannel-1_0.html#BCSupport) defines, and the authorize step's `SessionId` accessor reads such a value as none. Both readings are this library's.
  - **Deviations.** Each is named with its reason and the member whose docs state its rule. At the token endpoint, `server_error` with status 500 is outside the error codes of [RFC 6749 §5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-5.2), the error response the token endpoint's clauses point to; the code is taken from [RFC 6749 §4.1.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-4.1.2.1), the authorization endpoint's error response, and using it at the token endpoint is this library's choice, so that the server's own defect is never reported as the client's error.
    - Reading a value that is empty or white space only as no principal, no resource owner and no End-User is this library's reading; no clause states it. The reason: such a value identifies nothing, and `JwtPayload.ForAccessToken` and `JwtPayload.ForIdJag` throw for such a subject, so a request that carried one to them would end in an exception the library does not answer. Stated in `Rfc7523AssertionValidationFailureReason.MissingSubject` and `IdJagValidationFailureReason.MissingSubject`.
    - The JWT bearer grant answers a blank subject from the delegate `invalid_grant`, though the delegate and not the assertion may be at fault. The reason: the library cannot tell the two apart, and the answer is the one a clause gives for the case it governs, so that a fault that depends on the request is never answered 500. The choice is this library's; stated in `JwtBearerGrant.Subject`.
    - The token exchange answers a blank subject from the authorization seam `server_error`, and not [RFC 8693 §2.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8693#section-2.2.2)'s "If the request itself is not valid or if either the "subject_token" or "actor_token" are invalid for any reason, or are unacceptable based on policy, the authorization server MUST construct an error response, as specified in Section 5.2 of [RFC6749]. The value of the "error" parameter MUST be the "invalid_request" error code." The reason: the refused value is the authorization, which is the application's, and not the request or its tokens; a subject token whose validated subject is blank is answered before the seam is asked, with that clause's answer (the next deviation). The rule is this library's; stated in `TokenExchangeAuthorization.Subject`.
    - The token exchange answers a subject token whose validated subject is blank `invalid_request`, an ID-JAG request included, though the validation seam and not the subject token may be at fault. The reason: the library cannot tell the two apart, and the answer is the one this grant gives for a subject token that is not valid ([RFC 8693 §2.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8693#section-2.2.2)), so that a fault that depends on the request is never answered 500, even when the authorization seam copies the subject. The choice is this library's; stated in `ValidatedSecurityToken.Subject`.
    - The token exchange answers an actor token whose validated subject is blank `invalid_request`, though the validation seam and not the actor token may be at fault. The reason: the library cannot tell the two apart, and the answer is the one [RFC 8693 §2.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8693#section-2.2.2) gives for an actor token that is not valid, so that a fault that depends on the request is never answered 500. The choice is this library's; stated in `ValidatedSecurityToken.Subject`.
    - The token exchange answers a shaped actor whose `sub` member is present and names no actor `server_error`, whatever the issued token type, as it answers a blank subject from the same seam, and not with the `invalid_request` of [RFC 8693 §2.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8693#section-2.2.2). The reason: the refused value is the authorization's, which is the application's, and not the request or its tokens. The rule is this library's; stated in `TokenExchangeAuthorization.Actor`.
    - The pre-authorized code grant answers a granted decision without a subject `server_error` with the fixed description. The reason: the subject is the application's choice and not the request's, so a blank one is the server's own defect, and the fixed description tells the client nothing of the server's internals. The rule is this library's; stated in `PreAuthorizedCodeDecision.Subject`.
    - The token endpoint answers a blank value from `ResolveSubjectIdentifierAsync`, and for the ID Token and the access token a `sub` that is absent or is not a string, `server_error` and returns none of the walk's tokens; [OpenID Connect Core §2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-core-1_0.html#IDToken) makes `sub` REQUIRED for the ID Token and [RFC 9068 §2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9068#section-2.2) for the access token, and neither names an answer for an issuer that cannot give one. The reason: the value is the resolver's or a claim contributor's and not the request's, so a missing or blank one is the server's own defect, and returning the tokens signed before it would answer the request with a token without one. The rule is this library's; stated in `ResolveSubjectIdentifierDelegate` (its returns) and in `AuthCodeEndpoints.IssueTokensAsync` (its returns).
    - The UserInfo endpoint answers a response it cannot give a `sub` `server_error`; [OpenID Connect Core §5.3.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-core-1_0.html#UserInfoResponse) requires the claim and names no answer for a response that lacks it. The reason is the token endpoint's above. The rule is this library's; stated in `ResolveSubjectIdentifierDelegate` (its returns) for the resolver's value, and for a response composed without a `sub` by the endpoint's own check in `UserInfoEndpoints.BuildUserInfo`.
    - The end-session endpoint reads an `id_token_hint` whose `sub` is blank as a hint without a `sub` and answers it `invalid_request`, and reads a blank `sid` as absent; [RP-Initiated Logout 1.0 §2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-rpinitiated-1_0.html#RPLogout) states neither reading. The reason: such a value identifies no End-User and no session, and passed on it would end a session for no End-User, or reach the terminate seam and the back-channel delivery as a session identifier, where the Logout Token builder throws for it. The reading is this library's; stated beside the reads in `EndSessionEndpoints.VerifyIdTokenHintAsync`, and for the ID Token's `sid` in `SidClaimContributor.GenerateSidClaim` (its remarks).
  - Proven by `TokenExchangeGrantTests.AnAuthorizationThatNamesNoSubjectIssuesNothingAsync` (an exchange that issues an access token, and one whose requested token type is a refresh token: 500 with the fixed body, no refresh token stored), `IdJagGrantTests.MintRefusesAnAuthorizationThatNamesNoSubjectAsync` (500 with the fixed body, no grant `jti` generated), `UnmetAuthenticationRequirementsTests.ABlankSubjectFailsWithLoginRequiredOnPushedCompletion`, `ABlankSubjectFailsWithLoginRequiredOnDirectAuthorize` and `ABlankSubjectFailsWithLoginRequiredOnJarByReference` (an empty subject and a subject of one space: the `login_required` redirect, no code), `Rfc7523AssertionValidationTests.ASubjectOfWhiteSpaceOnlyIsNoSubject`, `JwtBearerGrantTests.AnAssertionWhoseSubjectIsWhiteSpaceIsRefusedAsNotValidAsync`, `JwtBearerGrantTests.AGrantWithABlankSubjectFromTheDelegateIsRefusedBeforeTheReplayDefenseAsync`, `TokenExchangeGrantTests.ASubjectTokenWhoseSubjectIsBlankIsNoValidSubjectTokenAsync` (a subject token whose validated subject is one space, copied by the authorization seam: 400 `invalid_request` for an access token, with or without an actor token presented, and for an ID-JAG, no token issued, no `jti` generated, and neither the actor token's validation nor the authorization seam called), `TokenExchangeGrantTests.AnActorTokenWhoseSubjectIsBlankIsNoValidActorTokenAsync` (an actor token whose validated subject is one space: 400 `invalid_request`, no token issued), `TokenExchangeGrantTests.AnAuthorizationWhoseActorNamesNoSubjectIssuesNothingAsync` (a shaped actor whose `sub` is one space, null, or the number 5 at an ID-JAG exchange, and one space at an exchange whose seam issues an access token: 500 with the fixed body, no token), `IdTokenIssuanceTests.AResolverThatReturnsABlankSubjectReturnsNoTokenAsync`, `AResolverThatThrowsReturnsNoTokenAsync` and `AResolverThatReturnsNullReturnsNoTokenAsync` (a resolver that returns one space, throws, or returns null for a code flow with `openid`: 500 with the fixed body, no `id_token` and no `access_token`), `IdTokenIssuanceTests.AContributorThatWritesANullSubjectForTheAccessTokenReturnsNoTokenAsync` (a claim contributor for the access token target that writes a null `sub`: 500 with the fixed body, no `access_token` and no `id_token`), `UserInfoEndpointTests.AResolverThatReturnsABlankSubjectSendsNoUserInfoResponseAsync`, `AResolverThatThrowsSendsNoUserInfoResponseAsync`, `AResolverThatReturnsNullSendsNoUserInfoResponseAsync` and `AResponseComposedWithoutASubjectIsNotSentAsync` (a resolver that returns one space, throws, or returns null, and a claim issuer without the subject identifier rule: 500 with the fixed body, no claim of the End-User), `Oid4VciPreAuthorizedCodeGrantTests.AGrantedCodeWithoutASubjectIssuesNothingAsync` (a grant with a subject of one space: 500 with the fixed body), `BackChannelLogoutTokenTests.BuildRejectsABlankSubject` and `BuildRejectsABlankSessionId` (the Logout Token builder throws `ArgumentException` for a `subject` given with a `sid`, and for a `sessionId` given without a subject or beside one, that is empty or one space, each for its own parameter), `EndSessionLogoutTests.AnIdTokenHintWhoseSubjectIsBlankIsRefusedAsync` (a hint signed by the host whose `sub` is one space, over the wire: 400 `invalid_request` with `id_token_hint is missing the sub claim.`, no redirect, nothing terminated, nothing delivered), `EndSessionLogoutTests.AnIdTokenHintWhoseSessionIdentifierIsBlankTerminatesWithoutASessionAsync` (a hint whose `sid` is one space, over the wire: the redirect with the `state`, the terminate seam and the delivery seam each called once with the subject and no session identifier), `RefreshedIdTokenParityTests.AnIdTokenIssuedForABlankSessionIdentifierCarriesNoSidAsync` (an authorize context whose session entry is one space, over the wire: the ID Token carries its `sub` and no `sid`) and `RefreshedIdTokenParityTests.TheSidContributorContributesNoSidForABlankSessionIdentifierAsync` (an ID Token target whose `IssuanceContext.SessionId` is one space: no `sid` contributed).
  - **Open.** The prior actors nested under the current actor in an `act` chain are carried as given, and a `sub` inside such a chain that is empty or white space only is written as given: the subject token's own `act` (`ValidatedSecurityToken.Act`), and the `act` member nested in a shaped `TokenExchangeAuthorization.Actor`.

- **The one-time step: claimed before the walk, released when the walk ends before the step's first store write.** [OAuth 2.1 draft-16 §4.1.3](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-v2-1-16#section-4.1.3): "The authorization server MUST return an access token only once for a given authorization code." [RFC 6749 §4.1.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-4.1.2): "The client MUST NOT use the authorization code more than once." and, for a code used more than once, "the authorization server MUST deny the request and SHOULD revoke (when possible)". [RFC 6749 §10.5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-10.5): "Authorization codes MUST be short lived and single-use." [OAuth 2.1 draft-16 §4.3.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-v2-1-16#section-4.3.1): "Authorization servers MUST utilize one of these methods to detect refresh token replay by malicious actors for public clients:" [RFC 9700 §2.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9700#section-2.2.2): "Refresh tokens for public clients MUST be sender-constrained or use refresh token rotation as described in Section 4.14."
  - **The ordering.** The token endpoint claims the presented grant's one-time step (`ServerIntegration.ClaimFlowStateAsync`) after every verification of the request has passed and before the issuance walk: the subject resolution, the claim contributors, the token producers and their signatures. Two reasons hold it there. Under concurrency the claim is what makes the exactly-once MUST hold: of two presentations of one code or one refresh token, one wins the claim and the other is refused `invalid_grant` before it mints anything. And a replayed grant is refused after the request's verification and before any token is signed, the subject resolved or a producer run, so a party holding an already-spent grant cannot make the server pay the walk's cost by presenting it again.
  - **The reading of a use.** This library reads a use of an authorization code as a redemption that issued tokens, after [OAuth 2.1 draft-16 §4.1.3](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-v2-1-16#section-4.1.3): "If a second valid token request is made with the same authorization code as a previously successful token request, the authorization server MUST deny the request". RFC 6749 Section 4.1.2's "used more than once" and Section 10.5's "single-use" are read the same way: a redemption whose walk ended before the step's first store write issued nothing, so it is not a use and the code can be redeemed again. The refresh token's rotation is read the same way under RFC 9700 Section 2.2.2: a refresh that rotated nothing has not used the presented refresh token.
  - **The release and its window.** When the walk ends after a won claim and before the step's first store write made by the library, by a failure it returns (a subject resolver that throws, answers null or answers a blank value; a signing key the resolver does not return; no applicable producer), by an exception it throws (a producer that throws, or any other seam whose exception escapes the walk, such as the identifier seam) or by the request's cancellation, the endpoint releases the claim through `ServerIntegration.ReleaseFlowStateAsync`, so the same grant presented again can complete: a fault of the application's seams does not spend the client's grant. The window is bounded by the library's first store write. The application's seams the walk calls (the token producers, the claim contributors, the resolvers and the identifier seam) run again when a released grant is presented again, so they keep no effect that a repeated walk would duplicate, or the application reconciles such effects itself.
  - **A release that fails.** When the walk threw, the walk's exception stays the request's fault: should the release throw as well, the release's exception is recorded on the request's activity when one is being recorded, and an application that needs every release failure observes it inside its release delegate. When the walk returned a failure, the walk has no exception of its own, so an exception of the release propagates as the request's fault. The library does not retry a release, so a release that throws or times out leaves the store's outcome to the store: a store that fails without removing its mark leaves the step claimed, so the grant is spent and the server fails closed; a store whose release may remove the mark and then throw documents that for its host, since the grant can then be presented again.
  - **The release's token and its bound.** The release runs under a token that the request's cancellation does not cancel, since that cancellation may be what ended the walk; the token cannot be cancelled. The application bounds its release inside its delegate, for example by its store's own timeout: a release that does not complete holds the request and the drain of an alteration that waits for it. The library calls the release at most once per won claim, so a store need not track which caller holds a mark.
  - **Fail closed after persistence.** From the step's first store write on, a failure leaves the step claimed, whatever fails afterwards and the write itself included. This rule is this library's: the write may have taken effect, and a persisted record of the step, such as a refresh token born of the grant, may already be live. A walk that completes is not released either, since the state it returns is the step's effect, which the dispatcher persists.
  - **The application's part.** The release is a required seam beside the claim (`ReleaseServerFlowStateDelegate`), and structural validation names `ReleaseFlowStateAsync` when it is missing. The host wires it on the same store as the claim and publishes it together with the load, claim, delete, save and correlation operations: an in-memory store removes the claim mark, a distributed store deletes the conditional-write mark or reverts the compare-and-swap its claim made, and a store whose claim always succeeds releases as a no-op. Releasing a pair that is not claimed does nothing, and the release neither steps, saves nor deletes the flow record.
  - **The two steps.** The authorization code's redemption, whose window ends at the save of the issued refresh token's `ServerRefreshTokenIssuedState`, and the refresh token's rotation, whose window ends at the save of the successor's `ServerRefreshTokenIssuedState` (`AuthCodeEndpoints`, the code grant's and the refresh grant's token steps, which share `ReleaseClaimedStepAsync`; their claim sites state the reasoning above in their comments, and the remarks of `ClaimServerFlowStateDelegate` and `ReleaseServerFlowStateDelegate` state it for the store).
  - Proven by `IdTokenIssuanceTests.ACodeSurvivesAWalkThatFailsBeforePersistenceAsync` and `RefreshGrantTests.ARefreshTokenSurvivesAWalkThatFailsBeforePersistenceAsync` (a subject resolver that throws, a failure the claim issuer records so the walk returns it: 500, and the same grant presented again answers 200, the refresh rotated), `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.ACodeSurvivesAWalkThatThrowsBeforePersistenceAsync` and `RefreshGrantTests.ARefreshTokenSurvivesAWalkThatThrowsBeforePersistenceAsync` (the identifier seam throws at the draw of the refresh token's value: 500 with the fault captured, then 200), `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.ACodeSurvivesAWalkThatTheRequestsCancellationEndsAsync` and `RefreshGrantTests.ARefreshTokenSurvivesAWalkThatTheRequestsCancellationEndsAsync` (the request's cancellation ends the walk; the claim is released once under a token that cannot be cancelled, and the same grant presented again answers 200), `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.TheWalksExceptionStaysPrimaryWhenTheCodesReleaseThrowsAsync` and `RefreshGrantTests.TheWalksExceptionStaysPrimaryWhenTheRefreshReleaseThrowsAsync` (the walk's exception is the captured fault, the release's is an `exception` event of the request's trace, and the grant presented again answers 400 `invalid_grant`), `IdTokenIssuanceTests.AReleaseThatThrowsAfterAReturnedFailureFailsTheRedemptionAsync` and `RefreshGrantTests.AReleaseThatThrowsAfterAReturnedFailureFailsTheRefreshAsync` (the walk returns a failure and the release throws before it removes the mark: the release's exception is the captured fault, and the grant presented again answers 400 `invalid_grant`), `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.AFirstStoreWriteThatTakesEffectAndThrowsLeavesTheCodeSpentAsync` and `RefreshGrantTests.AFirstStoreWriteThatTakesEffectAndThrowsLeavesTheRefreshTokenSpentAsync` (the first store write takes effect and throws: the release is not called and the grant presented again answers 400 `invalid_grant`), `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.AFailureAfterPersistenceBeganLeavesTheCodeSpentAsync` (no release of a walk that completed: the dispatcher's save of the resulting state throws, and the same code presented again answers 400 `invalid_grant`), `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.ConcurrentTokenRedemptionOfSameCodeYieldsExactlyOneSuccess`, `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.ValidReplayOfRedeemedCodeIsInvalidGrantAndRevokesTheIssuedAccessToken` and `AuthorizationServerFeatureTests.IntegrationValidateThrowsWhenReleaseFlowStateAsyncIsMissing`.
  - **Open.** The replay defence the JWT bearer grant uses (`JtiReplayGuard`) records a presented assertion's `jti` as a saved record and then claims that record's first step, so the saved record itself is the replay signal, and a walk that fails after it leaves the assertion refused as a replay. Releasing that claim alone would not restore the assertion, since the record's presence refuses a later presentation before the claim is consulted. A restoration would have to delete the record as well, which has to be reconciled with the claim's own retention rule: a deletion must not let a stale caller claim a consumed step again.

- **The token exchange request seam, the issued type and the ID-JAG mint rule.** [RFC 8693 §2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8693#section-2.1) defines the request: "Client authentication to the authorization server is done using the normal mechanisms provided by OAuth 2.0." and, among its parameters, "requested_token_type OPTIONAL. An identifier, as described in Section 3, for the type of the requested security token. If the requested type is unspecified, the issued token type is at the discretion of the authorization server and may be dictated by knowledge of the requirements of the service or resource indicated by the "resource" or "audience" parameter." and "audience OPTIONAL. The logical name of the target service where the client intends to use the requested security token." [RFC 8693 §2.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8693#section-2.2.2) names two codes: "If the request itself is not valid or if either the "subject_token" or "actor_token" are invalid for any reason, or are unacceptable based on policy, the authorization server MUST construct an error response, as specified in Section 5.2 of [RFC6749]. The value of the "error" parameter MUST be the "invalid_request" error code." and "If the authorization server is unwilling or unable to issue a token for any target service indicated by the "resource" or "audience" parameters, the "invalid_target" error code SHOULD be used in the error response." [RFC 8707 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2) provides that code for a token request: "The following error code is provided for an authorization server to indicate problems with the requested resource(s) in response to an authorization request or access token request." [draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04 §4.3](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-4.3) (draft-04 below) defines the request for an ID-JAG: "requested_token_type: REQUIRED - The value urn:ietf:params:oauth:token-type:id-jag indicates that an Identity Assertion JWT Authorization Grant is being requested." and "audience: REQUIRED - The identifier of the Resource Authorization Server in another trust domain as the intended audience for the ID-JAG." The application decides which request shapes this server serves and gives its reason; the library writes the error.
  - **The seam.** `ValidateTokenExchangeRequestDelegate` takes the `TokenExchangeRequest` after the library's parameter checks, the authenticated `ClientRecord`, the `ExchangeContext` and the cancellation token, and answers a `TokenExchangeRequestRefusal` (`Parameter`, the refused parameter's name as `OAuthRequestParameterNames` spells it, and `Description`) or `null` to continue. It is wired as `AuthorizationServerIntegration.ValidateTokenExchangeRequestAsync`, which is optional: unwired, the endpoint skips it, and the grant's activation does not depend on it. `AuthCodeEndpoints.BuildTokenExchange` runs in this order: the client's identification and authentication, the issuer resolution (`ResolveIssuerAsync`), the RFC 9449 proof check, the library's parameter checks (a single-valued parameter sent with a value more than once, `subject_token` and `subject_token_type`, `actor_token` and `actor_token_type`, the `requested_token_type` identifier, the `resource` grammar and fold, the `audience` set, the ID-JAG audience count, an `authorization_details` of white space only), the request seam, the subject token's validation (`ValidateTokenExchangeTokenAsync`), the actor token's, the `may_act` check, the authorization seam, the issued type against a stated `requested_token_type`, the subject token's binding to a proof-of-possession key, and for an ID-JAG the mint's audience count and the seam's shaped audience. A request shape the application refuses is answered before its subject token is validated. The request the seam receives carries the presented `subject_token` and `actor_token` unvalidated: the seam judges the request's shape and must not trust, return or log those two values, and the records' text leaves them out (`TokenExchangeRequest.ToString`, as `TokenExchangeActor.ToString`, `TokenExchangeBuilderOptions.ToString`, `IdJagMintOptions.ToString` and `IdJagRedeemOptions.ToString` on the client side, the last leaving out the ID-JAG presented as the assertion).
  - **The code.** The library derives the error code from `TokenExchangeRequestRefusal.Parameter`, compared ordinally: `resource` and `audience` give `invalid_target`, the code RFC 8693 Section 2.2.2 gives a target service the server will not issue a token for and RFC 8707 Section 2 provides for a token request; every other parameter gives `invalid_request`, RFC 8693 Section 2.2.2's MUST for a request that is not valid. The answer is 400 with `Description` as the `error_description`. `ServerHttpResponse.BadRequest` writes it through `ErrorDescriptionCharset.Sanitize`, which removes every character [RFC 6749 §5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-5.2) excludes ("Values for the "error_description" parameter MUST NOT include characters outside the set %x20-21 / %x23-5B / %x5D-7E."), so the record checks nothing when it is built; reducing the text rather than refusing it is this library's rule. The record's text shows `Parameter` alone. The derivation is this library's reading, with its reason: the library, not the application, names the code, so one parameter is answered with one code at every endpoint (a malformed or unacceptable `resource` is `invalid_target` at every endpoint, the token exchange included, the entry on resource indicators above) and the code follows the refused parameter, never the application's wording. A refused `scope` is answered `invalid_request`, although [RFC 6749 §5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-5.2) defines `invalid_scope` ("The requested scope is invalid, unknown, malformed, or exceeds the scope granted by the resource owner.") and RFC 8693 Section 2.2.2 reads "Other error codes may also be used, as appropriate.": the token exchange's own clause names `invalid_request` for a request the server will not serve, and another code is permitted there, not required, so the derivation keeps the MUST code for every parameter that names no target.
  - **The ID-JAG mint rule.** `IdJagMintRequestRule.Build` takes an explicit, non-empty set of `audience` values (an ordinal copy; an empty set, or one holding a member that is null, empty or white space only, throws `ArgumentException`, a null one `ArgumentNullException`) and returns a `ValidateTokenExchangeRequestDelegate`. It refuses, naming `requested_token_type` with the description `requested_token_type must be urn:ietf:params:oauth:token-type:id-jag.`, a request whose `subject_token_type` is `urn:ietf:params:oauth:token-type:id_token`, `urn:ietf:params:oauth:token-type:saml2` or `urn:ietf:params:oauth:token-type:refresh_token` (the three draft-04 Section 4.3 names), whose one `audience` is in the set and whose `requested_token_type` is absent or is not `urn:ietf:params:oauth:token-type:id-jag`; the endpoint answers it 400 `invalid_request`. A request it cannot classify (no `audience`, several, one outside the set, another `subject_token_type`) passes untouched. The classification is this library's rule. The set has no mode that covers every audience: an IdP mints ID-JAGs for the Resource Authorization Servers it knows, and only a request naming one of them is read as an ID-JAG request. A member that names no audience could match no request, since the endpoint drops an empty `audience` and refuses one of white space only, so a rule over it would refuse nothing; it is the caller's bad argument and throws. The rule is opt-in: the application wires it as `ValidateTokenExchangeRequestAsync` alone, or composes it in its own delegate, which calls the rule's delegate first, returns a refusal the rule answers, and otherwise runs its own checks.
  - **The issued type, the token codes and the request's values.** Each is this library's reading of the clause it names.
    - A stated `requested_token_type` the authorization seam does not issue is answered 400 `invalid_request` with `The requested_token_type cannot be issued for this request.`, before any token identifier, signing material or stored state is obtained. The reason: RFC 8693 Section 2.1 leaves the issued type to the server only "If the requested type is unspecified", so a stated type the server will not issue is a request it does not honor, and Section 2.2.2's MUST names the code. A request that states no type leaves the choice to the authorization seam.
    - A subject token or an actor token the validation seam does not accept, or whose validated subject is blank, is answered 400 `invalid_request` whatever type the request asks for, an ID-JAG included (RFC 8693 Section 2.2.2's MUST, quoted above). A denial by the authorization seam is answered `invalid_target` when the request names a `resource` or an `audience` (Section 2.2.2's SHOULD, quoted above) and `invalid_request` when it names neither, an ID-JAG request included; an ID-JAG request names its Resource Authorization Server as the `audience`, so its denial is `invalid_target`. The library cannot tell why the authorization seam denied, so it reads a denial of a request that names a `resource` or an `audience` as unwillingness to issue for that target, this library's rule: a denial on the requested scope (a scope beyond the authorization context of the subject token, for one) is answered `invalid_target` as well, although RFC 6749 Section 5.2 defines `invalid_scope` for a scope that "exceeds the scope granted by the resource owner" (the sentence quoted under the code above). The reason for the ID-JAG codes: [draft-04 §4.3.4.3](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-4.3.4.3) reads "On an error condition, the IdP returns an OAuth 2.0 Token Error response as defined in Section 5.2 of [RFC6749], e.g:"; the `invalid_grant` it shows is the code of that example and does not set the token exchange's MUST aside. The Resource Authorization Server's redemption of an ID-JAG through the JWT bearer grant keeps its own `invalid_grant`.
    - An ID-JAG names one Resource Authorization Server (draft-04 Section 4.3's `audience`, quoted above). When the authorization seam chooses the id-jag issued type without shaping its audience, a request naming no `audience` is answered 400 `invalid_request` with `An ID-JAG is issued for exactly one audience; the request named none.` and one naming several with `An ID-JAG is issued for exactly one audience; the request named several.`, before the grant's `jti` is obtained; the descriptions name the issued ID-JAG, since such a request may state no `requested_token_type`. A request that states the id-jag type is counted earlier, with the descriptions of the parameter checks. The reason: the count is keyed on what is issued. A shaped audience whose first value is null, empty or white space only names no Resource Authorization Server, and a shaped audience of more than one value names more than the one the grant's `aud` holds: either is the seam's own defect, answered 500 `server_error` with `WellKnownOAuthErrorDescriptions.ServerNotReady` before the `jti`, as a tenant value of white space only from the same seam is. So an ID-JAG is never minted for the first of several audiences, requested or shaped.
    - An `authorization_details` of white space only is answered 400 `invalid_authorization_details` with `authorization_details could not be parsed.`, the answer this endpoint gives an `authorization_details` it cannot parse, before the request seam and before the subject token is validated. The reason: [RFC 6749 §3.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.2) reads "Parameters sent without a value MUST be treated as if they were omitted from the request.", so a value of white space only is a value and no omission, while an empty value is omitted (the authorization seam receives none); the library's own read of the parameter would drop a value of white space only before either seam, so the library refuses it rather than lose it, and parsing and processing any other value is the application's, in its seams, under the MUST of [draft-04 §4.3.3](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-4.3.3): "If authorization_details is present, the IdP MUST parse it as a JSON array and process each authorization detail object according to [RFC9396]." The code is the one [RFC 9396 §5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9396#section-5) gives details that do not conform ("The AS MUST refuse to process any unknown authorization details type or authorization details not conforming to the respective type definition.") and [RFC 9396 §6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9396#section-6) names at the token request ("Otherwise, the AS refuses the request with the error code invalid_authorization_details (similar to invalid_scope)."). A `scope` of white space only holds no `scope-token` under [RFC 6749 §3.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.3)'s grammar, `scope-token = 1*( %x21 / %x23-5B / %x5D-7E )`, and the library reads it as omitted at every token request, the reading `ResolveEffectiveScope`'s summary states; that reading is this library's. It reads the value's content: on the wire such a value is sent with a value, so the repeat count below counts it. An `authorization_details` of white space only is read otherwise, as above: it is not a JSON array, and the library's own read would drop it before either seam could parse it, so the library refuses it.
    - A single-valued parameter (`subject_token`, `subject_token_type`, `actor_token`, `actor_token_type`, `requested_token_type` and `scope`, which [RFC 8693 §2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8693#section-2.1) defines for the request, and `authorization_details`, the token request parameter of [RFC 9396 §6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9396#section-6)) sent with a value more than once is answered 400 `invalid_request` with `A token exchange parameter was sent more than once.`, before the subject token is read, before the request seam and before the subject token is validated. The count reads only the occurrences with a value: an occurrence that is null or empty is sent without a value and omitted, so `actor_token=&actor_token=x` holds one occurrence of `actor_token` and is answered as the request that sends `actor_token=x` alone, while an occurrence of white space only is a value and counts. Each of the seven is read as its one occurrence with a value, and the library never writes into the request's `RequestFields`: the application's seams see the request as it was received (`IncomingRequest.Fields`). `resource` and `audience` repeat, as RFC 8693 Section 2.1 lets each of them name several targets: "Multiple "resource" parameters may be used to indicate that the issued token is intended to be used at the multiple resources listed." and "Multiple "audience" parameters may be used to indicate that the issued token is intended to be used at the multiple audiences listed." The reason: the single value is the rule of [RFC 6749 §3.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.2), "Request and response parameters MUST NOT be included more than once.", beside that section's "Parameters sent without a value MUST be treated as if they were omitted from the request.", and a parameter sent with a value more than once could be read as no value at all: a repeated `requested_token_type` would leave the issued type to the authorization seam, a repeated `actor_token` would turn a delegation into an impersonation, a repeated `scope` would hand the authorization seam no requested scope, and a repeated `authorization_details` would carry no authorization details into the issued token. Refusing the request is this library's reading; RFC 8693 Section 2.2.2's MUST names the code.
  - **The application's turn.** Every policy beyond the shape is the application's: which client may mint for which Resource Authorization Server, for which subjects and scopes, in its own request seam and in `AuthorizeTokenExchangeAsync`; and which `audience` values the rule's set holds, the implementation-specific values draft-04 Section 4.3 lets an IdP support included.
  - **Deviations.** Each is named with its reason.
    - Outside the rule's classification, draft-04 Section 4.3's REQUIRED `requested_token_type` is not enforced: an exchange that omits it reaches the authorization seam, and when that seam chooses the id-jag issued type the endpoint mints an ID-JAG (an exchange that states another type is refused, the bullet above). The reason: RFC 8693 Section 2.1 makes the parameter OPTIONAL for every exchange and leaves an unspecified type to the server, and without the set of Resource Authorization Servers the server mints for, the endpoint cannot tell an ID-JAG request that omits it from a plain exchange; the rule over an explicit set is how an application opts in.
    - The rule reads a request as an ID-JAG request only by one `audience` in its set; a request naming that audience beside another passes the rule. The library's audience count refuses it `invalid_request` when it states the id-jag type, and the mint's audience count when the authorization seam issues an ID-JAG without shaping its audience. The reason: draft-04 Section 4.3's `audience` is "The identifier of the Resource Authorization Server", one, and the rule refuses nothing it cannot classify.
    - A refused `scope` is answered `invalid_request`, not RFC 6749 Section 5.2's `invalid_scope`; the reason is under the code above.
  - **Open.** The exchange's refusals of a subject token bound to a proof-of-possession key (`ValidatedSecurityToken.RequiredKeyThumbprint`) that no presented proof matches, a proof required and none presented or a proof made with another public key, answer 400 `invalid_grant`, by analogy with [draft-04 §9.8.1.2](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-9.8.1.2), which governs the Resource Authorization Server's redemption of an ID-JAG (it opens "When a client exchanges an ID-JAG for an access token at the Resource Authorization Server"); [draft-04 §9.8.1.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-9.8.1.1), which governs the token exchange, names no code. RFC 8693 Section 2.2.2's MUST, quoted above, names `invalid_request` for a subject token that is "unacceptable based on policy", and [RFC 9449 §5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-5) defines `invalid_dpop_proof` for a proof that is not valid: "If the DPoP proof is invalid, the authorization server issues an error response per Section 5.2 of [RFC6749] with invalid_dpop_proof as the value of the error parameter." Which code the token exchange answers is not settled.
  - **Open.** The library reads RFC 6749's repeat rule in two ways. The token exchange omits an occurrence sent without a value before it counts (the bullet on single-valued parameters above), under RFC 6749 Section 3.2's two sentences. The authorization and pushed authorization endpoints refuse any repeat of `request` and `request_uri`, and the code grant at the token endpoint any repeat of `redirect_uri`, an empty occurrence included (`OAuthRequestFieldsExtensions.HasRepeatedOccurrence`, whose summary holds that "MUST NOT be included more than once" holds even when every repeated occurrence is itself empty); [RFC 6749 §3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.1) states the same two sentences for the authorization endpoint. Which one reading the library holds at every endpoint is not settled.
  - Proven by `TokenExchangeGrantTests.ARequestSeamRefusalAnswersBeforeTheSubjectTokenIsValidatedAsync` (a refusal of `requested_token_type`: 400 `invalid_request` with the description, the subject-token validator not called), `TokenExchangeGrantTests.AnUnauthenticatedClientIsRefusedBeforeTheRequestSeamAsync` (wrong form client credentials at a server that advertises no `client_secret_basic`: 400 `invalid_client`, the request seam not called), `TokenExchangeGrantTests.ARequestTheLibraryRefusesNeverReachesTheRequestSeamAsync` (a `resource` with a fragment: 400 `invalid_target` with the library's description; an `audience` of one space: 400 `invalid_request`; a registration that requires a DPoP proof, with none: 400 `use_dpop_nonce`; the request seam not called in each), `TokenExchangeGrantTests.WithoutTheRequestSeamRequestedTokenTypeStaysOptionalAsync` (no seam and no `requested_token_type`: 200 with the type the authorization seam chose), `TokenExchangeGrantTests.ARefusalOfATargetIsAnsweredInvalidTargetAsync` (a refusal of `resource` and one of `audience`: 400 `invalid_target` with the description), `TokenExchangeGrantTests.ARefusalDescriptionIsWrittenInsideTheRfc6749CharsetAsync` (a description holding U+0022 and U+00E9: written without them), `TokenExchangeGrantTests.AStatedRequestedTokenTypeTheAuthorizationDoesNotIssueIsRefusedAsync` (the access token type stated and an ID-JAG chosen, the ID-JAG type stated and an access token chosen: 400 `invalid_request` with the description, no `jti` generated), `TokenExchangeGrantTests.ASubjectTokenWhoseSubjectIsBlankIsNoValidSubjectTokenAsync` (an ID-JAG request among the cases: 400 `invalid_request`), `IdJagGrantTests.MintWithInvalidSubjectTokenIsInvalidRequest`, `IdJagGrantTests.MintEnforcesSubjectTokenAudienceEqualsClient`, `IdJagGrantTests.MintWithInvalidRefreshTokenSubjectIsInvalidRequest` and `IdJagGrantTests.MintWithSamlAssertionSubjectTokenMintsIdJag` (a subject token the validation seam does not accept: 400 `invalid_request` with `The subject_token is not valid.`), `IdJagGrantTests.MintDeniedByPolicyIsInvalidTarget` and `IdJagGrantTests.MintWithRefreshTokenSubjectScopeBeyondContextIsInvalidTarget` (a denied ID-JAG request naming its audience: 400 `invalid_target` with `The token exchange was not authorized.`), `IdJagGrantTests.MintRefusesAShapedAudienceOfWhiteSpaceFromTheSeamAsync` and `IdJagGrantTests.MintRefusesAShapedAudienceOfSeveralFromTheSeamAsync` (a shaped audience of one space, and one of two values: 500 with the fixed body, no `jti` generated), `TokenExchangeGrantTests.AnAuthorizationDetailsOfWhiteSpaceIsRefusedBeforeEitherSeamAsync` (400 `invalid_authorization_details` with the description; the request seam, the subject-token validator and the authorization seam not called), `TokenExchangeGrantTests.AnEmptyAuthorizationDetailsIsOmittedAsync` (an empty value: 200, the authorization seam receiving no `authorization_details`), `TokenExchangeGrantTests.ASingleValuedParameterSentMoreThanOnceIsRefusedBeforeEitherSeamAsync` (`requested_token_type` sent twice; `actor_token` sent twice with its type once; both sent twice; `scope`, `authorization_details`, `subject_token` and `subject_token_type` each sent twice; an `actor_token` of one space beside one with a value: 400 `invalid_request` with the description, the request seam, the token validator and the authorization seam not called), `TokenExchangeGrantTests.AnActorTokenSentWithoutAValueBesideOneWithAValueIsOneOccurrenceAsync` (`actor_token=` then `actor_token` with a value, its type once: 200, two token validations, the authorization seam receiving the valued `actor_token` and its type), `TokenExchangeGrantTests.ARequestedTokenTypeSentWithoutAValueBesideOneWithAValueIsOneOccurrenceAsync` (`requested_token_type=` then the access token type: 200, the authorization seam receiving the access token type), `TokenExchangeGrantTests.AnOccurrenceSentWithoutAValueStaysInTheRequestTheApplicationSeesAsync` (`actor_token=` then `actor_token` with a value: 200, the incoming request the authorization seam's context carries holding both occurrences as sent), `IdJagGrantTests.TheIdJagMintRuleRequiresTheIdJagTypeForItsAudiencesAsync` (the rule over the Resource Authorization Server's issuer, an ID Token subject: `requested_token_type` absent or the access token type, 400 `invalid_request` with the rule's description and neither the subject-token validator nor the authorization seam called; the id-jag type, 200 and an ID-JAG; no `audience`, or two, the authorization seam reached once and the mint's audience count, 400 `invalid_request` with its description and no `jti` generated; one `audience` outside the set, or an access token subject, the authorization seam reached once and an ID-JAG minted), `TokenExchangeRecordTests.ATokenExchangeRequestRendersNoPresentedTokenAsText`, `TokenExchangeRecordTests.ATokenExchangeActorRendersNoPresentedTokenAsText`, `TokenExchangeRecordTests.TokenExchangeBuilderOptionsRenderNoPresentedTokenAsText`, `TokenExchangeRecordTests.IdJagMintOptionsRenderNoPresentedTokenAsText`, `TokenExchangeRecordTests.IdJagRedeemOptionsRenderNoPresentedAssertionAsText` and `TokenExchangeRecordTests.ARefusalRendersItsParameterOnly` (the records' text holds no part of a presented token or assertion; the refusal's shows its parameter alone), and `TokenExchangeRecordTests.BuildRefusesANullSetOfAudiences`, `TokenExchangeRecordTests.BuildRefusesAnEmptySetOfAudiences`, `TokenExchangeRecordTests.BuildRefusesAMemberThatNamesNoAudience` (a null, an empty and a one-space member: `ArgumentException`) and `TokenExchangeRecordTests.TheRuleKeepsTheSetItWasBuiltOverAsync` (the source list changed after `Build`: the answers unchanged).

- **A presented JWS fails to verify as a value: the registry answers an unserved pair, and `Jws` answers presented input.** [RFC 8725 Section 3.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8725#section-3.3): "All cryptographic operations used in the JWT MUST be validated and the entire JWT MUST be rejected if any of them fail to validate." [RFC 7515 Section 5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7515#section-5.2): "If any of the listed steps fails, then the signature or MAC cannot be validated." [RFC 7515 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7515#section-7.1) gives the JWS Compact Serialization as three base64url parts joined by periods. A key proof, an access token, a client assertion or any other JWS a server endpoint receives is the presenter's input, and so is the public key it is verified with when that public key is read from the JWS itself (a key proof's header `jwk`).
  - **An unserved pair.** A `PatternMatcher` answers `null` for an (algorithm, purpose) pair its backend does not serve and never throws for one; the last arm of the matchers the library registers (`CryptoProviderStartup`), of the test registry (`TestSetup`) and of the browser-wasm smoke project answers `null`, and a test's deliberate fault-injection matcher (the throwing dependency of `VcalmVerifierEndpointTests`) is the one matcher that throws, on purpose. `CryptoFunctionRegistry.TryResolveVerification` answers `false` with no function for such a pair and for a registry that is not initialized. `ResolveVerification` and `ResolveSigning` take the pair as their caller's argument and throw `ArgumentException` naming the algorithm, the purpose and the qualifier for such a pair; a registry that is not initialized makes them throw `InvalidOperationException`. The tree's other verifiers of presented input resolve a presented pair through `ResolveVerification` and fail closed by its `ArgumentException` where they catch it; moving them onto `TryResolveVerification` is not this commit. An application's matcher that throws for an unserved pair faults every caller, a verifier that resolves through `TryResolveVerification` included.
  - **`Jws`.** A presented compact JWS never throws out of a `Jws` verify body: every way it fails to verify is the body's not-verified value (`false`, or a `JwsVerificationResult` whose `IsValid` is `false`): a JWS longer than the bound (`Jws.DefaultMaxJwsLength` or the caller's `maxJwsLength`, this library's own guard against a large allocation, stated by no specification), one that is not three parts, a segment that is empty where a part cannot be, that is not the canonical unpadded base64url [RFC 7515 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7515#section-2) defines (padding, white space, a character outside the alphabet, a length of 1 modulo 4, a set bit of the last character that carries no octet; the library checks the form itself before the caller's decoder runs, since the .NET `Base64Url` decoders accept padding and skip white space, so each segment is read only in the one canonical spelling of its encoding; that is a rule on each segment's encoding, not a claim that a token has one spelling: an ECDSA signature (r, s) has the twin (r, n - s), which [RFC 7518 Section 3.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7518#section-3.4) does not forbid and the library does not refuse), or that the base64url decoder refuses (`FormatException`) or decodes to no octets, a protected header that fails the JSON gate ([RFC 7515 Section 5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7515#section-5.2), step 3: octets that are not valid UTF-8, no well-formed JSON object by the library's reader, a member name repeated at any depth ([RFC 7515 Section 4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7515#section-4) and [RFC 7519 Section 4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7519#section-4) for the names of the JOSE Header and of the JWT Claims Set, [RFC 8259 Section 4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8259#section-4) for a repeat at a nested depth, [RFC 7517 Section 4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7517#section-4) for the members of a header's `jwk`), or a document beyond the reader's depth, member-count and length bounds, this library's own guard whose values no specification states) or that names a critical extension the consumer does not understand, a claims set that fails the same gate where the body reads the payload as a JWT Claims Set ([RFC 7519 Section 7.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7519#section-7.2), step 10; the three `VerifyAndDecodeAsync` bodies and the resolver `VerifyAsync`), both checked before the caller's part decoder runs, a protected header or claims set the caller's part decoder refuses with `FormatException` (the library's own `JwtPartJson.Default` refuses a number outside the range it reads, an empty member name and an escaped unpaired surrogate that way; one private boundary in `Jws` answers it), a payload segment that is not base64url in the explicit compact `VerifyAsync` (checked before the backend is asked), a signature the backend refuses (one boundary for every body, the resolver bodies included), a public key whose pair the registry does not serve (the registry overloads of `VerifyAsync` and `VerifyAndDecodeAsync` resolve through `TryResolveVerification`), and a resolver that answers no key material for the presented header and payload. The length is checked first and the part count second, both before any decoding. A decoder's refusal of presented input is `FormatException` (the documented contract of `DecodeDelegate` and `JwtPartDecoder`; a base64url decoder refuses a non-empty input that decodes to no octets the same way and rents no buffer of no octets, as the library's `u` multibase payload decoder in `CryptoFormatConversions` and the test tree's decoder do); any other exception of a decoder is its own fault and propagates. What throws, generally, is what the caller composes or a dependency that faults: a `null` argument `ArgumentNullException`, a bound of zero or less `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`, cancellation, a registry overload when `IsInitialized` is `false` `InvalidOperationException` naming the registry, the `JwsMessage` overloads of `VerifyAsync` for a message with several signatures `InvalidOperationException`, and every exception a caller's delegate (a decoder, a resolver, a binder, a verification function) or the pool raises for its own fault, a verification backend's exception outside the refusals the bodies answer included. The providers answer presented material provider by provider. Every BouncyCastle verifier answers `false`, raising nothing, for presented public key and signature octets it or BouncyCastle refuses, and reads only the encodings its algorithm defines (for RSA also the bare modulus under the exponent 65537, this library's own convention, not RFC 8017's; an RSA verifier of either provider reads the public key octets, which do not state their own encoding, by form, by one rule in this order, stated once in each provider's class documentation: octets that are exactly a DER `RSAPublicKey`, whatever their length, are that modulus and that exponent; any other octets are the bare modulus they spell under 65537, and octets whose first octet is zero are no modulus, the minimum number of octets [RFC 7518 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7518#section-2) holds a JWK's `n` to; the JWK reader hands a bare modulus of 256 or 512 octets and the managed certificate verifiers one of the certificate's own size after holding its exponent to 65537 themselves, while `did:key`, a COSE_Key and a certificate's subject public key hand the DER `RSAPublicKey`; every reading is held to `RsaUtilities.IsValidPublicKey` before it is used): an RSA signature that is not the modulus length or whose value is 0, 1, n - 1 or more ([RFC 8017](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8017#section-8.1.2) Sections 8.1.2 and 8.2.2, step 1, and Section 5.2.2, which admits 0 to n - 1; BouncyCastle's RSA engine refuses 0, 1 and n - 1 with `DataLengthException`, RSAVP1 maps each of them to itself and none is an encoded message of either scheme, so the member refuses them before the engine runs), for RSASSA-PSS under a modulus whose bit length is 1 modulo 8 a signature whose recovered representative needs more than emLen octets ([RFC 8017 Section 8.1.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8017#section-8.1.2), step 2.c; checked before the signer runs), an exact DER `RSAPublicKey` ([RFC 8017 Appendix A.1.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8017#appendix-A.1.1)) whose modulus or exponent is not positive, a bare modulus whose first octet is zero, a modulus and exponent that are no valid RSA public key ([RFC 8017 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8017#section-3.1): held to `RsaUtilities.IsValidPublicKey` before anything is constructed, an exponent below 3, not below the modulus or even and a modulus that is even or below 1024 bits, and to BouncyCastle's construction, a modulus with a small prime factor) and a modulus the PSS signer refuses ([RFC 8017 Section 9.1.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8017#section-9.1.2), step 3), an elliptic-curve public key other than 0x02 or 0x03 at the compressed length or 0x04 at the uncompressed length (the hybrid encodings 0x06 and 0x07 and the infinity encoding included) and a point the curve's decoder refuses ([RFC 8725 Section 3.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8725#section-3.4)), an ECDSA signature that is not exactly its two components ([RFC 7518 Section 3.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7518#section-3.4): "The JWS Signature value MUST be a 64-octet sequence.  If it is not a 64-octet sequence, the validation has failed."), an Ed25519 public key that is not 32 octets or 32 octets BouncyCastle refuses to decode, and an ML-DSA public key BouncyCastle refuses for the parameter set; BouncyCastle's own `ArgumentException` and `FormatException` are caught only around the decoding, the parameter construction and the signer's initialization, never around the verification arithmetic. The Microsoft ECDSA verifier answers `false`, raising nothing, for public key octets that are no point of the curve its public member names (a length or first octet that is neither SEC1 encoding of that curve, the field length stated by the member; a coordinate not below the field prime or a point not on the curve, `EllipticCurveUtilities.CheckPointOnCurve`; both checked before the platform is asked), for a point the platform refuses to import with `CryptographicException` and for a signature the platform does not verify; `PlatformNotSupportedException` leaves it only when the platform does not serve the curve. The Microsoft RSA verifier reads exactly what the BouncyCastle one reads, by the same rule, and answers `false`, raising nothing, for an exact DER `RSAPublicKey` with a negative INTEGER, for a bare modulus whose first octet is zero, for a modulus and exponent `RsaUtilities.IsValidPublicKey` refuses, for the platform's `CryptographicException` (on the Windows CNG backend a public exponent of more than 8 octets is among its causes, an import refusal BouncyCastle does not share) and for a signature the platform does not verify. `RsaUtilities.IsValidPublicKey` does not refuse a modulus that is prime, under which anyone computes a private exponent and signs, nor one with a small prime factor (for a modulus that is one small prime times one large prime, anyone signs as well): BouncyCastle's construction refuses both and the platform neither; the refusal of both for both providers is the next commit's. Libsodium's Ed25519 raises `ArgumentException` for a public key that is not 32 octets or a signature that is not 64, which a `Jws` body answers as not verified. The header gate does not require a string-valued `alg`; the binding of the algorithm to the public key is a later commit's. The class remarks of `Jws` and `CryptoFunctionRegistry` state the rule. The JWK conversion of an RSA public key the library holds reads it by the same rule (`RsaPublicKeyEncoding.DecodeKeyMaterial`): a DER key keeps the exponent it carries, which no reading replaces with 65537, and a bare modulus converts under 65537; a format that carries a DER key (a multicodec body, EF.DG15, a certificate) is read as exactly that DER (`RsaPublicKeyEncoding.Decode`). Proven by `RsaPublicKeyConversionTests.ACarriedExponentIsKeptAndABareModulusMeans65537` (one modulus with exponents three and 65537, on both providers: the DER key of exponent three verifies only its own signature and the bare modulus only the 65537 one) and `RsaPublicKeyConversionTests.ABareModulusConvertsToAJwkUnder65537`.
  - **At the Credential Endpoint.** A key proof longer than the bound, or one whose header `jwk` names a pair the registry does not serve (the JWK converter reads a P-256 public key carrying `"use":"enc"` as a key agreement public key), is a proof that does not verify: 400 `invalid_proof` with `A jwt key proof failed validation: SignatureFailed (Appendix F.4).`, never a server fault. So is a correctly signed key proof whose signature segment carries `==` after it: the segment is not the encoding, and the answer is 400 `invalid_proof`.
  - Proven by `CryptoFunctionRegistryTests.UnservedPairIsNoFunctionNotAFault` ((X25519, key agreement) and (P-256, key agreement): `TryResolveVerification` answers `false` with no function, `ResolveVerification` throws `ArgumentException` naming the pair), `CryptoFunctionRegistryTests.UnservedSigningPairIsAnArgumentFaultNamingThePair` ((X25519, key agreement): `ResolveSigning` throws `ArgumentException` naming the pair), `DidCommSignedMessageTests.AuthenticationMethodCarryingKeyAgreementPublicKeyFailsSignerResolution` and `DidCommFromPriorTests.PriorAuthenticationMethodCarryingKeyAgreementPublicKeyRejected` (a signer's, and a prior DID's, authentication method carrying an X25519 key agreement public key:
