- **An assertion issued beyond the skew is not yet valid; one reader serves the RFC 7523 claim rules, `PrivateKeyJwtClientAuthentication`, `FederationClientAuthentication`, `JwsAccessTokenValidator` and `Oidc10IdTokenValidator`'s `auth_time`.** [RFC 7523 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7523#section-3): "In order to issue an access token response as described in OAuth 2.0 [RFC6749] or to rely on a JWT for client authentication, the authorization server MUST validate the JWT according to the criteria below. Application of additional restrictions and policy are at the discretion of the authorization server." [RFC 7519 Section 4.1.6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7519#section-4.1.6): "The "iat" (issued at) claim identifies the time at which the JWT was issued. This claim can be used to determine the age of the JWT. Its value MUST be a number containing a NumericDate value. Use of this claim is OPTIONAL."
  - **The refusal.** An `iat` later than the current instant plus the skew is refused, this library's policy under RFC 7523 Section 3's second sentence: in the shared time rules (`Rfc7523AssertionValidation.ValidateTemporalClaims`) as `NotYetValid` with "The assertion iat is in the future beyond the skew tolerance.", so `Rfc7523AssertionValidation.Validate` answers `Rfc7523AssertionValidationFailureReason.NotYetValid` and `IdJagAssertionValidation` answers `IdJagValidationFailureReason.NotYetValid` through its map of the shared outcome; and in `FederationClientAuthentication.Validate` with "The client assertion iat is in the future beyond the skew tolerance.", the refusal and the words of `PrivateKeyJwtClientAuthentication`. An `iat` equal to the current instant plus the skew is accepted. The check follows the `exp`-at-or-before-`iat` check and compares the two instants through their difference, as the `nbf` check does. At the token endpoint the application's assertion delegate answers such an assertion with no grant, so the JWT bearer grant and the ID-JAG redeem answer 400 `{"error":"invalid_grant","error_description":"The JWT assertion is not valid."}` ([RFC 7523 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7523#section-3.1): "The value of the "error" parameter MUST be the "invalid_grant" error code."), and a federation endpoint whose gate validates the client assertion answers `invalid_client`.
  - **No maximum age.** No rule refuses an `iat` far in the past ([RFC 7523 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7523#section-3) item 6: "Note that the authorization server may reject JWTs with an "iat" claim value that is unreasonably far in the past."): the age of an assertion is the application's delegate's to judge, and no parameter carries one.
  - **One reader.** `Rfc7523AssertionValidation.ReadNumericDate` (internal) reads the NumericDates of five users: the RFC 7523 claim rules, `PrivateKeyJwtClientAuthentication`, `FederationClientAuthentication`, the `exp`, `iat` and `nbf` of `JwsAccessTokenValidator` and the `auth_time` of `Oidc10IdTokenValidator`. A number a decoder hands as a `long`, `int`, `short`, `byte`, `ulong`, `uint`, `double` or `decimal` is read with its fraction kept; a `float` and every other shape are malformed (a `float` is a number whose 24 mantissa bits hold only one epoch second in 128 of this era exactly: between 2^30 and 2^31 seconds its spacing is 128 seconds, so an instant read from one is off by up to 64 seconds; refusing the shape as a whole is this library's rule, and the RFC 7523 claim rules and the ID-JAG describe a `float` claim as a NumericDate this server cannot represent), and so are a JSON string and a number no instant can hold. The JAR, SIOP, JARM, OID4VCI proof and `BearerTokenValidation` validators read their NumericDates with their own readers (`src/Verifiable.OAuth/Jar/JwtClaimReaders.cs`, `src/Verifiable.OAuth/Siop/SelfIssuedIdTokenValidation.cs`, `src/Verifiable.OAuth/Jarm/JarmResponseValidation.cs`, `src/Verifiable.OAuth/Oid4Vci/CredentialProofValidator.cs`, `src/Verifiable.OAuth/Server/BearerTokenValidation.cs`).
  - **The ID-JAG's REQUIRED claims on the wire.** [draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04 Section 3.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.1): "iss: REQUIRED - The issuer identifier of the IdP Authorization Server as defined in [RFC8414]." and "exp: REQUIRED - as defined in Section 4.1.4 of [RFC7519]."; a grant without either is refused at the redeem as `MissingIssuer` or `MissingExpiration`, as a grant without `jti` or `iat` is.
  - Proven by `Rfc7523AssertionValidationTests.TheEdgeOfTheSkewDecidesTheIssuedAtToTheTick` (an `iat` one skew after the clock accepted and surfaced; a tick later `NotYetValid` with the description), `IdJagAssertionValidationTests.TheEdgeOfTheSkewDecidesTheIssuedAtToTheTick` (the same through the ID-JAG map), `JwtBearerGrantTests.AnAssertionIssuedBeyondTheSkewIsRefusedAsNotValidAsync` (over the wire, `iat` the clock plus the skew plus one second: 400 with the body above, the recorded `NotYetValid` and description), `JwtBearerGrantTests.AnAssertionIssuedAtTheEdgeOfTheSkewIsValidAsync` (over the wire, the edge: 200 and an access token), `IdJagGrantTests.AnIdJagIssuedBeyondTheSkewIsRefusedAtTheRedeemAsync` (over the wire, `iat` the clock plus 61 seconds: 400, recorded `NotYetValid`, the association not called), `IdJagGrantTests.AnIdJagIssuedAtTheEdgeOfTheSkewIsRedeemedAsync` (over the wire, plus 60 seconds: 200), `FederationFetchEndpointTests.FetchEndpointRefusesAClientAssertionIssuedBeyondTheSkew` (over the wire, the client assertion in a POST form body, plus 61 seconds: 400 `{"error":"invalid_client","error_description":"The client assertion iat is in the future beyond the skew tolerance."}`, the resolver not consulted), `FederationFetchEndpointTests.FetchEndpointServesAClientAssertionIssuedAtTheEdgeOfTheSkew` (over the wire, POST, plus 60 seconds: 200 and the Subordinate Statement verifying under the anchor's federation public key), `IdJagGrantTests.AnIdJagWithoutIssOrExpIsRefusedAtTheRedeemAsync` (over the wire: `MissingIssuer`, `MissingExpiration`), `Rfc7523AssertionValidationTests.AFractionalNumericDateKeepsItsFraction` (an `iat` and an `exp` a fraction apart within the clock's own second), `JwsAccessTokenValidatorTests.ValidatorRefusesATimeClaimThatIsNoRepresentableNumericDateAsMalformed` (an `exp`, `iat` or `nbf` that is a string of digits, an `iat` of 1e15: `Malformed`), `JwsAccessTokenValidatorTests.ValidatorReadsTheTimeClaimsInEachNumberShapeTheReaderNames` (the `exp`, `nbf` and `iat` handed in each of the eight shapes: valid, the instants to the tick), `JwsAccessTokenValidatorTests.ValidatorRefusesATimeClaimHandedAsAFloatAsMalformed` (an `exp`, `iat` or `nbf` handed as a `float`: `Malformed`), `Rfc7523AssertionValidationTests.ATimeClaimAsAFloatIsInconsistent` (an `exp`, `iat` or `nbf` as a `float`: `InconsistentTemporalClaims` with "The assertion &lt;claim&gt; claim is a NumericDate this server cannot represent."), `IdJagAssertionValidationTests.AnIssuedAtAsAFloatIsANumericDateThisServerCannotRepresent` (an `iat` handed as a `float`: `InconsistentTemporalClaims` with the same description through the ID-JAG map), `PrivateKeyJwtClientAuthenticationTests.AnIssuedAtAsAFloatIsRejectedAsMalformed` (an `iat` handed as a `float`: rejected, "The client assertion iat claim is malformed."), `FederationClientAuthenticationTests.AnIssuedAtAsAFloatIsRejectedAsMalformed` (an `iat` handed as a `float`: rejected, "The client authentication JWT iat claim is malformed."), `Oidc10IdTokenNonceAudienceScenarioTests.IdTokenAuthTimeIsReadInEachNumberShapeTheReaderNames` (over the wire, the `auth_time` handed in each of the eight shapes: surfaced to the tick), `Oidc10IdTokenNonceAudienceScenarioTests.IdTokenAuthTimeHandedAsAFloatIsRejected` (over the wire, an `auth_time` handed as a `float`: `Malformed`), and `Oidc10IdTokenNonceAudienceScenarioTests.IdTokenWithUnrepresentableAuthTimeIsRejected` (over the wire, an `auth_time` no instant can hold: `Malformed`).

- **A token's lifetime is read from one resolution, and the first configured value of the kind is the lifetime, within the profile's ceilings.** [RFC 6749 Section 5.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-5.1): "expires_in RECOMMENDED. The lifetime in seconds of the access token." [RFC 6749 Section 1.5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-1.5): "Refresh tokens are credentials used to obtain access tokens." [RFC 8693 Section 2.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8693#section-2.2.1): "expires_in RECOMMENDED. The validity lifetime, in seconds, of the token issued by the authorization server." [draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04 Section 4.3.4](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-4.3.4): "expires_in:  RECOMMENDED - The lifetime in seconds of the authorization grant."
  - **The resolution.** `TokenLifetimes.TryResolve(ClientRecord, TokenType, ExchangeContext, out TimeSpan)` reads the lifetime of an access token, an ID Token, a refresh token or an ID-JAG: the registration's override (`ClientRecord.TokenLifetimes` under the kind's `WellKnownTokenTypes` name), else the request's policy value of that kind (set by the built-in profiles from the `TimingPolicy`, or by an application's policy resolver; the ID-JAG has none), else the server's `TimingPolicy`, else `TimingPolicy.Default` when no server is on the context. Any other `TokenType` answers `false` with no lifetime. So does a resolved lifetime whose whole seconds are under one or above `int.MaxValue`, the library's own rule (the library writes `iat`, `exp` and a 32-bit `expires_in` in whole seconds; a lifetime under one second would issue a token born expired), and a value present under the kind's policy name that is not a `TimeSpan` (presence and type are two checks: a mistyped value states no lifetime, never an absent value the server's longer default stands in for). An issuable lifetime is issued as its whole seconds, the fraction dropped, so the `expires_in` a response states equals `exp` less `iat`. The one rule is `TokenLifetimes.IsIssuable`. A registration's lifetime map is the application's own record (the registration endpoint writes an empty map; no library path reads lifetimes from metadata), and its `init` accessor refuses a lifetime outside the same bound with `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`, naming the token type, and holds a frozen snapshot of the map under the map's own comparer (the one a `Dictionary`, a `FrozenDictionary` or an `ImmutableDictionary` exposes; ordinal comparison, as the `WellKnownTokenTypes` names are compared, for a map of any other kind), so a lookup answers as the application's own map answered it, a map holding two keys equal under that comparer is refused with `ArgumentException`, a change the application makes afterwards to the map it passed in never reaches the record and a registration never holds such a lifetime. A `false` answer means the resolved lifetime is not issuable, whoever supplied it. Every token-endpoint path resolves the access-token and ID Token lifetimes before it runs the producer that issues with them (a producer is known by its `BuildAsync` delegate, so a copy of a built-in producer under another name is resolved as the built-in is), the Pre-Authorized Code grant before its code-validation seam may consume the single-use code (its OpenID4VCI Section 13.10 guard runs there too, so a plain bearer request it refuses leaves the code unspent for the Wallet's DPoP-bound retry, and runs again, failing closed, right before issuance on the lifetime the application's seams leave on the request), and every site that receives `false` answers 500 `{"error":"server_error","error_description":"The server is not ready to serve this request."}` itself, with nothing issued and the presented code or refresh token left unspent; the producers keep their `InvalidOperationException` for a direct caller. The authorization code lifetime and the pushed `request_uri` lifetime (the request's policy values) are read through the same rule at the authorization endpoint's three code sites, the JARM response and the two pushed authorization request sites, and a value outside it is answered the same 500 before anything is stored; the pushed authorization response's `expires_in` is the stored lifetime itself.
  - **The readers.** The access-token producer and the ID Token producer (`exp`), the code grant's refresh token, the token exchange's refresh token (its stored expiry and the `expires_in` the exchange answers, one value), the refresh grant's rotated successor, the ID-JAG mint (`exp` and `expires_in`, one value) and the OpenID4VCI Section 13.10 guard for a bearer Credential access token all read it. No inline order remains.
  - **`TimingPolicy`.** Every token lifetime default lives there: `AccessTokenLifetime` and `IdTokenLifetime` (an hour), `RefreshTokenLifetime` (30 days) and `IdJagLifetime` (five minutes). The FAPI 2.0 profile, and the profiles built on it, derive the request's access, ID and refresh token lifetimes, the authorization code lifetime (`AuthorizationCodeLifetime`), the pushed `request_uri` lifetime (`AuthCodeParLifetime`), the request object lifetime ceiling (`AuthCodeRequestObjectLifetime`) and the clock skew from the server's `TimingPolicy`. The FAPI 2.0 profile holds three of them to its ceilings: [FAPI 2.0 Security Profile Section 5.3.2.1](https://openid.net/specs/fapi-security-profile-2_0-final.html#section-5.3.2.1) item 11: "shall issue authorization codes with a maximum lifetime of 60 seconds;" (the code lifetime is the smaller of `AuthorizationCodeLifetime` and 60 seconds); [Section 5.3.2.2](https://openid.net/specs/fapi-security-profile-2_0-final.html#section-5.3.2.2) item 12: "shall issue pushed authorization requests request_uri with expires_in values of less than 600 seconds;" (the `request_uri` lifetime is the smaller of `AuthCodeParLifetime` and 599 seconds, the largest whole-second `expires_in` below 600); Section 5.3.2.1 item 13: "to accommodate clock offsets, shall accept JWTs with an iat or nbf timestamp between 0 and 10 seconds in the future but shall reject JWTs with an iat or nbf timestamp greater than 60 seconds in the future. See Note 3 for further details and rationale; and" (the clock skew is `ClockSkewTolerance` raised to 10 seconds and lowered to 60 seconds). The code and `request_uri` lifetimes it derives are at least one second. The RFC 6749 with PKCE profile re-derives those three from the `TimingPolicy` without the ceilings ([RFC 6749 Section 4.1.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-4.1.2) recommends a 10-minute code; it requires none). The HAIP 1.0 profile ([HAIP 1.0 Section 4](https://openid.net/specs/openid4vc-high-assurance-interoperability-profile-1_0-final.html#section-4): "MUST comply with the provisions of [FAPI2_Security_Profile] that are applicable to this specification.") inherits the FAPI 2.0 ceilings and holds the `request_uri` and request object lifetimes to the library's own 60-second ceiling: each is the smaller of the `TimingPolicy` value and 60 seconds, so a shorter deployment value is kept. The profiles' ceilings bound the values the profile derives; an application's policy resolver that sets a lifetime after the profile ran owns the value it set, and the library does not hold that value to the profile's ceilings. Every lifetime the `TimingPolicy` holds refuses, at its `init` accessor (`ArgumentOutOfRangeException`), a value whose whole seconds are under one or above `int.MaxValue`, and holds an issuable value as its whole seconds, so every reader of the record issues whole seconds; `ClockSkewTolerance` and `KbJwtIatMaxAge` refuse a negative value (zero stays allowed), and `CredentialAccessTokenSenderConstraintThreshold` a value that is not positive (a threshold above the access-token lifetime disables the Section 13.10 refusal for that token). The policy values' getters (the token lifetimes, the code and `request_uri` lifetimes, the request object lifetime ceiling and the clock skew) fall back to the request server's `TimingPolicy`, else `TimingPolicy.Default`, so a request no built-in profile ran on is held to the deployment's own values; a getter answers the configured duration, and for a present value of another type, or one that is not issuable, it reads it as it is while `TryResolve` refuses it. The HAIP 1.0 request builders (`HaipProfile.BuildJarAsync` and `BuildUnsignedJarAsync`) refuse a request object lifetime outside the one rule with `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` and stamp its whole seconds.
  - **For a deployment.** A registration's token lifetime override takes effect for every kind; a lifetime set through the policy resolver or the `TimingPolicy` takes effect, and so do the `TimingPolicy`'s code, `request_uri` and request object lifetimes under the FAPI 2.0, HAIP 1.0 and RFC 6749 with PKCE profiles, held to the FAPI 2.0 ceilings (and HAIP's 60-second ones) under those profiles. A value the deployment's own policy resolver sets after the profile ran is issued as it is set. The defaults are an hour, an hour, 30 days and five minutes; a registration with no profile (FAPI 2.0) is issued codes of 60 seconds. A `ClientRecord.TokenLifetimes` override outside one to `int.MaxValue` whole seconds throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` when the record is constructed, so a map a host persisted outside [1 s, int.MaxValue s] throws when the record is rebuilt; the library never writes such a map. The library's client issues its own request objects (`AuthCodeStartJarParOptions.JarLifetime` and `AuthCodeStartJarAuthorizeOptions.JarLifetime`, thirty seconds by default), client assertions (`ClientAssertionLifetime` of `ClientAssertionOptions`, `IdJagMintOptions` and `IdJagRedeemOptions`, a minute by default, and `ClientTokenEndpointAuthentication.AttachClientAssertionAsync`), the inline-parameter request object's `exp` (`AuthorizationRequestObjectFormFields.Parse`), the wallet's Self-Issued ID Token (`SelfIssuedIdTokenIssuance`, both Subject Syntax Types) and the pending authorization of the plain start (`AuthCodeClient.StartAsync`'s `requestLifetime`, stored and stated as one value) by the same rule: a value outside it is the caller's own argument and throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`, and an issuable one is issued as its whole seconds; the defaults are the library's own values. The Federation Resolve Response's `exp` is the minimum OpenID Federation 1.0 Section 8.3.2 names, never extended; a resolution whose stated expiry leaves under one whole second after the response's issuance is answered 400 `invalid_trust_chain` with nothing signed.
  - **No flow ceiling.** No `TimingPolicy` member holds a flow ceiling, since no code of the library would read one; `WalletFlowAutomaton.Create`'s `maxFlowDuration` takes the application's own.
  - Proven by `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.ARefreshTokenIssuedAtTheCodeRedemptionExpiresAtTheRegistrationsLifetimeAsync` (a registration's one-hour refresh lifetime under the 30-day policy: refused `invalid_grant` one hour and a minute after the code redemption), `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.ARotatedRefreshTokenExpiresAtTheRegistrationsLifetimeAsync` (the rotated successor expires one hour after its rotation), `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.ARefreshTokenExpiresAtTheServersTimingPolicyLifetimeAsync` (a `TimingPolicy` refresh lifetime of 24 hours), policy resolver or the `TimingPolicy`, for the access token and the ID Token: `exp` 300 seconds after `iat`, `expires_in` 300), - **Verify every implemented DPoP clause by a test (`32623877`).** Twenty-four clauses of [RFC 9449](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449) and five members of [RFC 9728](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728) that the library implements are each held by a test of their own, the clause quoted here and its test named. The identifiers in parentheses (`DPOP-nnn` for RFC 9449 in document order, `PRM-nnn` for RFC 9728) are those of the clause list the project keeps. "Over the listener" is a request over a Kestrel listener, "raw wire" a request whose field bytes the test writes itself, "in process" a call with no transport.
  - **Transport.** [RFC 9449 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-2) (DPOP-002): "DPoP is not, however, a substitute for a secure transport and MUST always be used in conjunction with HTTPS." The client half is that every client dial passes `OutboundFetchPolicy`, whose default admits `https` alone; the server half is the host's transport and no library clause. Proven in process (no transport is reached) by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientNeverSendsAProofToATokenEndpointOutsideHttpsAsync` and `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.WalletNeverSendsAProofToAResourceOutsideHttpsAsync`.
  - **A proof for each request.** [RFC 9449 Section 4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-4) (DPOP-007): "Each HTTP request requires a unique DPoP proof." The clients mint a proof with a fresh `jti` for every attempt. Proven over the listener by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientSendsADistinctProofInEveryTokenRequestAsync`.
  - **The header field.** [RFC 9449 Section 4.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-4.1):
    - (DPOP-009) "Note that per [RFC9110], header field names are case insensitive; thus, DPoP, DPOP, dpop, etc., are all valid and equivalent header field names." The field name is looked up case-insensitively. Proven on the raw wire by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoReadsTheProofUnderAnySpellingOfTheDpopFieldNameAsync` and `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointReadsTheProofUnderAnySpellingOfTheDpopFieldNameAsync` (`dpop` and `DPOP`), and in process, for outgoing and response headers, by `OutgoingHeadersDpopExtensionsTests.ValuesLookupIsCaseInsensitive` and `AuthorizationServerResponseHeaderTests.ServerHttpResponseHeadersLookupIsCaseInsensitive`.
    - (DPOP-010) "However, case is significant in the header field value." The value is split and decoded as received, with no case folding. Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoReadsTheDpopFieldValueWithItsCaseSignificantAsync`; the signature check holds the refusal half.
    - (DPOP-011) "The DPoP HTTP header field value uses the token68 syntax defined in Section 11.2 of [RFC9110] and is repeated below in Figure 3 for ease of reference." The client emits a compact JWS, whose base64url segments and dots lie inside token68; the receiver checks no token68 grammar of its own, and a value outside base64url fails the decode as malformed. Proven over the listener by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientSendsItsProofInTheToken68SyntaxAsync` (the value read where the endpoint received it) and `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesAProofValueOutsideTheToken68SyntaxAsync`.
  - **The `jti`.** [RFC 9449 Section 4.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-4.2) (DPOP-016): "The value MUST be assigned such that there is a negligible probability that the same value will be assigned to any other DPoP proof used in the same context during the time window of validity." The clients draw it from the host's `GenerateIdentifierDelegate`; the library holds no generator of its own for it, and the default generator answers a version 7 GUID. Proven over the listener by `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.WalletAssignsEveryProofItsOwnGeneratedJtiAsync`; the token client draws from the same delegate and is held by the row of Section 4 above.
  - **The `DPoP` challenge.** [RFC 9449 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.1):
    - (DPOP-068) "The authentication parameter realm MAY be included to indicate the scope of protection in the manner described in [RFC9110], Section 11.5." Every `DPoP` challenge of the library's resources carries the `realm`, the header-safe form of the issuer the request is served under, and none where no issuer resolves or the issuer cannot be written into a header (the entry for `c59d4e1d` below). Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoDpopChallengeCarriesTheRealmOfItsProtectionSpaceAsync` and `UserInfoDpopChallengeOmitsTheRealmWhereNoIssuerResolvesAsync`.
    - (DPOP-069) "A scope authentication parameter MAY be included as defined in [RFC6750], Section 3." UserInfo names `openid` in every `DPoP` challenge it sends, the 403 `insufficient_scope` included; the OID4VCI resources, which require no scope, name none (the entry for `c59d4e1d` below). Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoInsufficientScopeDpopChallengeNamesTheRequiredScopeAsync` and `UserInfoDpopChallengeNamesTheRequiredScopeInEveryRefusalAsync`.
    - (DPOP-072) "An error_description parameter ([RFC6750], Section 3) MAY be included along with the error parameter to provide developers a human-readable explanation that is not meant to be displayed to end-users." The challenge carries no `error_description`; the description rides the body. Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoDpopChallengeCarriesNoErrorDescriptionParameterAsync`.
    - (DPOP-074) "Additional authentication parameters MAY be used, and unknown parameters MUST be ignored by recipients." The resource writes no additional parameter in its `DPoP` challenge. The clients recognise a nonce challenge by the challenge's `error` parameter, read through the `DPoP` challenge reader (`DpopNonceRetry.IsWwwAuthenticateNonceChallenge`, `DpopChallenge.TryRead`), or by the body's `error` member (`DpopNonceRetry.IsErrorBodyNonceChallenge`); unknown parameters are ignored, and the text `use_dpop_nonce` inside an unknown parameter's value or an `error_description` is no nonce challenge. Proven over the listener, the 401 rewritten after it crossed the wire, by `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.WalletAnswersANonceChallengeCarryingAnUnknownParameterAsync`, `WalletDoesNotReadAnUnknownParameterAsANonceChallengeAsync`, `WalletDoesNotReadAnErrorDescriptionAsACredentialEndpointNonceChallengeAsync` and `WalletDoesNotReadAnErrorDescriptionAsATokenEndpointNonceChallengeAsync`, and by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientDoesNotReadAnErrorDescriptionAsANonceChallengeAsync`.
    - (DPOP-076) "Therefore, this authentication scheme MUST NOT be used with the Proxy-Authenticate or Proxy-Authorization header fields." The presentation neither writes nor reads a `Proxy-` field. Proven on the raw wire by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoReadsNoDpopCredentialFromProxyAuthorizationAsync` and over the listener by `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.WalletPresentsItsAccessTokenUnderAuthorizationNeverProxyAuthorizationAsync`.
  - **A fresh proof on retry.** [RFC 9449 Section 7.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.3) (DPOP-081): "It is RECOMMENDED that clients generate a unique DPoP proof, even when retrying idempotent requests in response to HTTP errors generally understood as transient." Every attempt mints a fresh proof. Proven over the listener by `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.WalletSendsAFreshProofWhenTheApplicationRetriesAfterATransientErrorAsync` (the credential POST retried after a 503; every proof the Credential Endpoint received carries a `jti` of its own). Every request the library's clients send under DPoP is a POST, so the clause's idempotent instance is not exercisable through them; the property is held by the POST row.
  - **The nonce.** [RFC 9449 Section 8](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-8):
    - (DPOP-086) "Nonce values MUST be unpredictable." A nonce holds 16 bytes from the host's `FillEntropyDelegate` and an HMAC tag. Proven over the listener by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointSuppliesADistinctNonceInEveryChallengeAsync` and `TokenEndpointNonceCarriesTheRandomBytesItsIssuerDrewAsync` (each nonce's random field equals the issuer's draw).
    - (DPOP-087) "This same error code is used when supplying a new nonce value when there was a nonce mismatch." Proven over the listener by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointAnswersANonceMismatchWithUseDpopNonceAndANewNonceAsync` (`nonce-character-changed`, `nonce-window-passed`); the resource's twin is held by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesAProofWhoseNonceIsNotTheOneItProvidedAsync`.
    - (DPOP-088) "Other HTTP headers and JSON fields MAY also be included in the error response, but there MUST NOT be more than one DPoP-Nonce header." `ServerHttpResponse.WithHeader` sets a field by name, so one value at most. Proven on the raw wire by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointNonceChallengeCarriesOneDpopNonceFieldAsync` and `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoNonceChallengeCarriesOneDpopNonceFieldAsync`.
    - (DPOP-090) "The nonce is opaque to the client." The client stores and echoes it as a string. Proven over the listener by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientEchoesTheSuppliedNonceExactlyAsItArrivedAsync` (a nonce holding `~`, `.`, `!`, `*` and `(`).
    - (DPOP-091) "If the nonce claim in the DPoP proof does not exactly match a nonce recently supplied by the authorization server to the client, the authorization server MUST reject the request." The presented text is held to exactly the issued text: the base64url alphabet alone, exactly the issued structure's octets, their one canonical spelling (`DefaultDpopNonceValidation.ValidateAsync`). The nonce is bound to the issuer and the tenant's HMAC key set, not to the client it was supplied to. Proven over the listener by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRejectsAProofWhoseNonceIsNotExactlyOneItSuppliedAsync` (a character added, the case changed), `TokenEndpointRejectsANonceThatIsNotTheTextItSuppliedAsync` (an octet added, a space, padding, a set pad bit) and `TokenEndpointRejectsAnotherSpellingOfTheSuppliedNonceItsDecoderReadsAsync` (a permissive [RFC 4648](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4648#section-3.3) decoder), and in process by `DefaultDpopNonceIssuanceAndValidationTests.ValidateAsyncRejectsTamperedNonce`.
    - (DPOP-092) "The rejection response MAY include a DPoP-Nonce HTTP header providing a new nonce value to use for subsequent requests." The token endpoint takes the option. Proven over the listener by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRejectionOfAMismatchedNonceSuppliesTheNonceOfTheNextRequestAsync`.
  - **The nonce in use.** [RFC 9449 Section 8.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-8.2):
    - (DPOP-095) "The client is expected to use the existing supplied nonce in DPoP proofs until the server supplies a new nonce value." Proven over the listener by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientUsesTheSuppliedNonceInItsNextTokenRequestAsync`, and for the cache alone in process by `InMemoryDpopNonceCacheTests.StoreThenLookupRoundTrips`.
    - (DPOP-097) "The DPoP-Nonce HTTP header field uses the nonce syntax defined in Section 8.1." The issued nonce is base64url. Proven on the raw wire by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointWritesTheNonceInTheNonceSyntaxAsync` (`1*NQCHAR`). The client stores and embeds a `DPoP-Nonce` only when it is the one such field and is `1*NQCHAR`, held by `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.WalletDoesNotRetryOnADpopNonceOutsideTheNonceSyntaxAsync` and `WalletDoesNotRetryOnAChallengeCarryingTwoDpopNonceFieldsAsync`.
    - (DPOP-098, [RFC 9449 Section 8.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-8.2)) "The client MUST use the new nonce value supplied for the next token request and for all subsequent token requests until the authorization server supplies a new nonce." The clients keep the `DPoP-Nonce` of every answer an endpoint gives, a 200, a refusal and a nonce challenge alike, when it is the one such field and is `1*NQCHAR`, and the next proof sent to that endpoint carries it. Proven over the listener, the server emitting the field line and the client's own HTTP transport parsing it, by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientUsesANonceSuppliedOnASuccessfulTokenResponseAsync` (the retry's 200; the first attempt's 200), `ClientUsesANonceSuppliedOnATokenErrorResponseAsync` (a 400 `invalid_scope`), `ClientStoresNoNonceFromAnAnswerOutsideTheOneFieldNonceSyntaxAsync` (two fields; a value outside the syntax) and `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.WalletUsesANonceSuppliedOnASuccessfulCredentialResponseAsync` (the Credential Endpoint's 200).
    - (DPOP-099) "Responses that include the DPoP-Nonce HTTP header should be uncacheable (e.g., using Cache-Control: no-store in response to a GET request) to prevent the response from being used to serve a subsequent request and a stale nonce value from being used as a result." `DPoP-Nonce` rides only 400 and 401 answers, which are not heuristically cacheable ([RFC 9111 Section 4.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9111#section-4.2.2)); no `Cache-Control: no-store` is added to them. Proven on the raw wire by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointNonceChallengeIsNotStorableByACacheAsync` and `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoNonceChallengeToAGetIsNotStorableByACacheAsync`.
    - (DPOP-102, [RFC 9449 Section 9](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-9)) "Likewise, should a client use multiple authorization servers and/or resource servers, a nonce issued by any of them should be used only at the issuing server." The clients keep one nonce per endpoint (scheme, authority and path), so the token endpoint and each resource on one origin hold their own; the library's resources (UserInfo and the OID4VCI Credential, Deferred Credential and Notification Endpoints) issue and accept nonces audienced at the target URI they serve, the one reconstructed from the host's listener authority or the issuer's and the request path, while the token endpoint audiences its nonces at the issuer, so a nonce presented at an endpoint other than the one that issued it is answered with that endpoint's own nonce challenge. Proven over the listener by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientKeepsTheNonceOfEachTokenEndpointOnOneOriginApartAsync`, `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.WalletKeepsTheTokenEndpointNonceApartFromTheResourceNoncesOnOneOriginAsync`, `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesTheTokenEndpointNonceAsync`, `TokenEndpointRefusesTheNonceUserInfoProvidedAsync`, `Oid4VciDpopCredentialEndpointTests.CredentialEndpointRefusesTheTokenEndpointNonceAsync` and `NotificationEndpointRefusesTheCredentialEndpointNonceAsync`.
  - **The authorization request parameter, the client's option.** [RFC 9449 Section 10](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-10) (DPOP-106): "Use of the dpop_jkt authorization request parameter is OPTIONAL." The library's AuthCode client takes the option when DPoP is wired (the entry for `9214b8eb` below); it sends no proof at the pushed authorization request. The server half of supporting it is the entry for `d1e9cead` below.
  - **The same nonce again.** [RFC 9449 Section 11.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-11.1) (DPOP-118): "Unlike cryptographic nonces, it is acceptable for clients to use the same nonce multiple times and for the server to accept the same nonce multiple times." The HMAC nonce is accepted any number of times within its window; no single-use record exists. Proven over the listener by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointAcceptsTheNonceItSuppliedInSeveralProofsAsync` and `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoAcceptsTheNonceItProvidedInSeveralProofsAsync`.
  - **The algorithm `none`.** [RFC 9449 Section 11.6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-11.6) (DPOP-123): "In particular, the algorithm none MUST NOT be allowed." No family the servers admit contains `none`. `DpopKey` accepts only the identifiers `DpopProofValidator.IsDpopProofAlg` accepts, so no proof is built under `none` or a MAC algorithm, and `JwtHeader.ForDpop` refuses an `alg` outside the JOSE asymmetric signature identifiers. Proven over the listener by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRefusesAProofWhoseAlgIsNoneAsync` and `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesAProofWhoseAlgIsNoneAsync`, and, where the host lists the algorithm, by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesAProofUnderNoAsymmetricSignatureAlgorithmTheHostListsAsync` and `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRefusesAProofUnderNoAsymmetricSignatureAlgorithmTheHostListsAsync` (a proof under `HS256`; under `none` over a signature; under `none` with an empty signature part, refused by the structural parse of a signed proof), and by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoChallengeSignalsNoAlgorithmWhereTheHostListsNoneAloneAsync` and `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.DiscoveryPublishesOnlyTheAsymmetricDpopAlgorithmsTheHostListsAsync` (the challenge and the discovery document signal no `none`), and in process by `DpopProofConstructionTests.ClientRefusesToBuildAProofUnderTheAlgorithmNoneAsync`, `DpopProofConstructionTests.ClientRefusesToBuildAProofUnderAnIdentifierOtherThanAnAsymmetricSignatureAlgorithmAsync` and `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientDeclaringTheAlgorithmNoneNeverSendsAProofAsync` (the refusal precedes any request).
  - **The protected resource metadata members that bear on DPoP.** [RFC 9728 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-2), [Section 3.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-3.2) and [Section 5.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-5.1). The library contributes no document of its own for UserInfo or the OpenID for Verifiable Credential Issuance resources; the host contributes the document.
    - (PRM-003) "If this entry is omitted, no default bearer methods supported are implied, nor does its absence indicate that they are not supported." The consumer reads an omitted member as absent and substitutes no default. Proven over the listener (the document served, fetched and parsed) by `ProtectedResourceMetadataTests.ConsumerReadsAnOmittedBearerMethodsSupportedAsNoListAtAllAsync`.
    - (PRM-004) "dpop_signing_alg_values_supported OPTIONAL. JSON array containing a list of the JWS alg values (from the "JSON Web Signature and Encryption Algorithms" registry [IANA.JOSE]) supported by the resource server for validating Demonstrating Proof of Possession (DPoP) proof JWTs [RFC9449]." The member is emitted from the host's contribution and parsed by `ProtectedResourceMetadataJsonParsing`. Proven over the listener by `ProtectedResourceMetadataTests.DocumentPublishesTheDpopSigningAlgorithmsTheResourceSupportsAsync`.
    - (PRM-006) "If omitted, the default value is false." `dpop_bound_access_tokens_required` is omitted when the contribution holds `null`; the consumer reads no stated value and substitutes none. Proven over the listener by `ProtectedResourceMetadataTests.ConsumerReadsAnOmittedDpopBoundAccessTokensRequiredAsNoStatedValueAsync`.
    - (PRM-009) "Parameters with zero values MUST be omitted from the response." A list of no value is written as nothing, except `bearer_methods_supported`, whose empty array is written as `[]` (PRM-002, the entry for `7ae07edc` below). Proven over the listener by `ProtectedResourceMetadataTests.DocumentOmitsEveryParameterWithZeroValuesAsync`.
    - (PRM-010) "This parameter MAY also be used in WWW-Authenticate responses using authorization schemes other than "Bearer" [RFC6750], such as the DPoP scheme defined by [RFC9449]." The `Bearer` challenge can carry `resource_metadata`, and `DpopChallenge.BuildChallenge` takes it; no library resource writes it into a `DPoP` challenge until a document names the endpoint (the entry for `c59d4e1d` below).

- **Bind the authorization code to a DPoP key (`d1e9cead`).** An authorization server that verifies DPoP proofs binds the code it issues to one DPoP key, by the `dpop_jkt` authorization request parameter or by the DPoP proof of the pushed request, and refuses the redemption of that code unless a proof for the same DPoP key is presented. The binding rides `ParRequestReceivedState.Confirmation`, `ServerParValidated.Confirmation`, `ServerDirectAuthorizeCompleted.Confirmation` and `ServerCodeIssuedState.Confirmation`, carried by the three transitions that produce a code, and the redemption passes the code's thumbprint as the expected thumbprint. The public name of the parameter is `OAuthRequestParameterNames.DpopJkt`. A server that verifies no DPoP proof ignores `dpop_jkt` and the `DPoP` field at the pushed request.
  - **The parameter.** [RFC 9449 Section 10](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-10) (DPOP-104): "The value of the dpop_jkt authorization request parameter is the JWK Thumbprint [RFC7638] of the proof-of-possession public key using the SHA-256 hash function, which is the same value as used for the jkt confirmation method defined in Section 6.1." The server reads the value from the pushed request's body, the direct request's query and a request object's claim, and holds it to the spelling of a JWK SHA-256 Thumbprint ([RFC 9449 Section 6.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-6.1), [RFC 7638 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7638#section-3); `DpopJwkUtilities.IsJwkSha256ThumbprintSpelling`). A repeated or malformed value is `invalid_request`, and an empty value is read as omitted. The client half is the entry for `9214b8eb` below. Proven over the listener by `DpopEndToEndTests.PushedDpopJktBindsTheCodeToThatKeyAsync`, `DpopEndToEndTests.DirectAuthorizationDpopJktBindsTheCodeAsync`, `DpopEndToEndTests.DpopJktThatIsNoThumbprintAtThePushedRequestIsAnInvalidRequestAsync` (six rows: 42 characters; 44 characters; 43 characters holding `+`; 43 characters whose last character sets a pad bit; 43 characters holding `/`; 44 characters ending `=`) and `DpopEndToEndTests.RepeatedDpopJktAtThePushedRequestIsAnInvalidRequestAsync` (two thumbprints; two empty values).
  - **The redemption.** [RFC 9449 Section 10](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-10) (DPOP-105): "When a token request is received, the authorization server computes the JWK Thumbprint of the proof-of-possession public key in the DPoP proof and verifies that it matches the dpop_jkt parameter value in the authorization request. If they do not match, it MUST reject the request." A code with a bound DPoP key, presented without a proof or under another DPoP key, is refused `invalid_grant` with the code's own not-found description before the code is claimed, so the code stays unspent. Proven over the listener by `DpopEndToEndTests.CodeBoundByPushedDpopJktRefusesARedemptionWithoutAProofAsync`, `DpopEndToEndTests.CodeBoundByPushedDpopJktRefusesARedemptionUnderAnotherKeyAsync`, `DpopEndToEndTests.DirectAuthorizationDpopJktBindsTheCodeAsync`, `DpopEndToEndTests.FrontChannelDpopJktBesideRequestUriDoesNotRebindTheCodeAsync`, `JarParTests.RequestObjectDpopJktBindsTheCodeAndTheOuterOneIsNotReadAsync` and `JarParTests.RequestObjectByValueDpopJktBindsTheCodeAsync`.
  - **A request object.** [RFC 9101 Section 5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9101#section-5) carries `dpop_jkt` as a claim of the request object, and [RFC 9101 Section 6.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9101#section-6.3) states: "The authorization server MUST only use the parameters in the Request Object, even if the same parameter is provided in the query parameter." The request object's claim binds the code and an outer `dpop_jkt` beside a `request_uri` is not read. Proven over the listener by the `JarParTests` and `DpopEndToEndTests.FrontChannelDpopJktBesideRequestUriDoesNotRebindTheCodeAsync` rows above.
  - **Beside PKCE.** [RFC 9449 Section 10](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-10) (DPOP-107): "Note that the dpop_jkt authorization request parameter MAY also be used in combination with Proof Key for Code Exchange (PKCE) [RFC7636], which is recommended by [SECURITY-TOPICS] as a countermeasure to authorization code injection." A pushed `dpop_jkt` beside PKCE `S256` binds the code, which redeems under that DPoP key with its PKCE verifier. Proven over the listener by `DpopEndToEndTests.PushedDpopJktBindsTheCodeToThatKeyAsync`.
  - **The pushed request.** [RFC 9449 Section 10.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-10.1) (DPOP-108): "The dpop_jkt parameter can be used as described in Section 10 to bind the issued authorization code to a specific key. In this case, dpop_jkt MUST be included alongside other authorization request parameters in the POST body of the PAR request." `BuildPar` reads `dpop_jkt` from the pushed request's body and `BuildJarPar` reads the request object's claim. Proven over the listener by `DpopEndToEndTests.PushedDpopJktBindsTheCodeToThatKeyAsync`, `DpopEndToEndTests.EmptyDpopJktAtThePushedRequestIsReadAsOmittedAsync`, `DpopEndToEndTests.ServerVerifyingNoDpopProofIgnoresDpopJktAndTheDpopFieldAtThePushedRequestAsync` and `JarParTests.RequestObjectDpopJktBindsTheCodeAndTheOuterOneIsNotReadAsync`.
  - **The proof at the pushed request.** [RFC 9449 Section 10.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-10.1) (DPOP-109): "Alternatively, the DPoP header can be added to the PAR request. In this case, the authorization server MUST check the provided DPoP proof JWT as defined in Section 4.3." The proof of a pushed request undergoes the checks of Section 4.3 that the token endpoint runs (`AuthCodeEndpoints.CheckPushedProofAsync`, called by `BuildPar` and `BuildJarPar` over `DpopTokenEndpointValidation.ValidateAsync`): `typ`, the DPoP key's algorithm, `htm`, `htu`, the signature, a `jwk` without private material, `iat`, the single-use `jti` and the nonce. The entry for `b0604be7` below adds check 1 and the accepted algorithms. Proven over the listener by `DpopEndToEndTests.ProofAtThePushedRequestIsCheckedAsAtTheTokenEndpointAsync` (nine rows: the `htu` of the token endpoint; `htm` GET; a signature over other bytes; no nonce; the same proof presented twice; `typ` JWT; `alg` HS256 over an ES256 signature; a `jwk` carrying the private member `d`; an `iat` ten minutes old), `DpopEndToEndTests.ProofAtThePushedRequestBindsTheCodeToItsKeyAsync`, `DpopEndToEndTests.PushedRequestProofPresentedAtTheTokenEndpointIsRefusedForItsHtuAsync` and `DpopEndToEndTests.UsedTokenEndpointProofIsRefusedAgainstAnUnspentBoundCodeAsync`.
  - **The proof binds the code.** [RFC 9449 Section 10.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-10.1) (DPOP-110): "It MUST further behave as if the contained public key's thumbprint was provided using dpop_jkt, i.e., reject the subsequent token request unless a DPoP proof for the same key is provided." The thumbprint of the proof checked at the pushed request becomes the code's binding. Proven over the listener by `DpopEndToEndTests.ProofAtThePushedRequestBindsTheCodeToItsKeyAsync` (a redemption under the other DPoP key refused, the same DPoP key then redeems).
  - **Both mechanisms.** [RFC 9449 Section 10.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-10.1) (DPOP-111): "Both mechanisms MUST be supported by an authorization server that supports PAR and DPoP." The library serves both at the pushed request when it verifies DPoP proofs. Proven over the listener by `DpopEndToEndTests.PushedDpopJktBindsTheCodeToThatKeyAsync` (the parameter), `DpopEndToEndTests.ProofAtThePushedRequestBindsTheCodeToItsKeyAsync` (the header) and `DpopEndToEndTests.PushedDpopJktBesideAProofUnderTheSameKeyBindsTheCodeAsync` (both).
  - **Both mechanisms, disagreeing.** [RFC 9449 Section 10.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-10.1) (DPOP-112): "If both mechanisms are used at the same time, the authorization server MUST reject the request if the JWK Thumbprint in dpop_jkt does not match the public key in the DPoP header." The pushed request is refused `invalid_request` with the description "dpop_jkt does not match the DPoP proof's public key." The code is this library's own choice through [RFC 9126 Section 2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9126#section-2.3), "The authorization server returns an error response with the same format as is specified for error responses from the token endpoint in Section 5.2 of [RFC6749] using the appropriate error code from therein or from Section 4.1.2.1 of [RFC6749].", since Section 10.1 names none. Proven over the listener by `DpopEndToEndTests.PushedDpopJktBesideAProofUnderAnotherKeyIsAnInvalidRequestAsync` and `JarParTests.RequestObjectDpopJktBesideAProofUnderAnotherKeyIsAnInvalidRequestAsync`.
  - **The client.** The library's AuthCode client sends `dpop_jkt` when DPoP is wired (the entry for `9214b8eb` below); it sends no proof at the pushed request.
  - **Open.** `AuthCodeEndpoints.HandleAuthorizationCodeReplayAsync` re-verifies the replayed presentation of a spent code (the PKCE verifier, `client_id`, `redirect_uri` and the client authentication) and, when the presentation is valid, revokes the whole grant. It does not consult the DPoP binding of the redeemed code, so a replay of a bound code, presented with its PKCE verifier and no `DPoP` field or under another DPoP key, revokes the grant, where the redemption of an unspent code refuses such a presentation before it claims the code. The owner has decided that the replay is held to the code's binding before it revokes anything; the change is not made.

- **Hold a DPoP proof to one field and the policy (`b0604be7`).** The authorization server and the library's protected resources hold a proof to the checks of [RFC 9449 Section 4.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-4.3) that name a field count, the required claims, the algorithm policy and the target URI, and the library ships the parser, the validator wiring and the algorithm list that make those checks run without the host's own code.
  - **One `DPoP` field.** [RFC 9449 Section 4.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-4.3), check 1 (DPOP-023): "To validate a DPoP proof, the receiving server MUST ensure the following: 1. There is not more than one DPoP HTTP request header field." `DpopRequestReading.ReadDpopField` reads the field by its lines, and a field presented on more than one line is refused before the absent-or-required decision: at the token endpoint 400 `invalid_dpop_proof` with the description "More than one DPoP header field was presented.", at the pushed request and the code redemption the same answer, and at the library's resources 401 with the one `DPoP realm="<issuer>", error="invalid_dpop_proof", scope="openid", algs="..."` challenge at UserInfo, without `scope` at the OID4VCI resources (the entry for `c59d4e1d` below). The field is `DPoP = token68` ([RFC 9449 Section 4.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-4.1)), no list, so [RFC 9110 Section 5.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-5.3) has a sender generate it on one line: "a sender MUST NOT generate multiple field lines with the same name in a message (whether in the headers or trailers) or append a field line when a field line of the same name already exists in the message, unless that field's definition allows multiple field line values to be recombined as a comma-separated list". At the token endpoint and the code redemption, a server that verifies no DPoP proof answers two lines 400 as well; its pushed request reads no `DPoP` field, and its resources check their DPoP wiring before they read the field. Proven over the listener by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRefusesTwoDpopFieldsAsync` (`rfc6749-with-pkce` and `haip-1.0`), `DpopEndToEndTests.PushedRequestRefusesTwoDpopFieldsAsync`, `DpopEndToEndTests.CodeRedemptionRefusesTwoDpopFieldsAsync` and `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesTwoDpopFieldsAsync`.
  - **The required claims.** [RFC 9449 Section 4.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-4.2) (DPOP-015): "The payload of a DPoP proof MUST contain at least the following claims:" and Section 4.3, check 3 (DPOP-025): "3. All required claims per Section 4.2 are contained in the JWT." The client builds `DpopProofClaims` with `htm`, `htu`, `iat` and `jti` required. The receiver's presence check is the parse bundle's contract (`DpopJwsPartParser`: the implementation throws on a missing required member), a throw the validator answers as malformed, and the library ships the parser: `DpopJwsPartParserJson.Default` reads `alg` and `jwk` from the header and `jti`, `htm`, `htu` and `iat` from the payload. It refuses an absent required claim, a member of another JSON type than its definition names and a repeated member name, each as a `FormatException` ([RFC 7515 Section 4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7515#section-4): "The Header Parameter names within the JOSE Header MUST be unique;"), and it reads `iat` as a NumericDate ([RFC 7519 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7519#section-2): "A JSON numeric value representing the number of seconds from 1970-01-01T00:00:00Z UTC until the specified UTC date/time, ignoring leap seconds.") to the whole second from the number's own digits. The validator refuses an empty `jti`. The codecs' slot `AuthorizationServerCodecs.DpopProofParser` runs the parser under the library default. Proven in process by `DpopJwsPartParserJsonTests.AClaimsSetWithoutARequiredClaimIsRefused` (`jti`, `htm`, `htu`, `iat`), and over the listener by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRefusesAProofMissingARequiredClaimAsync` (`htm`, `htu`, `iat`, `jti`) and `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesAProofWithAnEmptyJtiAsync`.
  - **The algorithm.** Section 4.3, check 5 (DPOP-027): "5. The alg JOSE Header Parameter indicates a registered asymmetric digital signature algorithm [IANA.JOSE.ALGS], is not none, is supported by the application, and is acceptable per local policy." One predicate, `WellKnownJwaValues.IsAsymmetricSignatureAlgorithm`, names the asymmetric JWS signature identifiers of the JOSE catalogue, and `DpopProofValidator.IsDpopProofAlg`, `DpopKey` and `JwtHeader.ForDpop` read it. The catalogue also holds the Brainpool ECDSA and ML-DSA identifiers, taken from the drafts their entries cite and not listed in the registry the clause names; the validator binds no public key family to them, so a proof under them is refused, and whether the predicate keeps them is open with the owner. The algorithm of the key reconstructed from `jwk` must be the proof's `alg` (`DpopProofValidator.IsAlgorithmOfKey`). "Supported by the application" is the verification the registry serves for the key's family or the primitive the host binds. "Acceptable per local policy" is `DpopProofValidationRequest.AcceptedAlgorithms`, the host's `ResolveSupportedSigningAlgorithmsAsync` answer for `DpopProof`, held ordinally by the validator at the token endpoint, the pushed request and the code redemption: an empty list accepts no proof, and a resolver that answers no list is a `server_error`. The library's resources hold a proof to the same list (the entry for `c59d4e1d` below). Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesAProofWhoseAlgIsNotTheAlgorithmOfItsKeyAsync`, `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRefusesAProofOutsideTheAcceptedAlgorithmsAsync`, `DpopEndToEndTests.PushedRequestRefusesAProofOutsideTheAcceptedAlgorithmsAsync`, `DpopEndToEndTests.CodeRedemptionRefusesAProofOutsideTheAcceptedAlgorithmsAsync`, `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRefusesEveryProofUnderAnEmptyAcceptedListAsync` and `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointProofWhoseAlgorithmResolverAnswersNoListIsAServerErrorAsync`, and in process by `DpopProofValidatorTests.ValidateAsyncRejectsUnacceptableAlg`, `DpopProofValidatorTests.ValidateAsyncRefusesAnIdentifierOutsideTheAcceptedAlgorithmsAsync` and `WellKnownJwaValuesTests.IsAsymmetricSignatureAlgorithmNamesTheCataloguesAsymmetricSignatureIdentifiers`.
  - **The algorithms the server publishes.** [RFC 9449 Section 5.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-5.1) (DPOP-049): "dpop_signing_alg_values_supported: A JSON array containing a list of the JWS alg values (from the [IANA.JOSE.ALGS] registry) supported by the authorization server for DPoP proof JWTs." The list the metadata composer publishes is the list the token endpoint, the pushed request and the code redemption accept: the host's `ResolveSupportedSigningAlgorithmsAsync` answer for `DpopProof`, by default `WellKnownDpopValues.SupportedSigningAlgorithms` (`ES256`, `ES384`, `ES512`, `ES256K`, `RS256`, `RS384`, `RS512`, `PS256`, `PS384`, `PS512`, `EdDSA` and `Ed25519`); an empty list publishes no member. `ES256K` ([RFC 8812 Section 4.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8812#section-4.3)) and `Ed25519` ([RFC 9864 Section 4.1.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9864#section-4.1.1); the key representation is that of [RFC 9864 Section 5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9864#section-5)) are verified over the wire at the token endpoint. The list is the server's policy, not its verified set: a host whose registry serves fewer families narrows it. The library's resources publish the slot's answer in the `algs` challenge parameter and hold a proof to it (the entry for `c59d4e1d` below). Proven over the listener by `DiscoveryEndpointTests.DiscoveryPublishesTheAlgorithmsTheDefaultSlotAcceptsAsync`, `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointVerifiesAProofUnderAnAlgorithmItsDiscoveryDocumentPublishesAsync`, `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointServesAProofUnderAnAlgorithmTheDefaultListAcceptsAsync`, `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRefusesAProofOutsideTheAcceptedAlgorithmsAsync`, `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRefusesEveryProofUnderAnEmptyAcceptedListAsync` and `DpopEndToEndTests.PushedRequestRefusesAProofOutsideTheAcceptedAlgorithmsAsync`, and in process by `DiscoveryEndpointTests.DiscoveryAdvertisesDpopSigningAlgValuesWhenDpopIsWired`.
  - **The signature.** Section 4.3, check 6 (DPOP-028): "6. The JWT signature verifies with the public key contained in the jwk JOSE Header Parameter." The library verifies it in one of two forms over one body, and a host reaches both: through the provider the cryptographic function registry serves for the tag of the public key reconstructed from `jwk` (`DpopProofValidator.ValidateAsync` without a `VerificationDelegate`, which `DefaultDpopProofValidation` runs for a server that assigns no `ValidateDpopProofAsync`), or through the primitive the host binds (the overload that takes a `VerificationDelegate`, which receives the public key's octets and no algorithm). A server that assigns no `ValidateDpopProofAsync` and wires `AuthorizationServerCodecs.DpopProofParser` beside its encoder and decoder runs the library default at every DPoP site.
    - An RSA public key is tagged by the scheme the proof's `alg` names (`RS256` keeps the size tag; `RS384`, `RS512`, `PS256`, `PS384` and `PS512` their own), so under the registry form a proof labelled with one scheme and signed under another fails ([RFC 7515 Section 5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7515#section-5.2), step 8: the signature is validated "in the manner defined for the algorithm being used, which MUST be accurately represented by the value of the "alg" (algorithm) Header Parameter, which MUST be present."; [RFC 8725 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8725#section-3.1): "The library MUST ensure that the "alg" or "enc" header specifies the same algorithm that is used for the cryptographic operation."). Under the explicit form the bound primitive receives no algorithm and verifies under its own scheme; the validator does not hold it to the scheme the `alg` names, so a host binding one RSA primitive must narrow its list to the one RSA identifier that primitive verifies.
    - The presented `jwk`'s thumbprint must equal the canonical thumbprint of the reconstructed public key ([RFC 7638 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7638#section-3), over the required members of [RFC 7518 Section 6.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7518#section-6.2.1) for an elliptic curve key and [Section 6.3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7518#section-6.3.1) for an RSA key), else the `jwk` is invalid.
    - A `jwk` declared for a `use` other than the string `sig` is invalid under both forms ([RFC 7517 Section 4.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7517#section-4.2): "The "use" value is a case-sensitive string."), a `use` of any JSON type included.
    - Proven over the listener by `IdJagGrantTests.RedeemBoundJagWithTamperedDpopIsInvalidDpopProof`, `DpopBoundRefreshTests.PublicRefreshWithInvalidDpopSignatureAnswersTheSameBodyForAnUnknownAndALiveTokenAsync`, `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointHoldsAPs256ProofToTheSchemeItsAlgNamesAsync`, `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointServesAProofUnderAnAlgorithmTheDefaultListAcceptsAsync` (`ES256K`, `Ed25519`), `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointVerifiesAProofUnderAnAlgorithmItsDiscoveryDocumentPublishesAsync` (`ES256`, `ES256K`, `Ed25519`), `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointVerifiesAProofThroughTheRegistryWhenNoValidatorIsAssignedAsync`, `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRefusesAProofWhoseJwkRespellsItsYCoordinateAsync` and `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRefusesAProofWhoseJwkDeclaresAUseOfAnotherJsonTypeAsync`; and in process by `DpopProofValidatorTests.ValidateAsyncRejectsBadSignature`, `DpopProofValidatorTests.ValidateAsyncVerifiesThroughTheProviderTheRegistryServesForTheProofKeysFamilyAsync` (`ES256`, `ES256K`, `Ed25519`, `EdDSA`, `RS256`, `RS384`, `RS512`, `PS256`, `PS384`, `PS512`), `DpopProofValidatorTests.ValidateAsyncRefusesAnRsaProofSignedUnderAnotherSchemeThanItsAlgNamesAsync` (`PS256`, `RS384`), `DpopProofValidatorTests.ValidateAsyncVerifiesAPs256ProofUnderTheSchemeOfTheBoundPrimitiveAsync`, `DpopProofValidatorTests.ValidateAsyncRefusesAJwkThatIsNoCanonicalRepresentationOfItsPublicKeyAsync` and `DpopProofValidatorTests.ValidateAsyncRefusesHostileOrCrossFamilyJwkMaterialThroughTheRegistryAsync`.
  - **No private key in the `jwk`.** Section 4.3, check 7 (DPOP-029): "7. The jwk JOSE Header Parameter does not contain a private key." The parser keeps a private-key member of any JSON type as its raw text, so the check refuses it as `JwkContainsPrivateKey`. Proven in process by `DpopProofValidatorTests.ValidateAsyncRejectsJwkContainingPrivateKey` and `DpopJwsPartParserJsonTests.AJwkMemberNamingPrivateMaterialIsKeptWhateverItsJsonType` (`oth`, `d`), and over the listener by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRefusesAProofWhoseJwkCarriesPrivateMaterialOfAnotherJsonTypeAsync`.
  - **The target URI.** Section 4.3, check 9 (DPOP-031): "9. The htu claim matches the HTTP URI value for the HTTP request in which the JWT was received, ignoring any query and fragment parts." The URI the claim is held to is the target the server reconstructs from its own configuration ([RFC 9110 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-7.1): "Upon receipt of a client's request, a server reconstructs the target URI from the received components in accordance with their local configuration and incoming connection context."): the scheme and authority of `IncomingRequest.ListenerAuthority` when the host stamped one, otherwise the issuer's, followed by the request's path (`DpopRequestReading.ComposeTargetUri`, read by the token endpoint, the pushed request, the code redemption and the library's resources). A `Host` field value never moves the judgement. The comparison itself is `DpopProofValidator.IsSameTargetUri`. Proven over the listener by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointHoldsHtuToTheListenerAuthorityAsync`, `DpopEndToEndTests.CodeRedemptionHoldsHtuToTheListenerAuthorityAsync` and `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoHoldsHtuToTheListenerAuthorityAsync` (the listener authority 200, the issuer's authority refused `HtuMismatch`), `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ProofIsJudgedAgainstTheConfiguredOriginWhateverHostTheRequestNamesAsync` and `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoJudgesTheProofAgainstTheConfiguredOriginWhateverHostTheRequestNamesAsync` (a rewritten `Host`), `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesAProofWhoseHtuNamesAnotherTargetAsync` and `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ProofWithWrongHtuIsRejectedWithNoAccessToken`.
  - **The proof at the pushed request, completed.** [RFC 9449 Section 10.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-10.1) (DPOP-109): the pushed request's proof now also undergoes check 1 (a repeated `DPoP` field is 400 `invalid_dpop_proof`) and check 5 with the host's accepted algorithms, and its `htu` is the target composed from the listener authority the host stamped, else the issuer's. Proven over the listener by `DpopEndToEndTests.PushedRequestRefusesTwoDpopFieldsAsync` and `DpopEndToEndTests.PushedRequestRefusesAProofOutsideTheAcceptedAlgorithmsAsync`, beside the rows of the entry for `d1e9cead`.
  - **Open.** No test presents a pushed request's proof under a listener authority. The pushed request reads the same composition as the token endpoint, and the tests that hold `htu` to the listener authority run at the token endpoint, the code redemption and UserInfo.

- **The client discards a non-DPoP token response, sends `dpop_jkt` and owns its start's names (`9214b8eb`).** The library's DPoP clients hold the token endpoint to the token type their request asked for, bind the authorization code they request to their DPoP key, and keep a caller's additional fields out of the names the flow composes.
  - **The token type.** [RFC 9449 Section 5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-5) (DPOP-047): "If the access token response contains a different token_type value than DPoP, the access token protection provided by DPoP is not given. The client MUST discard the response in this case if this protection is deemed important for the security of the application; otherwise, the client may continue as in a regular OAuth interaction." The protection is deemed important when DPoP is wired and `OAuthClientInfrastructure.RequiresDpopTokenType` (set through `Create`'s last parameter, `true` by default) or `Oid4VciWalletConfiguration.RequiresDpopTokenType` (`true` by default) holds: the code redemption, the refresh and the `client_credentials` request answer `invalid_response` with an `OAuthInvalidFieldValue` naming `token_type` and echoing no server value, the ID-JAG redemption fails with that `OAuthInvalidFieldValue`, and the wallet's Pre-Authorized Code Token Request answers `MalformedResponse` naming the Token Endpoint with the answer's status; nothing is saved and no token is surfaced. The comparison is case-insensitive ([RFC 6749 Section 5.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-5.1): "Value is case insensitive."). `RequiresDpopTokenType = false` lets the application continue as in a regular OAuth interaction. The ID-JAG mint keeps its `N_A` answer ([RFC 8693 Section 2.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8693#section-2.2.1)). Proven over the listener by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientDiscardsATokenResponseWhoseTokenTypeIsNotDpopAsync` (`Bearer`; `N_A`; `bearer`), `ClientReadsTheDpopTokenTypeInAnyCaseAsync`, `ClientNotRequiringTheDpopTokenTypeContinuesWithABearerResponseAsync`, `DpopEndToEndTests.ClientDiscardsACodeRedemptionAnsweredWithAnotherTokenTypeAsync`, `DpopBoundRefreshTests.ClientDiscardsARefreshAnsweredWithAnotherTokenTypeAsync`, `IdJagGrantTests.RedeemDiscardsATokenResponseWhoseTokenTypeIsNotDpopAsync`, `IdJagGrantTests.MintCompletesAfterOneNonceRetryOverRealWireUnderMandatingRegistration`, `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.WalletDiscardsATokenResponseWhoseTokenTypeIsNotDpopAsync` (`Bearer`; `N_A`), `WalletNotRequiringTheDpopTokenTypePresentsABearerAnswerUnderBearerAsync` and `WalletReadsTheDpopTokenTypeInAnyCaseAsync`; every non-`DPoP` answer is emitted by the listener and read by the client's own transport.
  - **The required `token_type` and the types the wallet uses.** [RFC 6749 Section 5.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-5.1): "token_type REQUIRED. The type of the token issued as described in Section 7.1." A Token Response without `token_type` is `MalformedResponse`. [RFC 6749 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-7.1): "The client MUST NOT use an access token if it does not understand the token type." The wallet understands `Bearer` and `DPoP`, compared case-insensitively: a Token Response naming any other type, a URI-reference included, is refused at the Token Endpoint with the answer's status, read as a type and not used; an access token the application hands in under any other type is refused before a request is sent, as `MalformedResponse` naming the endpoint the token would have been presented at, with no status, at the Credential, Deferred Credential and Notification entries alike. [RFC 9449 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.1): "A DPoP-bound access token is sent using the Authorization request header field per Section 11.6.2 of [RFC9110] with an authentication scheme of DPoP." A token typed `DPoP` is presented under `DPoP` with a proof when a DPoP key is wired; a wallet without a DPoP key refuses it the same way, handed in or named by its own Token Response, since a resource rejects a DPoP-bound token received as a bearer token ([RFC 9449 Section 7.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.2)). A `Bearer` token is presented under `Bearer`. Proven over the listener by `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.WalletRefusesATokenResponseWithoutATokenTypeAsync` (DPoP wired; not wired), `WalletRefusesAHandedInTokenOfATypeItDoesNotUnderstandAsync` (`N_A`; `MAC`), `WalletRefusesAHandedInTokenOfATypeItDoesNotUnderstandAtTheDeferredCredentialEndpointAsync` and `WalletRefusesAHandedInTokenOfATypeItDoesNotUnderstandAtTheNotificationEndpointAsync` (`N_A`; `MAC`; `DPoP` without a DPoP key, the endpoints served), `WalletWithoutADpopKeyRefusesAHandedInDpopTokenAsync`, `WalletWithoutADpopKeyDoesNotUseATokenResponseTypedDpopAsync`, `WalletRefusesATokenResponseTypeItCannotUseAsync` (`N_A` with DPoP wired and the discard not required; `dpop` without a DPoP key), `WalletPresentsAHandedInBearerTokenUnderBearerAsync`, `WalletReadsAHandedInTokenTypeInAnyCaseAsync` (`bearer`; `dpop`) and `APreAuthorizedUriReferenceTokenTypeIsReadButNotUsed`; in process by `AUriReferenceTokenTypeIsReadButNotUsed` (seven rows, the Token Response delivered through the wallet's transport delegate).
  - **The authorization request parameter, taken.** [RFC 9449 Section 10](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-10) (DPOP-104, DPOP-106, DPOP-107): "The value of the dpop_jkt authorization request parameter is the JWK Thumbprint [RFC7638] of the proof-of-possession public key using the SHA-256 hash function, which is the same value as used for the jkt confirmation method defined in Section 6.1." and "Use of the dpop_jkt authorization request parameter is OPTIONAL." The AuthCode client takes the option: when DPoP is wired it sends the thumbprint of its DPoP key, computed in the infrastructure's pool from the same projection the proof's `jwk` carries, once in the pushed request's body ([RFC 9449 Section 10.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-10.1), DPOP-108: "dpop_jkt MUST be included alongside other authorization request parameters in the POST body of the PAR request."), on the direct authorization request beside the PKCE pair (DPOP-107), and as a JSON string claim of the request object it signs, pushed or by value ([RFC 9101 Section 4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9101#section-4): "Parameter names and string values MUST be included as JSON strings."); the code binds to that key and redeems under it alone. Proven over the listener by `DpopEndToEndTests.ClientPushesItsAuthorizationRequestWithDpopJktAsync`, `ClientSendsDpopJktOnItsDirectAuthorizationRequestAsync`, `CodeBoundByTheClientsPushedDpopJktIsRedeemedOnlyUnderItsKeyAsync` (a redemption under another DPoP key; a redemption with no proof) and `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.ARequestObjectStartSignsTheClientsDpopJktAsync` (pushed request object; request object by value), each reading what the server received.
  - **The flow-owned names.** [RFC 6749 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.1): "Request and response parameters MUST NOT be included more than once." [RFC 9126 Section 2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9126#section-2.1): "The request_uri authorization request parameter is one exception, and it MUST NOT be provided." [RFC 9101 Section 5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9101#section-5): with `request` present, `request_uri` MUST NOT be present. Every start the AuthCode client makes drops a caller's additional field under a name the flow composes (`client_id`, `response_type`, `redirect_uri`, `scope`, `state`, `code_challenge`, `code_challenge_method`, `resource`, `dpop_jkt`, `request`, `request_uri`), so the composed request is the flow's, a pushed reference of another start never re-points a direct start, and a request-object start's outer request carries the `client_id`, the `request` and the caller's fields under names the start does not own, which a server uses only from the object ([RFC 9101 Section 6.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9101#section-6.3)). Proven over the listener by `DpopEndToEndTests.ClientOwnsTheDpopJktOfItsPushedRequestAsync` (DPoP not wired; wired), `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.ARequestObjectStartOwnsTheClientIdAndDpopJktOfItsOuterRequestAsync` (pushed; by value) and `AStartOwnsTheRequestAndRequestUriOfItsOuterRequestAsync` (direct; pushed; pushed request object; request object by value; direct with the reference alone).
  - **Deviation, recorded.** A request-object start's pushed body relays a caller's fields under names the start does not own beside `request`, where [RFC 9126 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9126#section-3) makes the client-authentication parameters "the only parameters other than request in the form body"; a conformant server uses the object's parameters alone. The owner decides whether the relay stays as documented or is dropped.

- **Bind a client to DPoP by its registration (`4bf56546`).** A client may declare that it always uses DPoP, and every grant the token endpoint serves holds it to the declaration; a refresh token issued to a public client that presents a valid proof is bound to that proof's DPoP key, every refresh token is held at every presentation to the binding it was issued under (none when unbound), and a confidential client's is not bound; the proof header's clauses are held over the listener; and an RSA public key is tagged by the scheme its `alg` names wherever the library reconstructs it from a JWK, client assertions included.
  - **The declaration.** [RFC 9449 Section 5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-5.2) (DPOP-050): "dpop_bound_access_tokens: A boolean value specifying whether the client always uses DPoP for token requests. If omitted, the default value is false." The name is `ClientMetadataParameterNames.DpopBoundAccessTokens` (registered by [RFC 9449 Section 12.10](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-12.10)); `ClientMetadata.DpopBoundAccessTokens` is `bool?`, `null` when omitted; `ClientRecord.DpopBoundAccessTokens` is `bool`, set by the registration, the RFC 7592 update and the Client ID Metadata Document overlay, `false` when omitted. The registration response and the RFC 7592 read and update answers carry the member as a JSON boolean when it was declared ([RFC 7591 Section 3.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7591#section-3.2.1): "the authorization server MUST return all registered metadata about this client"). A Client ID Metadata Document whose member is present and is no JSON boolean is a defective document, `ClientIdMetadataDocumentDefects.InvalidDpopBoundAccessTokens`, which the resolver refuses as it refuses every defect ([RFC 7591 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7591#section-2): "All data member types (strings, arrays, numbers) are defined in terms of their JSON [RFC7159] representations."). A declaration the server provisions on the record is carried on the RFC 7592 read as the record holds it, `true` over an omitted member and `false` over a registered `true` ([RFC 7592 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7592#section-3): "Additionally, the authorization server MUST return all registered metadata about this client, including any fields provisioned by the authorization server itself.", beside [RFC 7591 Section 3.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7591#section-3.2.1)), and an RFC 7592 update that omits the member deletes it ([RFC 7592 Section 2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7592#section-2.2): "Omitted fields MUST be treated as null or empty values by the server, indicating the client's request to delete them from the client's registration."), whatever value the host provisioned on the record; a host's DPoP mandate that the client cannot remove is the registration's policy profile (`ClientPolicyProfiles.RequiresDpop`), which no client configuration update changes. Proven over the listener by `DynamicRegistrationEndToEndTests.RegistrationEchoesDpopBoundAccessTokensAsync`, `DynamicRegistrationEndToEndTests.RegistrationUpdateReplacesDpopBoundAccessTokensAsync`, `DynamicRegistrationEndToEndTests.RegistrationReadEchoesAServerProvisionedDpopDeclarationAsync`, `DynamicRegistrationEndToEndTests.RegistrationReadReportsAProvisionedFalseOverARegisteredTrueAsync`, `DynamicRegistrationEndToEndTests.RegistrationUpdateOmittingDpopBoundAccessTokensDeletesItAsync`, `ClientIdMetadataDocumentAdversarialFlowTests.ADocumentDeclaringDpopBoundAccessTokensBindsTheClientAsync` (declared `true`; omitted) and `ClientIdMetadataDocumentAdversarialFlowTests.ADocumentWhoseDpopBoundAccessTokensIsNoBooleanAbortsAsync` (a JSON string, number, null, object or array; a member name escaping a lone surrogate), and in process by the `dpop_bound_access_tokens` rows of `ClientMetadataParameterNamesTests.MemberWireSpellingAndSpanAgreeWithRfc` and `ClientMetadataParameterNamesTests.MemberPredicateIsExactAndOrdinal`.
  - **The refusal.** Section 5.2 (DPOP-051): "If the value is true, the authorization server MUST reject token requests from the client that do not contain the DPoP header.", which [Section 5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-5) makes "applicable for all access token requests regardless of grant type". The six grants the token endpoint serves (the code redemption, the refresh, client credentials, token exchange, the JWT bearer grant and the pre-authorized code) read one predicate, `AuthCodeEndpoints.IsProofRequired`: the registration's profile mandates DPoP, or the client declared `true`. A request without the field is answered as a request under a mandating profile is, 400 `use_dpop_nonce` "DPoP proof required." with one `DPoP-Nonce` ([Section 8](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-8)); Section 5.2 names no error code. Proven over the listener by `ClientDeclaringDpopBoundAccessTokensWithoutAProofIsRefusedAsync` in `ClientCredentialsGrantTests`, `DpopEndToEndTests`, `DpopBoundRefreshTests`, `TokenExchangeGrantTests`, `JwtBearerGrantTests` and `Oid4VciPreAuthorizedCodeGrantTests`, by `DynamicRegistrationEndToEndTests.ClientDeclaringDpopBoundAccessTokensOverTheWireIsRefusedATokenRequestWithoutAProofAsync` (declared in the registration; in an update), by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientDeclaringDpopBoundAccessTokensIsServedByItsDeclarationAsync` (`true` with a valid proof answered `DPoP`; `false` without a proof answered `Bearer`) and by the declared row of `ClientIdMetadataDocumentAdversarialFlowTests.ADocumentDeclaringDpopBoundAccessTokensBindsTheClientAsync`.
  - **A server that verifies no proof.** [RFC 7591 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7591#section-2): "The authorization server MAY reject any requested client metadata values by replacing requested values with suitable defaults as described in Section 3.2.1 or by returning an error response as described in Section 3.2.2."
