A registration or an RFC 7592 update declaring `true` at a server that verifies no DPoP proof (`DpopRequestReading.IsProofValidationWired` false) is refused 400 `invalid_client_metadata` "dpop_bound_access_tokens cannot be true at an authorization server that verifies no DPoP proof.", since every token request of such a client would fail. A Client ID Metadata Document is the client's own and is not refused at materialization for the declaration: the token endpoint answers a token request of such a client without a proof 500 `server_error` "DPoP enforcement is required by policy but DPoP delegates are not wired." and issues no token. Proven over the listener by `DynamicRegistrationEndToEndTests.RegistrationDeclaringDpopBoundAccessTokensUnderAServerVerifyingNoProofIsRefusedAsync` (the registration; the update) and `ClientIdMetadataDocumentAdversarialFlowTests.ADocumentDeclaringDpopBoundAccessTokensAtAServerVerifyingNoProofIsAnsweredServerErrorAsync` (a code redemption without a proof).
  - **The refresh token of a confidential client.** [RFC 9449 Section 5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-5) (DPOP-048): "Refresh tokens issued to confidential clients (those having established authentication credentials with the authorization server) are not bound to the DPoP proof public key because they are already sender-constrained with a different existing mechanism.", the mechanism of [RFC 6749 Section 6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-6): the authorization server MUST "require client authentication for confidential clients". The binding is decided at issuance: a refresh token issued to a public client that presents a valid proof is bound to that proof's DPoP key, and every refresh token is held at every presentation to the binding it was issued under (none when unbound), whatever the registration declares later; a confidential client's is not bound. The record of a refresh token, whether the code redemption, the token exchange or a refresh rotation issues it, stores the issuance's binding only when the client is public under the registration in force at that request (`AuthCodeEndpoints.RefreshTokenConfirmationOf` over `AuthCodeEndpoints.IsPublicClient`, the one reading of a public client, which the loopback redirect fallback also reads); the access token issued beside it keeps its own binding. A presented refresh token is held to the binding its record stores, whatever the client's registration declares at the presentation, so an RFC 7592 update that turns a public client confidential leaves its refresh tokens bound, and one that turns a confidential client public leaves its refresh tokens unbound. A retired refresh token is held at its reuse to its own binding, the one it was issued under: the rotation that retires it carries that binding from the retired refresh record onto the retired record as the public member `ServerTokenIssuedState.RetiredRefreshTokenConfirmation`, beside `ServerTokenIssuedState.Confirmation`, which mirrors the access token the rotation issued and sets its `token_type`. A confidential client refreshes under another DPoP key, or without a proof under a profile that leaves DPoP optional. An authenticated reuse of a confidential client's retired refresh token, under any DPoP key or none, is a valid reuse and revokes the grant ([OAuth 2.1 draft-16 Section 4.3.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-v2-1-16#section-4.3.1): "The authorization server cannot determine which party submitted the invalid refresh token, but it will revoke the active refresh token as well as the access authorization grant associated with it."); a public client's retired refresh token that was bound, presented with no proof or with a proof by another DPoP key, is refused and revokes nothing; presented under the DPoP key it is bound to, it revokes the grant; every reuse of a public client's retired refresh token that was unbound and that passes the request's own checks and the client checks revokes the grant, whatever proof its rotation carried (a malformed `DPoP` field is refused before the record is read). A retired record that carries no `RetiredRefreshTokenConfirmation`, such as one an application stored without the member, reads null and holds its retired refresh token to no binding, so the reuse of a retired token that was bound, recorded without the member, revokes the grant when it is presented without that token's DPoP key, for that token's lifetime: a denial of service and nothing more, since no token is issued and nothing escapes revocation. An application store that persists this state persists the member. Proven over the listener by `RefreshConfidentialClientAuthenticationTests.ConfidentialClientRefreshesUnderAnotherDpopKeyAsync`, `RefreshConfidentialClientAuthenticationTests.ConfidentialClientRefreshWithoutAProofUnderAnOptionalProfileIsServedBearerAsync`, `RefreshConfidentialClientAuthenticationTests.ConfidentialClientTurnedPublicRefreshesItsUnboundTokenAsync`, `RefreshConfidentialClientAuthenticationTests.ConfidentialClientReuseOfARotatedRefreshTokenRevokesTheGrantAsync` (under another DPoP key; with no proof), `DpopBoundRefreshTests.PublicClientTurnedConfidentialIsHeldToItsRefreshTokensBindingAsync` (no proof and another DPoP key refused 400 `invalid_grant`; the bound DPoP key served), `DpopBoundRefreshTests.PublicClientTurnedConfidentialReusingARetiredRefreshTokenAnswersAsABoundReuseAsync` and `DpopBoundRefreshTests.PublicClientsUnboundRefreshTokenRotatedUnderAProofIsRevokedByItsReuseAsync` (the retired refresh token presented with no proof; under a proof by another DPoP key; under a proof by the DPoP key of the rotation).
  - **The refresh of a public client.** Section 5 (DPOP-043): "When an authorization server supporting DPoP issues a refresh token to a public client that presents a valid DPoP proof at the token endpoint, the refresh token MUST be bound to the respective public key.", and "To sender-constrain the access token after checking the validity of the DPoP proof, the authorization server associates the issued access token with the public key from the DPoP proof"; (DPOP-046) "An authorization server MAY elect to issue access tokens that are not DPoP bound, which is signaled to the client with a value of Bearer in the token_type parameter of the access token response per [RFC6750]." The refreshed access token carries the binding of the validated proof, and the rotated refresh token of a public client carries it too, so a public client presenting a valid proof with an unbound refresh token is answered `DPoP` and its successor is bound to that DPoP key; without a proof the issuance is `Bearer`. Proven over the listener by `DpopBoundRefreshTests.PublicClientRefreshOfAnUnboundTokenWithAValidProofBindsTheIssuanceAsync` and `DpopBoundRefreshTests.DpopMandatingProfileChallengesAnUnboundRefreshRecordObtainedBeforeTheProfileMandatedItAsync`, beside `DpopBoundRefreshTests.DpopSenderConstrainedRefreshTokenRefreshesThroughRealClientAsync`.
  - **The proof header, over the listener.** [RFC 9449 Section 4.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-4.2) (DPOP-012): "The JOSE Header of a DPoP JWT MUST contain at least the following parameters:"; (DPOP-013) `alg` "MUST NOT be none or an identifier for a symmetric algorithm (Message Authentication Code (MAC))."; (DPOP-014) `jwk` "MUST NOT contain a private key."; (DPOP-017) "htm: The value of the HTTP method (Section 9.1 of [RFC9110]) of the request to which the JWT is attached."; (DPOP-018) "htu: The HTTP target URI (Section 7.1 of [RFC9110]) of the request to which the JWT is attached, without query and fragment parts." These tests hold each clause over the listener, at the token endpoint and, where named, at UserInfo: a proof lacking a required header parameter, under a MAC algorithm or whose `jwk` carries a private key is refused, and the proof the library's client sends carries the three parameters, a public `jwk`, and the method and target of its request. Proven by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRefusesAProofWhoseHeaderLacksARequiredParameterAsync` (`typ` `InvalidTyp`; `alg` and `jwk` `Malformed`), `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesAProofWhoseHeaderLacksARequiredParameterAsync` (`typ`, `alg`, `jwk`), `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientSendsAProofHeaderCarryingTypAlgAndJwkAsync`, `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRefusesAProofUnderAMacAlgorithmAsync` and `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesAProofUnderAMacAlgorithmAsync` (an HMAC with SHA-256 under `HS256`), `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientSendsAProofWhoseJwkCarriesNoPrivateKeyAsync`, `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRefusesAProofWhoseJwkCarriesAPrivateKeyAsync` (a string `d`), `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientSendsAProofNamingTheMethodOfItsRequestAsync`, `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.WalletSendsEveryProofNamingTheMethodOfItsRequestAsync` and `Oid4VciPreAuthorizedCodeGrantTests.ProofWithWrongHtmIsRejectedWithoutConsultingTheSeam` (the receiving half), and `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientSendsAProofNamingTheTargetOfItsRequestAsync`. No endpoint the test host serves carries a query in its URL, so the `htu` rows assert the target of a query-less endpoint, its `htu` carrying no query and no fragment.
  - **The client assertion's RSA scheme.** [RFC 7515 Section 5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7515#section-5.2), step 8: the signature is validated "in the manner defined for the algorithm being used, which MUST be accurately represented by the value of the "alg" (algorithm) Header Parameter", and [RFC 8725 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8725#section-3.1): "The library MUST ensure that the "alg" or "enc" header specifies the same algorithm that is used for the cryptographic operation." `DpopJwkUtilities.PublicKeyFromJwk` tags an RSA public key by the `alg` it is used under (`RS256` keeps the size tag; `RS384`, `RS512`, `PS256`, `PS384` and `PS512` the tag of their scheme), for the DPoP proof and the `private_key_jwt` client assertion alike, and `CryptoFormatConversions.DefaultAlgorithmToJwkConverter` renders an RSA public key under a scheme tag as under its size tag, so the canonical thumbprint is one rendering. A `private_key_jwt` client assertion signed under the scheme its `alg` names authenticates the client; under the registry form of `PrivateKeyJwtClientAuthentication.BuildValidator`, one labelled `PS256` and signed RSASSA-PKCS1-v1_5 with SHA-256 does not. Under the explicit form, whose one bound verification primitive verifies the scheme it implements whatever the `alg` names, the host narrows `ClientAssertionSigningAlgorithmsSupported`, or the registration's `token_endpoint_auth_signing_alg`, to the one RSA identifier that primitive verifies, and an assertion under another identifier is refused before any signature is checked. The library publishes no list of its own: `AuthorizationServerIntegration.ClientAssertionSigningAlgorithmsSupported` is empty by default and the server publishes the host's declaration, under which honest `PS256`, `PS384`, `PS512`, `RS384` and `RS512` assertions authenticate under the registry form. Proven over the listener by `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointAuthenticatesAnRsaClientAssertionUnderTheSchemeItsAlgNamesAsync` (`PS256`, `RS384`), `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRefusesAPs256ClientAssertionSignedUnderAnotherSchemeAsync` and `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ExplicitAssertionValidatorNarrowedToRs256RefusesAPs256LabelledAssertionAsync` (the explicit form, a PKCS#1 v1.5 SHA-256 primitive, the list `RS256`), beside `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointHoldsAPs256ProofToTheSchemeItsAlgNamesAsync`, and in process by `RsaPublicKeyConversionTests.AnRsaPublicKeyUnderASchemeTagConvertsToTheJwkOfItsOctets` (the six scheme tags).
  - **Open.** `ClientRecord.TokenEndpointAuthMethod` `null` is read as a public client, so a refresh token issued to a client that declared no method is bound as a public client's; [RFC 7591 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7591#section-2) makes an omitted `token_endpoint_auth_method` `client_secret_basic` ("If unspecified or omitted, the default is "client_secret_basic", denoting the HTTP Basic authentication scheme as specified in Section 2.3.1 of OAuth 2.0."). The reading is the library's own, kept until a change of its own moves it to the specification's default together with the authentication the token endpoint requires of such a client.

- **Publish the DPoP challenge and its parser (`c59d4e1d`).** The library builds and reads the `WWW-Authenticate: DPoP` challenge through one public pair, every `DPoP` challenge of its own resources carries the realm, the scope the resource requires and the algorithms the authorization server accepts, every answer carrying one exposes its fields to a browser application, and a repeated `Authorization` field is answered with the challenge of the mechanism its lines attempt.
  - **The challenge.** [RFC 9449 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.1): "The scheme name is DPoP." (DPOP-067); "The authentication parameter realm MAY be included to indicate the scope of protection in the manner described in [RFC9110], Section 11.5." (DPOP-068); "A scope authentication parameter MAY be included as defined in [RFC6750], Section 3." (DPOP-069); "An error parameter ([RFC6750], Section 3) SHOULD be included to indicate the reason why the request was declined, if the request included an access token but failed authentication." (DPOP-070, 071); "An algs parameter SHOULD be included to signal to the client the JWS algorithms that are acceptable for the DPoP proof JWT. The value of the parameter is a space-delimited list of JWS alg (Algorithm) header values ([RFC7515], Section 4.1.1)." (DPOP-073). `DpopChallenge.BuildChallenge` writes the scheme and then `realm`, `error`, `scope`, `algs` and `resource_metadata` in that order, each at most once ([RFC 9110 Section 11.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-11.2): "each parameter name MUST only occur once per challenge") and each as a quoted-string ([RFC 9110 Section 11.5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-11.5): "For historical reasons, a sender MUST only generate the quoted-string syntax."), with no parameter the scheme alone ([RFC 9110 Section 11.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-11.3): "challenge = auth-scheme [ 1*SP ( token68 / #auth-param ) ]"); a value outside its character set ([RFC 6750 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3) for `error` and `scope`, a quoted-string for `realm`, a token for each algorithm name) is the caller's own argument and throws. It writes no `error_description` (DPOP-072, "MAY be included"): a refusal's description travels in the response body. Every `DPoP` challenge of UserInfo and the OID4VCI Credential, Deferred Credential and Notification endpoints is built by it from one context resolved once per request, before the `Authorization` field is read (`CredentialEndpointDpopValidation.ReadPresentationAsync`): the `realm` is the issuer the request is served under in its header-safe form, the one realm of the one protection space ([RFC 9110 Section 11.5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-11.5): "A "protection space" is defined by the origin (see Section 4.3.1) of the server being accessed, in combination with the realm value if present."); the `scope` is `openid` at UserInfo and absent at the OID4VCI resources, which name no scope; the `algs` are the identifiers of the authorization server's accepted DPoP proof algorithms that a proof can be accepted under (`DpopProofValidator.AcceptableProofAlgorithms`), the parameter absent when none remains; the list is the one slot the token endpoint and the discovery document read (`DpopTokenEndpointValidation.ResolveAcceptedProofAlgorithmsAsync`, by default `WellKnownDpopValues.SupportedSigningAlgorithms`), and the resources hold every proof's `alg` to the list (the entry for `23539b6c` below), refusing another as `invalid_dpop_proof` `InvalidAlg` ([RFC 9449 Section 4.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-4.3), check 5: "is acceptable per local policy"); a host resolver that answers no list, an empty list or a list holding a name outside the token grammar leaves the challenge without `algs`, and under no list or such a name a presented proof is answered 500 `server_error` with a fixed text, as the token endpoint answers no list. The token grammar of [RFC 9110 Section 5.6.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-5.6.2) is the library's chosen bound on an algorithm name, and it excludes no name a DPoP proof may carry: [RFC 9449 Section 4.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-4.2), "alg: An identifier for a JWS asymmetric digital signature algorithm from [IANA.JOSE.ALGS]", and [Section 4.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-4.3) check 5, a registered algorithm, where every registered name is made of tchar only, while a quoted-string could carry a Collision-Resistant Name of [RFC 7515 Section 4.1.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7515#section-4.1.1) that no proof may carry. An issuer the header-safe mapping cannot write (a host IDNA cannot map; a relative URI answered by an application resolver) is read as no realm, as an issuer that does not resolve is. A host's misconfiguration is answered by a decision, never by an exception escaping the endpoint; the slot's answer is read twice in a request, at the check and at the challenge and the validation, and must not change under it. A request without credentials is answered 401 with an empty body and both challenges, `Bearer realm="<issuer>", scope="openid", DPoP realm="<issuer>", scope="openid", algs="..."` at UserInfo and `Bearer realm="<issuer>", DPoP realm="<issuer>", algs="..."` at the OID4VCI resources, each challenge also naming its resource's metadata document where one is served (the entry for `bd54b3f7` below), no `error` in either ([RFC 6750 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3.1): "the resource server SHOULD NOT include an error code or other error information"; [RFC 9449 Section 7.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.2), rule 1); a `DPoP` credential outside the `token68` form ([RFC 9449 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.1), Figure 12: `credentials = "DPoP" 1*SP token68`; DPOP-063) is answered the same way, the deviation recorded below. Where no issuer resolves, the `DPoP` challenge is sent without `realm` ([RFC 9110 Section 11.6.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-11.6.1): "A server generating a 401 (Unauthorized) response MUST send a WWW-Authenticate header field containing at least one challenge."), a repeated field is answered 400 with its challenge without `realm` (the section's "A server MAY generate a WWW-Authenticate header field in other response messages to indicate that supplying credentials (or different credentials) might affect the response."), and a request without credentials at an OID4VCI resource, whose `Bearer` challenge then has no auth-param to carry ([RFC 6750 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3): "This scheme MUST be followed by one or more auth-param values."), is answered 500 `server_error`, the fault being the server's own wiring. Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoDpopChallengeCarriesTheRealmOfItsProtectionSpaceAsync`, `UserInfoDpopChallengeNamesTheRequiredScopeInEveryRefusalAsync` (`invalid_dpop_proof`; `use_dpop_nonce`; `invalid_token`; the no-credential answer), `UserInfoRefusesAProofOutsideTheAcceptedAlgorithmsAsync`, `UserInfoAnswersAProofWhenTheAlgorithmResolverAnswersNoListAsServerErrorAsync`, `UserInfoRefusesEveryProofUnderAnEmptyAcceptedListAsync`, `UserInfoAnswersANoCredentialRequestWithoutAlgsWhereTheAlgorithmListIsUnusableAsync`, `UserInfoAnswersAProofWhereTheAlgorithmListIsUnusableAsServerErrorAsync`, `UserInfoDpopChallengeOmitsTheRealmWhereNoIssuerResolvesAsync` (the default resolver refusing; an application resolver answering nothing), `UserInfoDpopChallengeOmitsTheRealmWhereTheIssuerHasNoHeaderFormAsync` (a host IDNA cannot map; a relative URI), `UserInfoRefusesADpopCredentialOutsideToken68Async` (a space; a double quote; no credentials after the scheme), `UserInfoDpopChallengeCarriesNoErrorDescriptionParameterAsync`, `Oid4VciDpopCredentialEndpointTests.CredentialEndpointDpopChallengeCarriesTheRealmAndNoScopeAsync` (a tampered token; the nonce challenge), `CredentialEndpointRefusesAProofOutsideTheAcceptedAlgorithmsAsync` and `CredentialEndpointAnswersANoCredentialRequestWhoseIssuerDoesNotResolveAsServerErrorAsync` (the default resolver refusing; an application resolver answering nothing), and in process by `BearerTokenChallengeTests.DpopChallengeIsBuiltWithItsParametersInTheOrderTheBuilderWritesThem` (every parameter; `error` and `algs`; none) and `DpopChallengeBuilderRefusesAValueOutsideItsCharset` (an `error` holding a double quote; a `scope` with two spaces; a `realm` holding U+0001; an algorithm name holding a space; a metadata URL holding CR).
  - **The parser.** [RFC 9449 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.1) (DPOP-074): "Additional authentication parameters MAY be used, and unknown parameters MUST be ignored by recipients." `DpopChallenge.TryParse` reads one `WWW-Authenticate` field value and `DpopChallenge.TryRead` every field line of a response, in received order, into `DpopChallengeParameters` (`Realm`, `Error`, `ErrorDescription`, `Scope`, `Algorithms`, `ResourceMetadata`), through the one challenge-list reader `BearerTokenChallenge` holds ([RFC 9110 Section 11.6.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-11.6.1): "WWW-Authenticate = #challenge"); an unknown parameter is ignored, a bare `DPoP` is read with every parameter absent, the names of `algs` are the SP-separated words of its value, a token68 after the scheme refuses the reading, and so does a `resource_metadata` that is no absolute http or https URL, both the library's own rules: [RFC 9110 Section 11.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-11.3) allows a token68 in a challenge, and the `DPoP` challenge of Section 7.1 is written with auth-params; [RFC 9728 Section 5.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-5.1) names no scheme for the parameter, while [Section 1.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-1.2) makes the resource identifier "a URL that uses the https scheme" and [Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-3) derives the metadata URL from it, so the reader accepts http as a recipient and the application's fetch policy decides what is fetched. Two `DPoP` challenges in one response read as none: [RFC 9110 Section 11.5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-11.5) lets a response carry "multiple challenges with the same auth-scheme but with different realms" and names no one of them for a client to act on, so the library's own rule, which its `Bearer` reading already applies, selects exactly one. The library's DPoP nonce retry and the wallet read a nonce challenge through it, with no change of behaviour. Proven in process by `BearerTokenChallengeTests.DpopChallengeIsReadFromTheChallengeList` (Section 7.1's Figures 15, 16, 17 and 19; a challenge continued on a second field line; an unknown parameter), `DpopChallengeReadingCarriesEveryParameterItNames` (every member asserted on each read: every parameter carried; the bare scheme; an `algs` with doubled spaces) and `DpopChallengeReadingFailsWhereTheListHoldsNoOneDpopChallenge` (two `DPoP` challenges; `Bearer` alone; an unterminated quoted-string; a token68; an unusable `resource_metadata`), and over the listener by the wallet's rows that answer a nonce challenge carrying an unknown parameter.
  - **The exposure to a browser application.** [RFC 9449 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.1) (DPOP-075): "In order for the application to obtain and use the WWW-Authenticate HTTP response header value, the server needs to make it available to the application by including WWW-Authenticate in the Access-Control-Expose-Headers response header list value." and [Section 8](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-8) (DPOP-093): "In order for the application to obtain and use the DPoP-Nonce HTTP response header value, the server needs to make it available to the application by including DPoP-Nonce in the Access-Control-Expose-Headers response header list value.", which [Section 9](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-9) carries to resource servers. Every resource answer carrying a `DPoP` challenge and every token endpoint nonce challenge (the pushed request's included) carries one field line `Access-Control-Expose-Headers: WWW-Authenticate, DPoP-Nonce` (`WellKnownHttpHeaderNames.AccessControlExposeHeaders`), written whether or not the request names an `Origin`: the library reads no `Origin` and owns no CORS policy, so the field has effect only where the host's CORS layer admits the origin, and a host layer that sets the field itself keeps the two names in its list. A 500 carries no challenge and no exposure field. Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoDpopAnswersExposeTheirFieldsToABrowserApplicationAsync` (the nonce challenge; an invalid proof; no credentials) and `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointNonceChallengeExposesTheDpopNonceFieldAsync`.
  - **The repeated field.** [RFC 9449 Section 7.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.2) (DPOP-079): "If the mechanism used to attempt authentication could be established unambiguously, then the corresponding challenge SHOULD be used to deliver error information (Figure 18)." and "Otherwise, both Bearer and DPoP challenges MAY be used to deliver error information (Figure 19)." An `Authorization` field sent on two or more lines is no credential ([RFC 9110 Section 11.6.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-11.6.2): "Authorization = credentials") and is refused 400 `invalid_request` ([RFC 6750 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3.1)) with the challenge on the 400 ([RFC 9110 Section 11.6.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-11.6.1): "A server MAY generate a WWW-Authenticate header field in other response messages to indicate that supplying credentials (or different credentials) might affect the response."): lines that all name `DPoP` get the `DPoP` challenge alone, lines that all name `Bearer` the `Bearer` challenge alone, and lines of two schemes, or a line without a scheme token, both. `AuthorizationFieldReading.RepeatedScheme` names the scheme every line of a repeated field carries, compared ASCII case-insensitively. The client configuration endpoint, which reads a `Bearer` token only, answers the `Bearer` challenge alone as before. Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesTwoBearerAuthorizationLinesWithTheBearerChallengeAloneAsync`, `UserInfoAnswersTwoDpopAuthorizationLinesWithTheDpopChallengeAloneAsync` (`DPoP` and `dpop`), `UserInfoAnswersAuthorizationLinesOfTwoSchemesWithBothChallengesAsync`, `UserInfoAnswersARepeatedAuthorizationFieldWhereNoIssuerResolvesAsync` (two `Bearer` lines; two `DPoP` lines; both schemes), `Oid4VciDpopCredentialEndpointTests.CredentialEndpointRefusesTwoBearerAuthorizationLinesWithTheBearerChallengeAloneAsync`, `CredentialEndpointAnswersTwoDpopAuthorizationLinesWithTheDpopChallengeAloneAsync`, `Oid4VciDeferredAndNotificationTests.ProtectedEndpointRefusesTwoBearerAuthorizationLinesWithTheBearerChallengeAloneAsync` (the Deferred Credential and Notification endpoints) and `ClientIdMetadataDocumentAdversarialFlowTests.TwoAuthorizationLinesAtUserInfoAreAnsweredWithTheBearerChallengeAloneAsync`, and in process by `AuthorizationFieldReadingTests.RepeatedLinesNameTheirOneScheme` (two `Bearer`; `DPoP` and `dpop`; `Bearer` and `DPoP`; a line with no scheme token).
