Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesAProofWhoseJtiIsLongerThanTheReplayGuardStoresAsync`, `UserInfoServesAProofWhoseIatIsSecondsAheadAsync` and `UserInfoRefusesAProofCarryingANonceOlderThanItsValidityWindowAsync`.
  - **`resource_metadata`, written only for the URL the client used.** [RFC 9728 Section 5.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-5.1) (PRM-010): "This parameter MAY also be used in WWW-Authenticate responses using authorization schemes other than "Bearer" [RFC6750], such as the DPoP scheme defined by [RFC9449]." The builder and the parser carry the parameter; a library resource writes it only where a document of its own is served and the URL the client used is the resource's identifier (the entry for `bd54b3f7` below), because [RFC 9728 Section 3.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-3.3) makes a conforming client discard a document whose `resource` is not that URL: "the resource value returned MUST be identical to the URL that the client used to make the request to the resource server. If these values are not identical, the data contained in the response MUST NOT be used." Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoDpopChallengeCarriesNoResourceMetadataParameterAsync` (a registration without the capability: no document served, the parameter absent) and `UserInfoChallengeNamesNoResourceMetadataUnderAListenerAliasAsync` (the metadata capability granted; a listener alias).
  - **A credential outside token68.** A `Bearer` or `DPoP` credential outside token68 is answered 400 `invalid_request` with the named scheme's challenge alone (the entry for `23539b6c` below).
  - **Open.** `WellKnownHttpHeaderNames.AccessControlExposeHeaders` has no `IsAccessControlExposeHeaders` predicate, which the class's other entries carry; the owner decides whether it is added. The resources resolve the accepted-algorithm slot and the issuer once per request before the `Authorization` field is read, also for a `Bearer` presentation they then serve, and a served `DPoP` request resolves the issuer three times (the challenge context, the token validation, the enforcement), so a host's `ResolveSupportedSigningAlgorithmsAsync` and issuer resolver answer on every resource request.

- **Make the DPoP resource checks public (`7ae07edc`).** The checks a DPoP-protected resource runs on a presented access token are one public composition an application's resource calls over its own `ResourceServerIntegration`, the library's resources run the same composition, an integration flag makes the library's resources a protection space that accepts DPoP-bound access tokens alone, and the protected resource metadata documents of those resources state that requirement and an empty `bearer_methods_supported` (the entry for `bd54b3f7` below).
  - **The composition.** [RFC 9449 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.1) (DPOP-064): "For such an access token, a resource server MUST check that a DPoP proof was also received in the DPoP header field of the HTTP request, check the DPoP proof according to the rules in Section 4.3, and check that the public key of the DPoP proof matches the public key to which the access token is bound per Section 6." and (DPOP-065): "The resource server MUST NOT grant access to the resource unless all checks are successful." `DpopResourceAccess.DecideAsync` takes the facts of one presentation (`DpopResourceRequest`: the access token, the scheme it came under, what the caller's own validation read of its `cnf`, the method, the target URI, every `DPoP` field line, the resource's realm, scope and metadata URL, and whether the resource requires DPoP-bound tokens) and the integration's slots (`ResourceServerIntegration.ValidateDpopProofAsync`, `IssueDpopNonceAsync`, `ValidateDpopNonceAsync`, `ConsultDpopProofJtiAsync`, `AcceptedDpopProofAlgorithms`, `DpopFreshnessWindow`, `StartTraceActivity`), starts the decision's span through the last of them (the entry for `a199cc7c` below), and answers a `DpopResourceDecision`: `Permit` with the proof's JWK SHA-256 Thumbprint, `Deny` with the exact response to send, or `IndeterminateDeny` where a check the policy needs is not wired or a slot answers an outcome the checks do not name, so that a presentation is never served by an absent check ([Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.1), DPOP-065). The order of checks: a `Bearer` presentation of a DPoP-bound token refused `invalid_token` under the `Bearer` challenge ([Section 7.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.2), DPOP-077: "such a protected resource MUST reject a DPoP-bound access token received as a bearer token per [RFC6750]"), and a token sender-constrained by a method the resource cannot check refused the same way; an unbound token under `DPoP` refused `invalid_token` ([Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.1), DPOP-062); two or more `DPoP` field lines refused `invalid_dpop_proof` ([Section 4.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-4.3), DPOP-023, check 1: "There is not more than one DPoP HTTP request header field."); no proof answered with the resource's nonce challenge ([Section 7](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7), DPOP-059: "Requests to DPoP-protected resources MUST include both a DPoP proof as per Section 4 and the access token as described in Section 7.1."; [Section 9](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-9), DPOP-101: "Resource servers use an HTTP 401 (Unauthorized) error code with an accompanying WWW-Authenticate: DPoP value and DPoP-Nonce value to accomplish this."); the proof validated by the wired validation against the method, the target URI, the token's hash and the accepted algorithms (Section 4.3's checks; the algorithms the one list `AcceptedDpopProofAlgorithms` names, by default `WellKnownDpopValues.SupportedSigningAlgorithms`); the proof's key compared with the token's `cnf.jkt` in fixed time ([Section 6.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-6.1)); the proof's `iat` held to `DpopFreshnessWindow` of the integration's clock either way, the bounds included, whatever tolerance the wired validation applies ([Section 11.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-11.1), DPOP-113: "servers MUST only accept DPoP proofs for a limited time after their creation"; DPOP-116, the future side taken); the resource's own nonce required in every proof and validated with the target URI as its audience ([Section 11.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-11.3), DPOP-120: "A server MUST NOT accept any DPoP proofs without the nonce claim when a DPoP nonce has been provided to the client."; [Section 9](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-9), DPOP-100, and "nonces will be only accepted by the server that issued them"), an empty `nonce` claim read as none ([Section 8.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-8.1), DPOP-094: `nonce = 1*NQCHAR`); the proof's `jti` refused before any store call where no record can hold it, empty, longer than `JtiReplayGuard.MaxJtiLength` UTF-16 code units, or holding a character of general category Cc or an unpaired surrogate ([Section 11.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-11.1), DPOP-115: "a server that is tracking jti values should reject DPoP proof JWTs with unnecessarily large jti values or store only a hash thereof"), and otherwise claimed in one atomic operation through `ConsultDpopProofJtiAsync`, a replay refused and a first use recorded until one tick past the later of the clock and the `iat` plus `DpopFreshnessWindow`, so the record covers every instant the proof is accepted at, the claim the entry for `23539b6c` below states (DPOP-114: "servers can store the jti value of each DPoP proof for the time window in which the respective DPoP proof JWT would be accepted to prevent multiple uses of the same DPoP proof"). Every `DPoP` challenge the composition answers is built by `DpopChallenge.BuildChallenge` with the resource's realm, scope, accepted algorithms and metadata URL, carries the exposure field, and a nonce challenge carries one fresh `DPoP-Nonce`. Every refusal of the presentation is a decision; the caller's own arguments throw: a `TargetUri` that is not an absolute http or https URI, or that carries userinfo, a query or a fragment ([Section 4.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-4.2): "htu: The HTTP target URI (Section 7.1 of [RFC9110]) of the request to which the JWT is attached, without query and fragment parts."; [RFC 9110 Section 4.2.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-4.2.4): "A sender MUST NOT generate the userinfo subcomponent (and its "@" delimiter) when an "http" or "https" URI reference is generated within a message as a target URI or field value."), a negative `DpopFreshnessWindow`, and a realm, scope, metadata URL or algorithm name no challenge can carry; an exception a slot raises passes to the caller and the request fails closed. The library's own resources (UserInfo and the OID4VCI Credential, Deferred Credential and Notification endpoints) run the composition through `CredentialEndpointDpopValidation`, with the replay guard's atomic claim keyed by the target URI as their `jti` record (DPOP-114's "In the context of the target URI") and the library validator's own tolerance, and their answers are unchanged. At an application's resource the `jti` is claimed once through `ResourceServerIntegration.ConsultDpopProofJtiAsync`, in the context of the target URI in its normalized form (the entry for `23539b6c` below); `JwsAccessTokenValidator.ValidateAsync`, the validation an application's resource calls first, refuses a token whose `cnf` carries no `jkt` it enforces (a certificate thumbprint `x5t#S256` alone, or a member it does not understand) as `UnsupportedConfirmationMethod`, and a token whose `jkt` is blank as `Malformed`, so a sender-constrained token is never served as a bearer token ([RFC 8705 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8705#section-3): "The protected resource MUST obtain, from its TLS implementation layer, the client certificate used for mutual TLS and MUST verify that the certificate matches the certificate associated with the access token."); only `jkt` is enforced, and a member beside a `jkt` is not read. Proven over the listener by `ResourceServerEndToEndTests.DpopBoundProtectedRequestWithMatchingProofReturns200` and `DpopBoundProofReplayedReturns401` (a bound token with its proof served, the replay refused), `DpopBoundTokenPresentedUnderBearerSchemeReturns401`, `AccessTokenConfirmedByAMethodOtherThanJktIsRefusedUnderBearerAsync` (an `x5t#S256`-only `cnf`), `ResourceServerRefusesAnUnboundTokenPresentedUnderDpopAsync`, `ResourceServerRefusesTwoDpopFieldsAsync`, `ResourceServerChallengesADpopPresentationWithoutAProofForItsNonceAsync`, `ResourceServerChallengesAProofWithoutItsNonceAsync`, `ResourceServerChallengesAProofWhoseNonceIsEmptyAsync`, `ResourceServerRefusesANonceIssuedForAnotherTargetUriAsync`, `ResourceServerRefusesAProofByAKeyOtherThanTheBoundOneAsync`, `DpopProofWithAFutureIatReplayedWithinItsAcceptanceWindowIsRefusedAsync`, `DpopProofReplayedAtTheLastInstantOfItsAcceptanceWindowIsRefusedAsync`, `DpopProofWhoseIatLiesOutsideTheResourcesFreshnessWindowIsRefusedAsync` (an `iat` 90 seconds ahead of the clock refused and one 60 seconds ahead served under a validation admitting 120; the same 90 and 60 seconds behind), `ResourceServerRefusesAProofWhoseJtiTheRecordCannotHoldAsync` (1025 characters; U+0001), `ResourceServerWithoutProofValidationAnswersABoundTokenWithAServerErrorAsync` (the proof and nonce slots unwired; the `jti` slots unwired), `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesAProofWhoseJtiIsLongerThanTheReplayGuardStoresAsync` and `Oid4VciDpopCredentialEndpointTests.ADpopProofWhoseJtiCarriesAControlCharacterIsRefusedBeforeAnyStoreCallAsync` (the one description at the library's resources), and in process by `DpopResourceAccessTests.DecideRefusesATargetUriNoProofCanNameAsTheCallersArgumentAsync` (a relative URI; a query; a fragment; ftp; userinfo), `DecideRefusesANegativeFreshnessWindowAsTheCallersArgumentAsync` and `DecideRefusesABearerPresentationWhereDpopBoundAccessTokensAreRequiredAsync` (the composition's own refusal of a validated `Bearer` presentation under the requirement, which a caller refusing it before validation never reaches over the wire).
  - **The requirement.** [RFC 9728 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-2) (PRM-005, PRM-007): "dpop_bound_access_tokens_required OPTIONAL. Boolean value specifying whether the protected resource always requires the use of DPoP-bound access tokens [RFC9449]." `AuthorizationServerIntegration.IsDpopBoundAccessTokenRequiredAtResources` makes UserInfo and the OID4VCI Credential, Deferred Credential and Notification endpoints a protection space requiring DPoP authentication ([RFC 9449 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.1): "Upon receipt of a request to a protected resource within the protection space requiring DPoP authentication, the server can respond with a challenge to the client to provide DPoP authentication information if the request does not include valid credentials or does not contain an access token sufficient for access."): under it a request without credentials, a malformed `Authorization` line and any `Bearer` presentation, whatever its token, are answered 401 with an empty body and the `DPoP` challenge alone, no `error` ([RFC 6750 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3.1): "If the request lacks any authentication information (e.g., the client was unaware that authentication is necessary or attempted using an unsupported authentication method), the resource server SHOULD NOT include an error code or other error information."; [RFC 9449 Section 7.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.2), DPOP-078), and a repeated `Authorization` field 400 `invalid_request` with the `DPoP` challenge alone carrying that error, each before any token is validated; a `DPoP` presentation is served or refused as without the flag. The integration refuses the flag at construction where no DPoP proof validation is wired, and admits it once one is, under traffic as every setting of the server. `DpopResourceAccess.DecideAsync` answers an application's resource the same way for a validated `Bearer` presentation where `DpopResourceRequest.IsDpopBoundAccessTokenRequired` is set; the earlier answers are the application's, as its reference consumer gives them. Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRequiringDpopBoundAccessTokensRefusesEveryBearerPresentationAsync` (an unbound token; a bound token; a value that is no token), `UserInfoRequiringDpopBoundAccessTokensChallengesARequestWithoutCredentialsWithTheDpopChallengeAloneAsync`, `UserInfoRequiringDpopBoundAccessTokensAnswersTwoBearerLinesWithTheDpopChallengeAloneAsync`, `UserInfoRequiringDpopBoundAccessTokensServesADpopPresentationAsync`, `Oid4VciDpopCredentialEndpointTests.CredentialEndpointRequiringDpopBoundAccessTokensRefusesABearerPresentationAsync`, `Oid4VciDeferredAndNotificationTests.ProtectedEndpointRequiringDpopBoundAccessTokensRefusesABearerPresentationAsync` (the Deferred Credential and Notification endpoints), `ResourceServerEndToEndTests.ResourceServerRequiringDpopRefusesABearerPresentationAsync`, `ResourceServerRequiringDpopRefusesABearerPresentationBeforeValidatingTheTokenAsync` (the token's key never resolved), `ResourceServerRequiringDpopChallengesARequestWithoutCredentialsWithTheDpopChallengeAloneAsync`, `ResourceServerAnswersAnAuthorizationFieldOnTwoLinesWithInvalidRequestAsync` (under the requirement the `DPoP` challenge alone; without it the challenge of the mechanism the lines attempt: `DPoP` alone, both, `Bearer` alone), `ResourceServerRequiringDpopReadsALowerCaseDpopSchemeAsTheDpopSchemeAsync`, `ResourceServerAnswersACredentialThatIsNoToken68ByItsSchemeAsync` (`required-dpop-credential`) and `ResourceServerReadsALowerCaseBearerSchemeAsTheBearerSchemeAsync` (the reference consumer reading a single `Authorization` line as the library's resources read it: `dpop <token>` served under the requirement, credentials outside token68 answered as a request without credentials, `bearer <token>` served without it), and in process by `LiveServerAlterationTests.DpopBoundAccessTokensRequiredWithoutProofValidationIsRefusedAtConstructionAsync`.
  - **The metadata.** [RFC 9728 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-2) (PRM-002): "The empty array [] can be used to indicate that no bearer methods are supported." A contribution stating an empty `bearer_methods_supported` is written as `[]` in the document and in the signed claims of [Section 2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-2.2), the one named exception to [Section 3.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-3.2)'s "Parameters with zero values MUST be omitted from the response." (PRM-009). (PRM-004) `dpop_signing_alg_values_supported` is the one list the server holds every proof to wherever it validates DPoP proofs; the discovery document and the resources' `algs` name the identifiers of that list a proof can be accepted under, and the protected resource metadata documents the same identifiers where the server validates DPoP proofs (the entry for `bd54b3f7` below). With `IsDpopBoundAccessTokenRequiredAtResources` set, the document of each of the library's resources states `dpop_bound_access_tokens_required` true and `bearer_methods_supported` `[]`, and the issuer's document states its contribution as given (the entry for `bd54b3f7` below). Proven over the listener by `ProtectedResourceMetadataTests.DocumentPublishesAnEmptyBearerMethodsSupportedAsync`, `SignedMetadataCarriesTheContributedBearerMethodsUnderTheResourcesRequirementAsync`, `DocumentPublishesTheDpopAlgorithmsTheServerAcceptsAsync` (the default list of twelve names; a narrowed list), `IssuerDocumentStatesOnlyItsContributionUnderTheResourcesRequirementAsync` (a contribution stating neither member: the document states neither) and `IssuerDocumentServesItsContributionUnderTheResourcesRequirementAsync` (`false`; a bearer method: each written as stated).
  - **Deviations, recorded.** `JwsAccessTokenValidator` refuses a `cnf` whose members it does not understand, where [RFC 7800 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7800#section-3.1) reads "Specific applications of JWTs will require implementations to understand and process some confirmation members in particular ways." and, in their absence, "all confirmation members that are not understood by implementations MUST be ignored.": the library's fail-closed choice, a token carrying a confirmation it cannot enforce refused rather than served unbound; a dedicated failure reason, `UnsupportedConfirmationMethod`, names the refusal, a blank `jkt` is refused as `Malformed`, and a `cnf` carrying `jkt` beside another member is enforced by the `jkt` alone (the entry for `23539b6c` below).
  - **Open.** None; the `DPoP` and `Bearer` challenges of the library's resources name their metadata document where it is served and the `Bearer` challenges carry the realm (the entry for `bd54b3f7` below).

- **Answer the open DPoP clauses (`23539b6c`).** An application's resource claims a proof's `jti` once, in one atomic operation, in the context of the target URI in the one normalized form the `htu` comparison, the replay record and the application's slot all read; a `jti` store that cannot record the proof is a server error, with one text across the library's endpoints; a `cnf` is refused by the rule that names its fault, a blank `jkt` as malformed and a confirmation carrying no `jkt` as an unsupported method; a `Bearer` or `DPoP` credential outside token68 is answered 400 `invalid_request` under the named scheme's challenge; the challenge and the authorization server's metadata signal only the algorithms a proof can be accepted under; and the introspection response conveys a DPoP-bound token's binding.
  - **The `jti` claim at an application's resource.** [RFC 9449 Section 11.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-11.1) (DPOP-114): "In the context of the target URI, servers can store the jti value of each DPoP proof for the time window in which the respective DPoP proof JWT would be accepted to prevent multiple uses of the same DPoP proof. HTTP requests to the same URI for which the jti value has been seen before would be declined." `ResourceServerIntegration.ConsultDpopProofJtiAsync`, a `ConsultDpopProofJtiDelegate`, is the one slot through which an application's resource records a proof's `jti`: one atomic claim of the `jti` in the context of the target URI. `DpopResourceAccess.DecideAsync` calls it once per validated proof, after the rule that refuses a `jti` no record can hold, handing it the `jti`, the target URI in the normalized form of the next bullet and a `recordUntil` one tick past the proof's acceptance window, and reads the answer as a `JtiReplayOutcome`: `FirstUse` continues the decision; `Replayed` is refused `invalid_dpop_proof` with the description "The DPoP proof jti has been presented previously."; `Unacceptable` is refused `invalid_dpop_proof` with the description of a `jti` no record can hold; `StoreUnavailable` is 500 `server_error` (the third bullet below); any other value, the fail-closed default arm, is 500 `server_error` too, so a presentation is never served by an outcome the checks do not name. The in-memory default `InMemoryDpopReplayCache.ConsultAsync` keys its record by the target URI and the `jti` and claims with one atomic operation: an expired record is taken over by the new claim, of concurrent presentations of one proof at one target URI one is served and the others are refused as replays, and the same `jti` in a proof for another target URI is another proof's. The claim is the whole of the surface: a seen query and a persist call (`IsDpopProofJtiSeenDelegate`, `PersistDpopProofJtiDelegate`, `ResourceServerIntegration.IsDpopProofJtiSeenAsync` and `PersistDpopProofJtiAsync`, `InMemoryDpopReplayCache.IsSeenAsync` and `PersistAsync`) are not part of it. Proven over the listener by `ResourceServerEndToEndTests.DpopProofJtiRecordedAtOneTargetUriIsServedAtAnotherAsync` (the same `jti` in a fresh proof at a second protected path served), `DpopBoundProofReplayedReturns401`, `DpopProofWithAFutureIatReplayedWithinItsAcceptanceWindowIsRefusedAsync` and `DpopProofReplayedAtTheLastInstantOfItsAcceptanceWindowIsRefusedAsync` (the replay refused), `OneProofPresentedConcurrentlyIsServedOnceAsync` (eight concurrent presentations of one proof: one served), `DpopProofJtiPresentedAtTwoSpellingsOfOneTargetUriIsServedOnceAsync` and `OneUnchangedProofPresentedAtTwoSpellingsOfOneTargetUriIsServedOnceAsync` (two spellings of one target: one record, and one unchanged proof served once), `ResourceServerWithoutProofValidationAnswersABoundTokenWithAServerErrorAsync` (`jti-record-unwired`: the slot unwired), `ResourceServerAnswersAJtiStoreRecordingNoFirstUseWithAServerErrorAsync` (`unnamed-outcome`: an outcome the checks do not name, the default arm) and `ResourceServerRefusesAProofWhoseJtiItsStoreCannotHoldAsync` (the slot answering `Unacceptable`), and in process by `InMemoryDpopReplayCacheTests.TwoSpellingsOfOneTargetUriClaimOneRecordAsync` and `ClaimOfAnExpiredPairReplacesItsRecordAsync` (an expired record taken over by the claim), and `DpopResourceAccessTests.DecideClaimsAProofJtiAtTheNormalizedTargetUriAsync`.
  - **The target URI's one form.** [RFC 9449 Section 4.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-4.3) (DPOP-036): "To reduce the likelihood of false negatives, servers SHOULD employ syntax-based normalization (Section 6.2.2 of [RFC3986]) and scheme-based normalization (Section 6.2.3 of [RFC3986]) before comparing the htu claim." [RFC 3986 Section 6.2.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-6.2.2.1): "the hexadecimal digits within a percent-encoding triplet (e.g., "%3a" versus "%3A") are case-insensitive and therefore should be normalized to use uppercase letters for the digits A-F."; [Section 6.2.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-6.2.2.2): "These URIs should be normalized by decoding any percent-encoded octet that corresponds to an unreserved character, as described in Section 2.3."; [Section 6.2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-6.2.3): "In general, a URI that uses the generic syntax for authority with an empty path should be normalized to a path of "/". Likewise, an explicit ":port", for which the port is empty or the default for the scheme, is equivalent to one where the port and its ":" delimiter are elided and thus should be removed by scheme-based normalization." `DpopProofValidator.NormalizeTargetUri` writes one form, and the `htu` comparison, the replay guard's key, the application's slot (`ConsultDpopProofJtiDelegate`'s `targetUri`) and the in-memory cache's key all read it; the delegate's documentation states it once: the scheme and host in lower case, a default port dropped, dot segments removed ([Section 6.2.2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-6.2.2.3)), an empty path given `/`, no userinfo, query or fragment, only the percent-encodings of unreserved characters decoded and the hexadecimal digits of every other percent-encoding in upper case, the host kept as written. A space, a control character, an octet outside US-ASCII and every reserved character in the path stay percent-encoded. The form is idempotent and safe to parse again: `Uri.OriginalString` of the `Uri` handed to the slot carries exactly that text, and the text normalized again is itself, so one target is one string whatever the application's spelling and `/a%20` and `/a` are two targets. Proven in process by `DpopProofValidatorTests.NormalizeTargetUriAnswersOneReparseSafeFormOfATarget` (nineteen cases: an encoded space ending the path, two, and alone; `%41`; `%7e%5F`; `%E2%82%AC` in upper and lower case; `%7B`; dot segments; host case with `:443`; `%2F` and `%2f`; an empty path; a query; a fragment; `[::1]:8443`; a Unicode host and its punycode; `http` with `:80`; each asserting the expected text, that normalizing again answers it and that parsing it answers it), `InMemoryDpopReplayCacheTests.TwoSpellingsOfOneTargetUriClaimOneRecordAsync` and `ATargetEndingInAnEncodedSpaceAndOneWithoutClaimTwoRecordsAsync`, `DpopResourceAccessTests.DecideClaimsAProofJtiAtTheNormalizedTargetUriAsync` and `DecideAndADirectClaimNameATargetEndingInAnEncodedSpaceByOneRecordAsync` (the composition and a direct claim at another spelling name one record), and over the listener by `ResourceServerEndToEndTests.DpopProofJtiPresentedAtTwoSpellingsOfOneTargetUriIsServedOnceAsync` (the slot handed the same normalized target at both spellings) and `OneUnchangedProofPresentedAtTwoSpellingsOfOneTargetUriIsServedOnceAsync`.
  - **A `jti` store that cannot record the proof.** [RFC 9449 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.1) (DPOP-065): "The resource server MUST NOT grant access to the resource unless all checks are successful." and [RFC 9110 Section 15.6.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-15.6.1): "The 500 (Internal Server Error) status code indicates that the server encountered an unexpected condition that prevented it from fulfilling the request." A proof whose `jti` the replay defense cannot record is not served: 500 `server_error` with no challenge. The library's resources and the token endpoint answer one text for every cause of the replay defense's failure (no replay store wired where one is required, a store with no atomic claim, a store that cannot resolve what it recorded), "The DPoP proof jti replay defense could not record the proof.", and an application's resource answers its own store's text, "The DPoP proof jti store could not record the proof.". The token endpoint describes a `jti` no record can hold as the resources do, "DPoP proof jti is empty, too long or carries a character the replay guard cannot store.": one refusal, one description. Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoAnswersAReplayStoreThatCannotRecordTheProofWithAServerErrorAsync`, `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointAnswersAReplayStoreThatCannotRecordTheProofWithAServerErrorAsync` (the `client_credentials` grant), `DpopEndToEndTests.DpopTokenEndpointFailsClosedWhenStoreCannotProveItself` (the code grant), `ResourceServerEndToEndTests.ResourceServerAnswersAJtiStoreRecordingNoFirstUseWithAServerErrorAsync` (`store-cannot-record`: the application's text), `DpopBoundRefreshTests.ADpopProofAtRefreshWhoseJtiCarriesAControlCharacterIsRefusedBeforeAnyStoreCallAsync` and `Oid4VciPreAuthorizedCodeGrantTests.ADpopProofWhoseJtiCarriesAControlCharacterIsRefusedBeforeAnyStoreCall` (the token endpoint's description of a `jti` no record can hold).
  - **The confirmation claim.** [RFC 9449 Section 6.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-6.1) (DPOP-055): "jkt: JWK SHA-256 Thumbprint confirmation method. The value of the jkt member MUST be the base64url encoding (as defined in [RFC7515]) of the JWK SHA-256 Thumbprint (according to [RFC7638]) of the DPoP public key (in JWK format) to which the access token is bound."; [RFC 7800 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7800#section-3.1): "all confirmation members that are not understood by implementations MUST be ignored."; [RFC 8705 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8705#section-3): "The protected resource MUST obtain, from its TLS implementation layer, the client certificate used for mutual TLS and MUST verify that the certificate matches the certificate associated with the access token." A `cnf` whose `jkt` is blank, empty or white space, binds the token to no key and is a malformed token, at `JwsAccessTokenValidator.ValidateAsync` (`JwsAccessTokenValidationFailureReason.Malformed`) and at the library's resources under one rule, 401 `invalid_token` in the presentation's scheme (the `Bearer` challenge for a `Bearer` presentation, the `DPoP` challenge for a `DPoP` presentation). A `cnf` that is a well-formed object carrying no `jkt` the validator enforces (a certificate thumbprint `x5t#S256` alone, a key identifier alone, any member it does not understand) is `JwsAccessTokenValidationFailureReason.UnsupportedConfirmationMethod`; the wire answer is the same 401 `invalid_token`, and the refusal is the library's fail-closed reading against RFC 7800 Section 3.1 (RFC 8705 Section 3), recorded below. A `cnf` carrying a non-blank `jkt` beside another member is enforced by the `jkt` alone. Proven in process by `JwsAccessTokenValidatorTests.ValidatorRefusesACnfWhoseJktIsBlankAsMalformedAsync` (an empty `jkt`; three spaces) and `ValidatorRefusesACnfWithoutJktAsAnUnsupportedConfirmationMethodAsync` (a key identifier alone; a certificate thumbprint alone), and over the listener by `ResourceServerEndToEndTests.AccessTokenWhoseCnfCarriesABlankJktIsRefusedAsMalformedAsync` (a `DPoP` and a `Bearer` presentation), `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesATokenWhoseCnfCarriesABlankJktAsync` (`DPoP`, `Bearer`) and `ResourceServerEndToEndTests.AccessTokenConfirmedByAMethodOtherThanJktIsRefusedUnderBearerAsync` (an `x5t#S256`-only `cnf`: the answer unchanged).
  - **A credential outside token68.** [RFC 9449 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.1), Figure 12 (DPOP-063): `credentials = "DPoP" 1*SP token68`; [Section 7.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.2), rule 2 (DPOP-079): "If the mechanism used to attempt authentication could be established unambiguously, then the corresponding challenge SHOULD be used to deliver error information (Figure 18)."; [RFC 6750 Section 2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-2.1): "b64token    = 1*( ALPHA / DIGIT / "-" / "." / "_" / "~" / "+" / "/" ) *"="" and "credentials = "Bearer" 1*SP b64token"; [Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3.1), `invalid_request`: "The resource server SHOULD respond with the HTTP 400 (Bad Request) status code." One `Authorization` line whose scheme is `Bearer` or `DPoP` (compared ASCII case-insensitively) and whose credentials are no token68 is answered 400 `invalid_request` with the named scheme's challenge alone and the body `{"error":"invalid_request","error_description":"The Authorization header credentials are not a token68."}`, at UserInfo and the OID4VCI Credential, Deferred Credential and Notification endpoints (`CredentialEndpointDpopValidation.ReadPresentationAsync`, the one place) and at the reference consumer of the test tree: the `DPoP` challenge `DPoP realm="<issuer>", error="invalid_request", scope="openid", algs="..."` (no `scope` at the OID4VCI resources) with the exposure field, the `Bearer` challenge `Bearer realm="<issuer>", error="invalid_request"` alone with none; where no issuer resolves, the same answer with the challenge without `realm`. Under the DPoP requirement (`IsDpopBoundAccessTokenRequiredAtResources`) a `DPoP` credential outside token68 is answered so with the `DPoP` challenge, and a `Bearer` credential of any shape stays 401 with an empty body and the `DPoP` challenge alone, no `error` (RFC 6750 Section 3.1's unsupported method). A scheme alone, a scheme other than the two and a request without an `Authorization` field are requests without credentials, 401 with both challenges and no `error`. Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesADpopCredentialOutsideToken68Async` (a space; a double quote; a comma), `UserInfoRefusesABearerCredentialOutsideToken68Async` (a space; a double quote; a comma; an auth-param, `a=b`), `UserInfoRequiringDpopBoundAccessTokensAnswersACredentialOutsideToken68ByItsSchemeAsync` (`DPoP`: 400; `Bearer`: 401), `UserInfoAnswersACredentialOutsideToken68WhereNoIssuerResolvesAsync` (both schemes, an issuer that does not resolve and a resolver answering nothing), `UserInfoChallengesARequestWithoutUsableCredentialsAsync` (no field; `Basic`; `Bearer` alone; `DPoP` alone), `Oid4VciDpopCredentialEndpointTests.CredentialEndpointAnswersACredentialOutsideToken68WithInvalidRequestAsync` (`DPoP`, `Bearer`), `Oid4VciDeferredAndNotificationTests.ProtectedEndpointAnswersACredentialOutsideToken68WithInvalidRequestAsync` (the Deferred Credential and Notification endpoints), `ResourceServerEndToEndTests.ResourceServerAnswersACredentialThatIsNoToken68ByItsSchemeAsync` (`required-dpop-credential`, `bearer-credential`, `required-bearer-credential`) and `ResourceServerAnswersAnAuthorizationFieldCarryingNoAccessTokenAsMissingCredentialsAsync` (`Basic`; `DPoP` alone; `Bearer` alone; no field).
  - **The advertised algorithms.** [RFC 9449 Section 5.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-5.1) (DPOP-049): "dpop_signing_alg_values_supported: A JSON array containing a list of the JWS alg values (from the [IANA.JOSE.ALGS] registry) supported by the authorization server for DPoP proof JWTs."; [Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.1) (DPOP-073): "An algs parameter SHOULD be included to signal to the client the JWS algorithms that are acceptable for the DPoP proof JWT."; [Section 11.6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-11.6) (DPOP-122): "Implementers MUST ensure that only asymmetric digital signature algorithms (such as ES256) that are deemed secure can be used for signing DPoP proofs." and (DPOP-123): "In particular, the algorithm none MUST NOT be allowed." The `algs` of a `DPoP` challenge (of the library's resources and of an application's resource) and the authorization server's `dpop_signing_alg_values_supported` carry only the identifiers a proof can be accepted under (`DpopProofValidator.AcceptableProofAlgorithms`: the host's list, in its order, of the asymmetric JWS signature identifiers `DpopProofValidator.IsDpopProofAlg` accepts), the parameter absent from the challenge and the member absent from the document when none remains, whatever the host lists. The list a proof is held to is the host's own, unfiltered, so a proof under `HS256` or `none` is refused even where the host lists it: `HS256` and `none` over a signature `invalid_dpop_proof` `InvalidAlg`, `none` with an empty signature part as malformed by the structural parse of [Section 4.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-4.3), check 2, before its algorithm is read. [Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-2) (DPOP-002): "DPoP is not, however, a substitute for a secure transport and MUST always be used in conjunction with HTTPS." The client credentials client with a DPoP key and the wallet never dial a plain-HTTP listener: the refusal precedes the dial. Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesAProofUnderNoAsymmetricSignatureAlgorithmTheHostListsAsync` and `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRefusesAProofUnderNoAsymmetricSignatureAlgorithmTheHostListsAsync` (`mac-algorithm-proof`: the host lists `ES256 HS256`; `none-algorithm-proof-with-a-signature`: the host lists `ES256 none`; `none-algorithm-proof`: the host lists `ES256 HS256`; at UserInfo each challenge signals `algs="ES256"`), `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesAProofUnderAMacAlgorithmAsync`, `UserInfoRefusesAProofWhoseAlgIsNoneAsync`, `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.TokenEndpointRefusesAProofUnderAMacAlgorithmAsync` and `TokenEndpointRefusesAProofWhoseAlgIsNoneAsync`, `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoChallengeSignalsNoAlgorithmWhereTheHostListsNoneAloneAsync` (a challenge without `algs`), `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.DiscoveryPublishesOnlyTheAsymmetricDpopAlgorithmsTheHostListsAsync` (`ES256 beside HS256` and `ES256 beside none` publish `["ES256"]`; `none alone` publishes no member), `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientNeverDialsAPlainHttpTokenEndpointListeningOnTheLoopbackAsync` and `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.WalletNeverDialsAPlainHttpResourceListeningOnTheLoopbackAsync` (a listener on the loopback receives no connection).
  - **The introspection response.** [RFC 9449 Section 6.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-6.2) (DPOP-056): "For a DPoP-bound access token, the hash of the public key to which the token is bound is conveyed to the protected resource as metainformation in a token introspection response. The hash is conveyed using the same cnf content with jkt member structure as the JWK Thumbprint confirmation method, described in Section 6.1, as a top-level member of the introspection response JSON." and (DPOP-058): "If the token_type member is included in the introspection response, it MUST contain the value DPoP."; [RFC 7662 Section 2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7662#section-2.2): "Specific implementations MAY extend this structure with their own service-specific response names as top-level members of this JSON object." and, of an inactive token, "the authorization server SHOULD NOT include any additional information about an inactive token, including why the token is inactive."; [RFC 9701 Section 5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9701#section-5): "If the access token is invalid, expired, revoked, or not intended for the calling resource server (audience), the authorization server MUST set the value of the active member in the token_introspection claim to false and MUST NOT include other members." `TokenIntrospectionResult.Confirmation` is written as the top-level `cnf` with `jkt`, by the writer that writes the `cnf` of an issued token (`CnfClaimContributor.BuildConfirmationMember`), and the `token_type` is `DPoP`, in the plain answer and in the RFC 9701 signed form, which is built through the same members. A `token_type` the application states that contradicts the confirmation (`Bearer` beside a confirmation; `DPoP` without one) is answered 500 `server_error` with no introspection member. A confirmation that is no JWK SHA-256 Thumbprint spelling is answered 500 `server_error` before the plain and the signed form is written, and so is an extension member (`TokenIntrospectionResult.AdditionalClaims`) that names a member the answer writes from typed state, before either form. A confirmed token whose answer states a case variant of `DPoP` as its `token_type` is written as `DPoP`, the text's literal. An inactive answer is exactly `{"active":false}` in both forms whatever the application's answer adds. A token bound to no key carries no `cnf`. Proven over the listener by `TokenIntrospectionServerTests.IntrospectionConveysTheJwkThumbprintBindingOfADpopBoundTokenAsync` (no token type stated; `DPoP` stated; `dpop` stated), `IntrospectionOfATokenBoundToNoKeyCarriesNoConfirmationAsync`, `IntrospectionAnswersATokenTypeContradictingTheConfirmationAsAServerErrorAsync` (`bearer-beside-a-confirmation`, `dpop-without-a-confirmation`), `IntrospectionAnswersAConfirmationWithoutAThumbprintSpellingAsAServerErrorAsync` (an empty thumbprint; three spaces; `not-a-thumbprint`; 42 characters), `IntrospectionAnswersAnExtensionMemberNamingAWrittenMemberAsAServerErrorAsync` (`token-type-extension`, `cnf-extension`), `IntrospectionOfAnInactiveTokenCarriesNothingTheAnswerAddsAsync`, `TokenIntrospectionJwtResponseTests.SignedResponseConveysTheJwkThumbprintBindingInsideTheClaimAsync`, `SignedResponseAnswersAConfirmationWithoutAThumbprintSpellingAsAServerErrorAsync`, `SignedResponseAnswersAnExtensionMemberNamingAWrittenMemberAsAServerErrorAsync` and `SignedResponseOfAnInactiveTokenCarriesNothingTheAnswerAddsAsync` (the signed form), and `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ADpopBoundAccessTokenIsIntrospectedWithTheThumbprintOfItsProofKeyAsync` (a token issued DPoP-bound at the token endpoint with a proof, introspected, its `jkt` the proof key's thumbprint).
  - **Deviation, recorded.** `JwsAccessTokenValidator` refuses a `cnf` whose members it does not understand as `UnsupportedConfirmationMethod`, where [RFC 7800 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7800#section-3.1) reads "all confirmation members that are not understood by implementations MUST be ignored.": the library's fail-closed choice, a token carrying a confirmation it cannot enforce refused rather than served unbound; only `jkt` is enforced.
  - **Open.** The resource server's local check of the binding an introspection response conveys, and the introspection client that reads the response ([RFC 9449 Section 6.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-6.2), DPOP-057), are the planned change D2i; the library's default nonce validation binds a nonce's audience by `Uri.OriginalString` while the `htu` comparison and the `jti` record read the normalized form, so an application composing its target URI from the request's spelling has a nonce issued at one spelling refused at another spelling of one target, a recoverable `use_dpop_nonce` challenge.

- **Hold the credential offer to its grammar (`85b22933`, `40f45edc`).** The Credential Offer reader refuses a present member of a JSON type its definition does not admit, a `credential_configuration_ids` that is no non-empty array of unique, non-empty strings and a `tx_code.length` that is no Integer from 0 to 2147483647; `tx_code.description` is counted in Unicode scalar values; a `credential_offer_uri` outside the `https` scheme is refused, by the Wallet before any request is sent and by the by-reference deep link as the composer's own argument; the composed `openid_credential` authorization details are written through the JSON appender with every member escaped; and the issuance clauses of Sections 4.1, 5.1, 6.1, 6.2 and 7 of OID4VCI 1.0 are held over the listener; all under unchanged signatures.
  - **A present member of a JSON type its definition does not admit.** [OID4VCI 1.0 Section 4.1.1](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-4.1.1): "grants: OPTIONAL. Object indicating to the Wallet the Grant Types the Credential Issuer's Authorization Server is prepared to process for this Credential Offer. Every grant is represented by a name/value pair. The name is the Grant Type identifier; the value is an object that contains parameters either determining the way the Wallet MUST use the particular grant and/or parameters the Wallet MUST send with the respective request(s).", "tx_code: OPTIONAL. Object indicating that a Transaction Code is required if present, even if empty." (VCI-035), "input_mode : OPTIONAL. String specifying the input character set.", "description: OPTIONAL. String containing guidance for the Holder of the Wallet on how to obtain the Transaction Code, e.g., describing over which communication channel it is delivered.", "authorization_server: OPTIONAL string that the Wallet can use to identify the Authorization Server to use with this grant type when authorization_servers parameter in the Credential Issuer metadata has multiple entries." and "issuer_state: OPTIONAL. String value created by the Credential Issuer and opaque to the Wallet that is used to bind the subsequent Authorization Request with a context set up during previous process steps." The offer reader refuses a present `grants`, or a Grant Type's value, that is no object, a present `tx_code` that is no object (a string, or `null`), and a present `input_mode`, `description`, `authorization_server` (in either grant block) or `issuer_state` that is no string, each with a description that names the member and the rule it breaks and never reading the member as absent: `CredentialOfferSerializer.TryParse` names the violated rule, which `Oid4VciWalletClient.FetchCredentialOfferAsync` answers as `Oid4VciRequestFailureKind.MalformedResponse` of status 200, and `FromJson` raises `ArgumentException`. An absent member is told from a present one after the document has passed the well-formedness gate, so a member of the wrong type is a refusal and an absent member stays absent. Proven over the listener by `Oid4VciWalletOfferTests.ByReferenceOfferCarryingAMemberOfAnotherJsonTypeIsRefusedAsync` (ten rows: `tx_code` as a string and as `null`; `grants` as a string; the Pre-Authorized Code grant's value as a string; the `authorization_code` grant's value as an array; `input_mode` as a number; `description` as a number; `authorization_server` as a boolean in the Pre-Authorized Code grant and in the `authorization_code` grant; `issuer_state` as a number).
  - **The `tx_code` object, even if empty.** [OID4VCI 1.0 Section 4.1.1](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-4.1.1): "tx_code: OPTIONAL. Object indicating that a Transaction Code is required if present, even if empty." (VCI-035) and "If no length, description, or input_mode is given, this object MAY be empty." (VCI-038); "input_mode : OPTIONAL. String specifying the input character set. Possible values are numeric (only digits) and text (any characters). The default is numeric." (VCI-039); "length: OPTIONAL. Integer specifying the length of the Transaction Code. This helps the Wallet to render the input screen and improve the user experience." (VCI-040); "The length of the string MUST NOT exceed 300 characters." (VCI-042, of `description`); [RFC 8259 Section 1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8259#section-1): "A string is a sequence of zero or more Unicode characters [UNICODE]." A `tx_code` object, empty or giving `input_mode`, `length` or `description`, is read as the requirement (`TxCodeRequirement`, never `null` for a present object); an object giving `input_mode` `text` reads that input character set and an object giving none reads none. `length` is read as an Integer from 0 to 2147483647: a present negative Integer, an Integer above 2147483647, a string, a fraction or `null` is the violated rule, with the description "the offer carries a length that is no Integer from 0 to 2147483647", answered `MalformedResponse` by the Wallet and `ArgumentException` by `FromJson`, never an arithmetic overflow; a `length` of 0 or 2147483647 is read as that hint. `CredentialOfferSerializer.ToJson` counts `description` in Unicode scalar values, as RFC 8259 Section 1 counts the characters of a string, so a description of 300 characters outside the Basic Multilingual Plane (600 UTF-16 code units) composes and one of 301 is refused with an `ArgumentException` naming `description`. Proven over the listener by `Oid4VciWalletOfferTests.ByReferenceOfferIgnoresUnrecognizedParametersOverTheWireAsync` (`{}`, read as the requirement with no `input_mode`; `{"input_mode":"text"}`, read with `text`), `ByReferenceOfferRefusalsOverTheWireAsync` (`length` 9999999999, -1, 2147483648, "6", 1.5 and `null`) and `ByReferenceOfferTransactionCodeLengthIsReadAsTheHintAsync` (2147483647; 0), and in process by `Oid4VciWalletOfferTests.EmptyTxCodeObjectRecreatesANonNullRequirement` and `Oid4VciCredentialOfferTests.TxCodeDescriptionIsCountedInCharacters` (300 and 301 characters of U+1F600).
  - **`credential_configuration_ids`.** [OID4VCI 1.0 Section 4.1.1](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-4.1.1) (VCI-025): "credential_configuration_ids: REQUIRED. A non-empty array of unique strings that each identify one of the keys in the name/value pairs stored in the credential_configurations_supported Credential Issuer metadata." An offer whose `credential_configuration_ids` is empty, holds an empty entry or a repeated one, holds an entry that is no string, or is no array is refused with a description that states the rule the value breaks (the entries are unique; every entry is a non-empty string; "the offer carries a value that is no array of strings"), and an offer without the member is refused with the description of absence ("a REQUIRED non-empty array; the offer JSON carries none"), so the text names the cause the document has. No description repeats an identifier the offer carries. `CredentialOfferSerializer.ToJson` raises `ArgumentException` for an empty array and for a repeated entry, from the one rule the reader answers with. Proven over the listener by `Oid4VciWalletOfferTests.ByReferenceOfferWithARepeatedOrEmptyConfigurationIdentifierIsRefusedAsync` (a repeated entry; an empty entry; an entry that is a number; a value that is a string), and in process by `Oid4VciCredentialOfferTests.CredentialConfigurationIdsEmptyIsRejectedAndNonEmptyIsAccepted` and `CredentialConfigurationIdsWithDuplicateEntriesIsRejected`.
  - **The by-reference offer, read over a published document.** [OID4VCI 1.0 Section 4.1.3](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-4.1.3) (VCI-044): "Upon receipt of the credential_offer_uri, the Wallet MUST send an HTTP GET request to the URI to retrieve the referenced Credential Offer Object, unless it is already cached, and parse it to recreate the Credential Offer parameters."; (VCI-046): "The response from the Credential Issuer that contains a Credential Offer Object MUST use the media type application/json."; (VCI-047, VCI-048): "When retrieving the Credential Offer from the Credential Offer URL, the application/json media type MUST be used. The Credential Offer cannot be signed and MUST NOT use application/jwt with "alg": "none"."; [Section 4.1.1](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-4.1.1) (VCI-029): "Additional Credential Offer parameters MAY be defined and used. The Wallet MUST ignore any unrecognized parameters."; [Section 4.1](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-4.1) (VCI-022, VCI-023): "credential_offer_uri: String that is a URL using the https scheme referencing a resource containing a JSON object with the Credential Offer parameters." `Oid4VciWalletClient.FetchCredentialOfferAsync`, which `AcceptCredentialOfferAsync` calls for a by-reference link, answers `MalformedResponse` of status 200, and no offer, for a response whose media type is not `application/json` (an HTML page; an unsigned `application/jwt` with `alg` `none`), with a description that names `application/json`, for a body that is no JSON object, with a description that names well-formed JSON, and for an offer without `credential_issuer`; an unrecognized top-level member and an unrecognized member of a grant are ignored and the recognized parameters beside them are read. A scanned link whose `credential_offer_uri` is outside the `https` scheme is refused before any request is sent: under the secure default outbound policy `AcceptCredentialOfferAsync` answers `Oid4VciRequestFailureKind.OutboundPolicyDenied` with no status code and the Wallet's transport is never called. `CredentialOfferSerializer.ToByReferenceDeepLink` composes the link only for an absolute `https` URL and refuses an `http` URL and a relative reference with an `ArgumentException` naming `credential_offer_uri`, as the composer's own argument. Proven over the listener by `Oid4VciWalletOfferTests.ByReferenceOfferRefusalsOverTheWireAsync` (`text/html`; `application/jwt`; a body that is no JSON object; an offer without `credential_issuer`) and `ByReferenceOfferIgnoresUnrecognizedParametersOverTheWireAsync`, and in process by `Oid4VciWalletOfferTests.HttpCredentialOfferUriIsRefusedBeforeAnyDialAsync` (a scanned link crosses no wire: the transport spy is never called), `UnrecognizedTopLevelAndGrantParametersAreIgnored` (`length` 6 and `input_mode` `numeric` read beside the unknown members) and `Oid4VciCredentialOfferTests.ByReferenceDeepLinkRefusesAUriOtherThanHttps` (`http://server.example.com/o`; `o/1`; `https://server.example.com/o` composes).
  - **The composed `openid_credential` authorization details.** [OID4VCI 1.0 Section 5.1.1](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-5.1.1) (VCI-053): "The request parameter authorization_details defined in Section 2 of [RFC9396] MUST be used to convey the details about the Credentials the Wallet wants to obtain."; [RFC 8259 Section 7](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8259#section-7): "All Unicode characters may be placed within the quotation marks, except for the characters that MUST be escaped: quotation mark, reverse solidus, and the control characters (U+0000 through U+001F)."; [Section 8.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8259#section-8.2): "However, the ABNF in this specification allows member names and string values to contain bit sequences that cannot encode Unicode characters; for example, "\uDEAD" (a single unpaired UTF-16 surrogate)."; [RFC 9126 Section 2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9126#section-2.1): "The client constructs the message body of an HTTP "POST" request with parameters formatted with "x-www-form-urlencoded" using a character encoding of UTF-8, as described in Appendix B of [RFC6749]."; [RFC 6749 Appendix B](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#appendix-B): "names and values MUST be encoded using the UTF-8 character encoding scheme [RFC3629] first; the resulting octet sequence then needs to be further encoded using the escaping rules defined in [W3C.REC-html401-19991224]." and "When parsing data from a payload using this media type, the names and values resulting from reversing the name/value encoding consequently need to be treated as octet sequences, to be decoded using the UTF-8 character encoding scheme." `CredentialAuthorizationDetailComposition.Compose` writes the details through the JSON appender (`JsonAppender`), as the Credential Request body is written, every member escaped, so a configuration identifier carrying a quotation mark, a reverse solidus or a control character, and a `locations` entry carrying a quotation mark, reach the credential decision seam as composed. An identifier holding a percent sign reaches the seam as itself: JSON leaves a percent sign as it is, the pushed request's form encoding carries it as an octet of the value and the server reverses that encoding once. An identifier holding an unpaired surrogate (a lone high surrogate, a lone low surrogate, or a high surrogate at the identifier's end) is refused as the caller's own argument, an `ArgumentException` with the parameter `credentialConfigurationId`, through the shared `TextHygiene` classifier. Proven over the listener by `Oid4VciAuthorizationCodeIssuanceTests.ComposedAuthorizationDetailsCarryAQuotedConfigurationIdentifierAsync` (`pid"1`; `pid\1`; `pid` U+001F `1`), `ComposedAuthorizationDetailsCarryAPercentSignAsItselfAsync` (`pid%411`) and `ComposedAuthorizationDetailsCarryAQuotedLocationAsync` (`https://credential-issuer.example.com/tenant"1`), and in process by `ComposeRefusesAConfigurationIdentifierHoldingAnUnpairedSurrogate` (a high surrogate before `1`; a low surrogate before `1`; a high surrogate at the end).
  - **The issuance clauses, pinned over the listener.** The Nonce Endpoint: [OID4VCI 1.0 Section 7.1](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-7.1) (VCI-103): "The Nonce Endpoint is not a protected resource, meaning the Wallet does not need to supply an access token to access it."; [Section 7.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-7.2) (VCI-105): "c_nonce: REQUIRED. String containing a challenge to be used when creating a proof of possession of the key" and (VCI-107): "Due to the temporal nature of the c_nonce value, the Credential Issuer MUST make the response uncacheable by adding a Cache-Control header field including the value no-store."; a POST with an empty body and no `Authorization` field is answered 200 `application/json` with the application's `c_nonce` as the one top-level member, one `Cache-Control: no-store` field and no `WWW-Authenticate` challenge. The Credential Offer Endpoint: the stored offer is served 200 `application/json`, unsigned, held to the grammar of the bullets above (Sections 4.1.1 and 4.1.3), carrying the `tx_code` object, empty, its stored Pre-Authorized Code grant requires, or the `issuer_state` of its `authorization_code` grant. The `openid_credential` shapes: [Section 5.1.1](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-5.1.1) (VCI-057): "Additional authorization_details data fields MAY be defined and used when the type value is openid_credential. Note that this effectively defines an authorization details type that is never considered invalid due to unknown fields." and (VCI-059): "Note: Applications MAY combine authorization details of type openid_credential with any other authorization details types in an Authorization Request."; [RFC 9396 Section 5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9396#section-5): "The AS MUST abort processing and respond with an error invalid_authorization_details to the client if any of the following are true of the objects in the authorization_details structure:" and "is missing required fields for the authorization details type."; a pushed authorization request carrying an `openid_credential` object without `credential_configuration_id`, or an empty array, is refused 400 `invalid_authorization_details`; an object carrying a data field the specification does not define, and an `openid_credential` object beside an object of a second registered type, are accepted 201 with a `request_uri`. The `locations` rule: Section 5.1.1 (VCI-058): "If the Credential Issuer metadata contains an authorization_servers parameter, the authorization detail's locations common data field MUST be set to the Credential Issuer Identifier value." and [Section 6.1](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-6.1) (VCI-082): "If the Token Request contains an authorization_details parameter (as defined by [RFC9396]) of type openid_credential and the Credential Issuer's metadata contains an authorization_servers parameter, the authorization_details object MUST contain the Credential Issuer's identifier in the locations element."; under metadata declaring two `authorization_servers`, a pushed object without `locations`, or with a `locations` naming another issuer, is refused 400 `invalid_authorization_details` naming the Credential Issuer Identifier, one naming that identifier is accepted 201, and a Pre-Authorized Code Token Request whose `authorization_details` carries no `locations` is refused 400 `invalid_authorization_details`. - **Trace the servers through a delegate (`a199cc7c`).** The application starts the span of the authorization server's dispatch and of an application's resource decision through a `StartTraceActivityDelegate`, the dispatch hands the delegate the inbound request before the span starts so the application's propagator reads `traceparent` and every `tracestate` line, a 5xx answer sets the dispatch span's status `Error` and `error.type`, the decision span carries the decision's kind, the check that refused and the error code, the library's own resources record each decision as an event on the dispatch span, and no value the library composes for a server operation's attributes and events carries a credential or a value of the presentation; the public surface grows and no existing member is withdrawn or re-signed.
  - **The delegate.** [W3C Trace Context Level 1, Section 2.3](https://www.w3.org/TR/2021/REC-trace-context-1-20211123/#design-overview) (TRC-002): "At a minimum they MUST propagate the traceparent and tracestate headers and guarantee traces are not broken."; [Section 3.4](https://www.w3.org/TR/2021/REC-trace-context-1-20211123/#mutating-the-traceparent-field) (TRC-044, TRC-050): "A vendor receiving a traceparent request header MUST send it to outgoing requests." and "Vendors MUST NOT make any other mutations to the traceparent header." `StartTraceActivityDelegate` (`Verifiable.Core.Diagnostics`) is begin-only: the library calls it once when an operation begins, with the operation's name, `ActivityKind.Internal`, its start tags (an empty list at both operations the library traces through it) and the exchange's context, and it answers a started `Activity` the library completes and stops when the operation ends, or `null` for no span. An activity that was already `Activity.Current` when the delegate was called is the application's own and is never stopped by the library; any other returned activity is stopped whatever trace it belongs to. An exception the delegate throws passes to the caller, and at the dispatch it fails the request before inspection stage 1. The context carries the request's credentials, the `Authorization` and `DPoP` field lines and the body, which the application's tracing must not record. The library does not propagate the trace itself: the application's propagator reads the inbound fields in the delegate and writes the outbound ones in its transport, and the activity the delegate answers is `Activity.Current` for the operation, so the work the operation does runs inside it. Proven over the listener by `TracingDelegateTests.DispatchSpanComesFromTheApplicationsDelegateAsync` (a `client_credentials` token request: one `server.handle` span, started on the application's source as `Internal`, and none on the library's static source), `DispatchSpanContinuesTheInboundTraceContextAsync` (`traceparent`, `TraceParent`) and `DelegateReadsEveryTracestateLineInReceivedOrderAsync` (the delegate called once for the request), `AResourceDelegateAnsweringTheCurrentActivityLeavesItRunningAsync` and `ADispatchDelegateAnsweringTheCurrentActivityLeavesItRunningAsync` (a delegate that answers the activity already current: it is the host's request span, not stopped by the library when the decision or the dispatch returns, and it carries the library's tags) and `ResourceServerCallingTheAuthorizationServerFormsOneTraceAsync` (both servers traced through the application's delegates: one connected tree).
  - **The three homes.** [RFC 9449 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.1) (DPOP-064): "For such an access token, a resource server MUST check that a DPoP proof was also received in the DPoP header field of the HTTP request, check the DPoP proof according to the rules in Section 4.3, and check that the public key of the DPoP proof matches the public key to which the access token is bound per Section 6." and (DPOP-065): "The resource server MUST NOT grant access to the resource unless all checks are successful." `ServerIntegration.StartTraceActivity` is the dispatch's home, a wiring member set during construction or on an alteration candidate and altered under traffic as every setting of the server, so a request is traced by the slot it was admitted under and the next one by the altered slot; `AuthorizationServerIntegration` inherits it, and `null` reads `ServerActivitySource.StartActivity`, which starts the dispatch span on `ServerActivitySource.Source` under the ambient activity and answers `null` when no listener samples the source, so an unwired server's dispatch span carries the tags the dispatch sets on it. `ResourceServerIntegration.StartTraceActivity`, init only, is the slot through which `DpopResourceAccess.DecideAsync` starts `oauth.resource.decide` (`OAuthActivityNames.DecideResourceAccess`): once per decision, after the caller's own arguments are checked, with `ActivityKind.Internal`, no start tags and the caller's context; with it `null` the decision is recorded as the `oauth.dpop.resource.decided` event (`OAuthEventNames.DpopResourceDecided`) on `Activity.Current`. `ServerActivitySource.StartActivity` is the public default, which an application names as a method group when it wants the default explicitly. The library's own resources, UserInfo and the OID4VCI Credential, Deferred Credential and Notification endpoints, record the same event on the dispatch span through `CredentialEndpointDpopValidation.EnforceAsync`, the one place, and their answers are unchanged. Proven over the listener by `TracingDelegateTests.DispatchSpanFollowsTheSlotAcrossALiveAlterationAsync` (the request before the alteration is traced on the library's source, the request after it by the application's delegate), `ApplicationResourceDecisionSpanCarriesTheDecisionAsync` (`no-proof`, `proof-with-the-nonce`: one `oauth.resource.decide` span on the application's source, `Internal`, a child of the host's request span), `AnUnwiredApplicationResourceRecordsEachDecisionAsAnEventAsync` (the slot unset: a refusal for the absent proof, 401, then a permit; each host request span carries exactly one `oauth.dpop.resource.decided` event, `Deny` with `proof_absent` and `use_dpop_nonce`, then `Permit` alone, and no `oauth.resource.decide` span) and `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoDispatchSpanRecordsTheDpopDecisionAsync` (`proof-by-another-key`, `proof-by-the-bound-key`: the dispatch span carries one decision event), and in process by `OAuthDiagnosticsTests.HandleAsyncEmitsActivityWithFlowKindAndStatusCode` (the unwired dispatch span). The OID4VCI endpoints run the same line and carry no row of their own.
  - **The inbound request before the span.** [W3C Trace Context Level 1, Section 3.2.1](https://www.w3.org/TR/2021/REC-trace-context-1-20211123/#header-name) (TRC-004): "Vendors MUST expect the header name in any case (upper, lower, mixed)"; [Section 3.3.1.1](https://www.w3.org/TR/2021/REC-trace-context-1-20211123/#tracestate-header-field-values) (TRC-026, TRC-027, TRC-031, TRC-034, TRC-035): "Tracestate MAY be sent or received as multiple header fields. Multiple tracestate header fields MUST be handled as specified by RFC7230 Section 3.2.2 Field Order.", "When receiving multiple tracestate header fields, they MUST be combined into a single header according to RFC7230." and "Vendors MUST accept empty tracestate headers but SHOULD avoid sending them."; [Section 3.3.1.5](https://www.w3.org/TR/2021/REC-trace-context-1-20211123/#tracestate-limits) (TRC-037): "Vendors SHOULD propagate at least 512 characters of a combined header."; Section 3.4 (TRC-044), quoted above, and [Section 3.5](https://www.w3.org/TR/2021/REC-trace-context-1-20211123/#mutating-the-tracestate-field) (TRC-051): "Vendors receiving a tracestate request header MUST send it to outgoing requests." The dispatch sets the inbound request on the context (`SetIncomingRequest`) before it starts the span, so the application's propagator reads `traceparent` and every `tracestate` line from the request's field lines, each line whole and in received order, an empty line as an empty line, and the field name in any case. The library reads and writes neither field and combines no line; the application's delegate does. The invalid forms are the application's propagator's: [Section 3.2.2.3](https://www.w3.org/TR/2021/REC-trace-context-1-20211123/#trace-id) (TRC-006): "If the trace-id value is invalid (for example if it contains non-allowed characters or all zeros), vendors MUST ignore the traceparent."; [Section 3.2.2.4](https://www.w3.org/TR/2021/REC-trace-context-1-20211123/#parent-id) (TRC-007): "Vendors MUST ignore the traceparent when the parent-id is invalid (for example, if it contains non-lowercase hex characters)."; [Section 3.2.2.1](https://www.w3.org/TR/2021/REC-trace-context-1-20211123/#version): "Version ff is invalid."; [Section 3.3](https://www.w3.org/TR/2021/REC-trace-context-1-20211123/#tracestate-header) (TRC-021): "If the vendor failed to parse traceparent, it MUST NOT attempt to parse tracestate." The sample runs under ASP.NET Core, whose host propagator ignores each of them and its `tracestate`, so the dispatch span the application's delegate starts is in a new trace, neither the presented trace-id nor all zeros, and carries no tracestate; the row pins the host's result, and the library reads no field. Proven over the listener by `TracingDelegateTests.DispatchSpanContinuesTheInboundTraceContextAsync` (`traceparent`, `TraceParent`: the delegate saw the one line, and the dispatch span carries trace-id `0af7651916cd43dd8448eb211c80319c` and tracestate `congo=t61rcWkgMzE`, descending from parent-id `b7ad6b7169203331`), `DelegateReadsEveryTracestateLineInReceivedOrderAsync` (`two-tracestate-lines`: `rojo=00f067aa0ba902b7` then `congo=t61rcWkgMzE`, carried combined; `tracestate-of-512-characters`: one line of exactly 512 characters, whole; `empty-tracestate-line`: one empty line reaches the delegate, and the span continues the presented trace with no list member) and `AnInvalidTraceparentIsIgnoredByTheApplicationsPropagatorAsync` (a zero trace-id; a zero parent-id; an upper-case parent-id; version `ff`).
  - **The status rule.** [OpenTelemetry semantic conventions for HTTP spans](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/specs/semconv/http/http-spans/): "For HTTP status codes in the 4xx range span status MUST be left unset in case of SpanKind.SERVER and SHOULD be set to Error in case of SpanKind.CLIENT."; "For HTTP status codes in the 5xx range, as well as any other code the client failed to interpret, span status SHOULD be set to Error."; "Don't set the span status description if the reason can be inferred from http.response.status_code."; "When instrumentation detects such errors it SHOULD set span status to Error and SHOULD set the error.type attribute."; "If the request fails with an error before response status code was sent or received, error.type SHOULD be set to exception type (its fully-qualified class name, if applicable) or a component-specific low cardinality error identifier."; "If response status code was sent or received and status indicates an error according to HTTP span status definition, error.type SHOULD be set to the status code number (represented as a string), an exception type (if thrown) or a component-specific error identifier."; "The error.type value SHOULD be predictable and SHOULD have low cardinality. Instrumentations SHOULD document the list of errors they report." A dispatch answer of 500 or above sets the span's status `Error` and `error.type` (`ServerTagNames.ErrorType`) to the status code as text, and an answer below 500 leaves both unset; a `Deny`, answered 4xx, leaves both unset on the decision span; an `IndeterminateDeny`, answered 500, sets `Error` and `"500"`; an exception a decision slot raises sets `Error` and `error.type` to the exception type's fully-qualified name, nothing of its text recorded, and clears the three decision tags (the next bullet), so the span carries the error alone, the exception passing on to the caller. The span carries no status description, and `server.response.status_code` stays a string. `ServerTagNames.ErrorType` documents its two values, the status code number and the exception type's fully-qualified name.   - **The decision span's tags.** `oauth.dpop.resource.decision` (`OAuthTagNames.DpopResourceDecision`) is the decision kind's name, `Permit`, `Deny` or `IndeterminateDeny`; `oauth.dpop.resource.check` (`OAuthTagNames.DpopResourceCheck`) is a name of the closed catalog `DpopResourceCheckNames`, internal, 17 names over the 19 refusal sites of `DecideCoreAsync` (13 `Deny` and 6 `IndeterminateDeny`; `jti` names two sites and `validated_proof_incomplete` two), no enum, the list documented on the member; `server.response.error_code` (`ServerTagNames.ErrorCode`) is the answer's error code. All three are set on every decision, an absent check or error code set to `null`, which removes the tag, so a span the application reuses carries the last decision alone; a status `Error` and an `error.type` an earlier decision or the application set are never cleared by a decision. The decision's values are the decision kind's name, a catalog check name, the error code and `error.type` (`500`, or the exception type's fully-qualified name). A value the application's own code hands the library is recorded as the application gave it, at five sites the remarks name: the exception of the application's outgoing-response inspection stage at the dispatch, the exception of the pushed authorization request's transport delegate, the exception of the flow-state release seam, the exception of the shared signals authorization seam, and the denial description the shared signals host answers.
