  - **The sample.** The test tree shows an application composing the delegate and the client side of the trace, and the library has no `HttpClient`. `SampleTracing` is the application's tracing over the delegate: its own `ActivitySource`, `StartServerActivity`, which records the `traceparent` and `tracestate` lines the context carries and starts the span under the ambient activity when the host continued the trace and otherwise under the remote parent its propagator reads from those lines, every `tracestate` line joined in received order, and `StartActivity`, for an operation that runs under an ambient activity. `HttpClientSpanHandler` starts one CLIENT span per attempt to send a request over the wire; `TraceContextPropagationHandler` writes the trace context of `Activity.Current` through the runtime's `DistributedContextPropagator`, replacing any value of a field it writes, one `traceparent` line and at most one `tracestate` line, each name in lowercase; `LoopbackTls.CreateTracedPinnedHttpClient` composes the two over the pinned handler with the runtime's own propagation off, so the application's handler is the one writer of both fields. [OpenTelemetry semantic conventions for HTTP spans](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/specs/semconv/http/http-spans/): "Instrumentations SHOULD create an HTTP span for each attempt to send an HTTP request over the wire."; the client span's attributes http.request.method "Required" "HTTP request method.", server.address "Required" "Server domain name if available without reverse DNS lookup; otherwise, IP address or UNIX domain socket name.", server.port "Required" of type "int" "Server port number." and http.response.status_code "Conditionally Required If and only if one was received/sent." of type "int"; "If the request has completed successfully, instrumentations SHOULD NOT set error.type."; [W3C Trace Context Level 1, Section 3.2.1](https://www.w3.org/TR/2021/REC-trace-context-1-20211123/#header-name) (TRC-005): "and SHOULD send the header name in lowercase." An unbound access token presented under `Bearer` at an application's resource over the traced client, inside a root span carrying `tracestate` `congo=t61rcWkgMzE`, the resource fetching the authorization server's JWKS over the wire inside the request, both servers traced through the application's delegates, is answered 200: the trace is one connected tree with a server span at each host, the authorization server's `server.handle` descends from the resource server's host span, the resource's `oauth.resource.decide` carries `Permit`, every span carries the root's tracestate, the authorization server received exactly one `traceparent` line on the JWKS request whose parent-id is the CLIENT span's, and that CLIENT span holds `http.request.method` `GET`, `server.address` the authorization server's host, `server.port` the listener's port as an `int`, `http.response.status_code` `200` as an `int`, a status left unset and no `error.type`. Proven over the listener by `TracingDelegateTests.ResourceServerCallingTheAuthorizationServerFormsOneTraceAsync`.
  - **Public surface.** Added: `StartTraceActivityDelegate` (`Verifiable.Core.Diagnostics`); `ServerIntegration.StartTraceActivity` (inherited by `AuthorizationServerIntegration`); `ServerActivitySource.StartActivity`; `ServerTagNames.ErrorTypeUtf8` and `ErrorType`; `ResourceServerIntegration.StartTraceActivity`; `OAuthActivityNames` (`DecideResourceAccessUtf8`, `DecideResourceAccess`); `OAuthTagNames.DpopResourceDecisionUtf8`, `DpopResourceDecision`, `DpopResourceCheckUtf8` and `DpopResourceCheck`; `OAuthEventNames.DpopResourceDecidedUtf8` and `DpopResourceDecided`. No existing member is withdrawn or re-signed; `DpopResourceAccess.DecideAsync` is async under its unchanged signature, so its argument exceptions surface when the task is awaited, still before any span. Behaviour under unchanged signatures: the dispatch span's status and `error.type` on a 5xx, the library's resources' decision event, the inbound request set before the dispatch span starts and the catch arm of `DecideAsync`. Proven in process by `OAuthDiagnosticsTests.AllActivityNamesAreNonEmptyAndDotDelimited`, `AllTagNamesAreNonEmptyAndDotDelimited` and `AllEventNamesAreNonEmptyAndDotDelimited` (the name lists carry the added names).
  - **Open.** The decision span's catch arm records a decision cancelled by the caller's token as `Error` with the cancellation exception type's fully-qualified name, where the OpenTelemetry text reads, of an HTTP client instrumentation, "If the HTTP client instrumentation can detect that the request was cancelled intentionally by the caller (e.g., via context cancellation or an abort signal), the cancellation SHOULD NOT be treated as an error: the span status SHOULD be left unset and error.type SHOULD NOT be set." and the sample's CLIENT span leaves a cancellation unset; the owner decides whether the decision span follows it. An exception that escapes the dispatch before any answer leaves the dispatch span's status unset and sets no `error.type`, and the exceptions of inspection stages 1 to 3 are not recorded on it (the exception of stage 4 is recorded as the application gave it); the owner decides whether the dispatch span records them. No row drives a delegate that throws, so the delegate's passing of its exception to the caller and the failing of the request before inspection stage 1 are held by the documentation of the delegate and of the dispatch alone. The sample records `url.full` with its query and fragment, and its scrub of the values `code`, `state` and `request_uri` and its scoping of the propagator's baggage are the planned change T1b. The sample's fallback parser (the `ActivityContext.TryParse` branch of `SampleTracing.StartServerActivity`) has no row with an asserted absent ambient activity, because the ASP.NET Core host continues or ignores the trace itself: the invalid-`traceparent` rows pin the host's result. The CLIENT span's transport-exception case, which sets `error.type` to the exception type's fully-qualified name and the status `Error`, has no row; the planned follow-up adds one. `SdJwtX5cTrustResolver.ResolveAsync` under `Verifiable.Core` records the chain-validation delegate's `SecurityException` and `NotSupportedException` on `Activity.Current` by `AddException`, outside the server operations the closed list scopes.

- **Serve a metadata document per resource (`bd54b3f7`).** UserInfo and the OID4VCI Credential, Deferred Credential and Notification endpoints each serve a protected resource metadata document of their own at the well-known location of their identifier, every URL the resources write into a header or a document is written in its URI form, every `DPoP` and `Bearer` challenge of those endpoints names the document in `resource_metadata` only where it is served and the URL the client used is the endpoint's identifier code point for code point, the `Bearer` challenges carry the realm the `DPoP` challenges carry, the DPoP-bound requirement is stated by the per-resource documents, each resource accepts a token audienced at its own identifier beside the issuer, and the contribution delegate is asked per resource; the public surface gains `IncomingRequest.RequestTarget` and the `endpointName` parameter of the contribution delegate, and nothing else is added, withdrawn or re-signed.
  - **A document per resource.** [RFC 9728 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-3) (PRM-028): "Protected resources supporting metadata MUST make a JSON document containing metadata as specified in Section 2 available at a URL formed by inserting a well-known URI string into the protected resource's resource identifier between the host component and the path and/or query components, if any."; [Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-2) (PRM-012): "resource REQUIRED. The protected resource's resource identifier, as defined in Section 1.2." and (PRM-014), of `jwks_uri`: "This URL MUST use the https scheme."; [Section 1.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-1.2) (PRM-011): "The protected resource's resource identifier, which is a URL that uses the https scheme and has no fragment component."; [Section 3.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-3.2) (PRM-034): "A successful response MUST use the 200 OK HTTP status code and return a JSON object using the application/json content type that contains a set of metadata parameters as its members that are a subset of the metadata parameters defined in Section 2."; [Section 2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-2.2) (PRM-024): "In addition to JSON elements, metadata values MAY also be provided as a signed_metadata value, which is a JSON Web Token (JWT) [JWT] that asserts metadata values about the protected resource as a bundle." Under a registration allowing `WellKnownCapabilityIdentifiers.OAuthProtectedResourceMetadata`, `ProtectedResourceMetadataEndpoints` serves, for each of UserInfo and the OID4VCI Credential, Deferred Credential and Notification endpoints (`ProtectedResourceMetadataEndpoints.LibraryResourceEndpointNames`), a document at the endpoint's URL with `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource` inserted between the host and the path (`WellKnownPaths.OAuthProtectedResource`), the tenant prefix of the path following the suffix ([Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-3.1): "Using path components enables supporting multiple resources per host."), answered 200 `application/json`. Its `resource` is the endpoint's advertised URL, the URL the host's endpoint resolver answers for the endpoint, and the library composes the rest: `authorization_servers` the issuer, `scopes_supported` `["openid"]` at UserInfo (`ProtectedResourceMetadataEndpoints.RequiredScopeOf`) and absent at the OID4VCI endpoints, which require no scope, `bearer_methods_supported` `["header"]` where the requirement of the bullet below is not set (the resources read the `Authorization` field only), `dpop_signing_alg_values_supported` and `dpop_bound_access_tokens_required` (the bullets below), the `jwks_uri` of the JWK Set endpoint's URL, written into this document and the issuer's only when it is https, and `signed_metadata` from the same claims through the application's signer (`AuthorizationServerIntegration.SignProtectedResourceMetadataAsync`), which owns the key, the algorithm and the `iss` claim. An endpoint whose URL is no resource identifier, one not https or carrying a fragment, has no document and its challenges name none. Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoProtectedResourceMetadataDocumentNamesUserInfoAsync` (`requirement-unset`, `requirement-set`, `symmetric-algorithm-listed`: the exact document, parsed and held to the URL requested), `Oid4VciDeferredAndNotificationTests.ProtectedEndpointProtectedResourceMetadataDocumentNamesTheEndpointAsync` (`oid4vci-credential`, `oid4vci-deferred-credential`, `oid4vci-notification`: the exact document, no `scopes_supported`), `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoProtectedResourceMetadataDocumentCarriesTheSignedMetadataAsync` (the signer's JWT in the document, the claims carrying UserInfo's URL, the issuer and `openid`, no `signed_metadata` claim), `UserInfoChallengeNamesNoResourceMetadataForAnIdentifierThatIsNotAResourceIdentifierAsync` (`http-scheme-identifier`, `fragment-identifier`: no pointer, and no 200 at the location) and `ProtectedResourceMetadataTests.DocumentOmitsAJwksUriThatIsNotHttpsAsync` (the issuer's document, whose `jwks_uri` is read the same way).
  - **The URI form of every URL the resources write.** [RFC 3987 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3987#section-3.1): "when an IRI is used for resource retrieval, the resource that the IRI locates is the same as the one located by the URI obtained after converting the IRI according to the procedure defined here." and "Replace the ireg-name part of the IRI by the part converted using the ToASCII operation specified in section 4.1 of [RFC3490] on each dot-separated label, and by using U+002E (FULL STOP) as a label separator, with the flag UseSTD3ASCIIRules set to TRUE, and with the flag AllowUnassigned set to FALSE for creating IRIs and set to TRUE otherwise."; [RFC 9728 Section 5.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-5.1): "resource_metadata: The URL of the protected resource metadata." A URL the resources write is a URL a client dials, so it is written as the URI the IRI maps to: an internationalized host in its IDNA ASCII form and every other character outside US-ASCII as the percent-encoding of its UTF-8 octets, the realm, the `resource_metadata` pointer, `resource`, `authorization_servers` and the well-known location carrying one text. `CredentialEndpointDpopValidation.UriTextOf` (internal) is the one mapping (a URL written in visible US-ASCII is used as written; otherwise the host through `Uri.IdnHost` and the rest through `Uri.AbsoluteUri`), and `UriTextOrNullOf` answers `null` for a relative URI and for a host the ToASCII operation does not map ([RFC 3987 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3987#section-3.1): "The ToASCII operation may fail, but this would mean that the IRI cannot be resolved."). It is read for the realm, for a resource's identifier (`ProtectedResourceMetadataEndpoints.TryResolveLibraryResourceIdentifier`, the text the audience bullet below compares), for the well-known location (`WellKnownPaths.OAuthProtectedResource`), for the comparison of the URL the client used with the identifier (the next bullet), and for the `resource` and `authorization_servers` of the per-resource documents and the `resource` of the issuer's document. So the challenges of an issuer with an internationalized host are answered 401 with an ASCII realm and pointer; an issuer with no URI form is read as no realm in the challenges and as an issuer that does not resolve in the documents (the contribution bullet below). Proven over the listener by `Oid4VciDeferredAndNotificationTests.LibraryResourceUnderAnInternationalizedIssuerNamesItsDocumentInUriFormAsync` (`userinfo`, `oid4vci-credential`, `oid4vci-deferred-credential`, `oid4vci-notification`: a request without credentials under the issuer `https://b` U+00FC `cher.example/tenant` is answered 401 with the realm and the pointer under `xn--bcher-kva.example`), `LibraryResourceDocumentUnderAnInternationalizedIssuerNamesItsUrlsInUriFormAsync` (`userinfo`, `oid4vci-deferred-credential`: `resource` and `authorization_servers` in the URI form), `IssuerDocumentUnderAnInternationalizedIssuerNamesTheIssuerInUriFormAsync` and `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoServesATokenAudiencedAtItsOwnIdentifierAsync` (`internationalized-issuer`).
  - **The pointer and the request-target.** [RFC 9728 Section 5.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-5.1) (PRM-010): "This parameter MAY also be used in WWW-Authenticate responses using authorization schemes other than "Bearer" [RFC6750], such as the DPoP scheme defined by [RFC9449]."; [Section 3.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-3.3) (PRM-037, PRM-038): "The resource value returned MUST be identical to the protected resource's resource identifier value into which the well-known URI path suffix was inserted to create the URL used to retrieve the metadata. If these values are not identical, the data contained in the response MUST NOT be used." and "If the protected resource metadata was retrieved from a URL returned by the protected resource via the WWW-Authenticate resource_metadata parameter, then the resource value returned MUST be identical to the URL that the client used to make the request to the resource server. If these values are not identical, the data contained in the response MUST NOT be used."; [Section 6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-6) (PRM-045): "Comparisons between the two strings MUST be performed as a Unicode code-point-to-code-point equality comparison."; [RFC 9112 Section 3.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9112#section-3.2.1): "origin-form = absolute-path [ "?" query ]" and "a client MUST send only the absolute path and query components of the target URI as the request-target." `IncomingRequest.RequestTarget` (public, `required`) is the request-target exactly as the client sent it, percent-encoding, dot segments and letter case untouched, set by the skin from the server's raw target (in ASP.NET Core, `IHttpRequestFeature.RawTarget`) and never from a request field, for the reason `IncomingRequest.ListenerAuthority` states: `Fields` merges the client's query and body, so a value there is one a client can send, and a value a client chooses would let the client choose the target a request is judged against. `IncomingRequest.Path` is the server's decoded path, the form routing reads. A pointer a client discards names nothing it can use, so every `DPoP` and `Bearer` challenge of those endpoints names the document in `resource_metadata` only where the document is served, an issuer resolves, and the scheme and authority of the listener (`IncomingRequest.ListenerAuthority`, otherwise the issuer), in their URI form, followed by `RequestTarget` are the endpoint's identifier code point for code point, its query included (`CredentialEndpointDpopValidation`, `ResourceMetadataOf` and `IsTargetTheResourceIdentifier`, an ordinal comparison). The pointer is withheld for a listener alias, a query the identifier does not carry, another query or none where it carries one, a percent-encoded or dot-segment spelling of the path, a terminating slash the identifier lacks, and an absolute-form request-target; the `Host` field's spelling (upper case, an explicit default port) takes no part, the authority being the host's configuration or the issuer's. A library resource's document is served only at its location's exact path and query (`ProtectedResourceMetadataEndpoints.FindLibraryResourceAtDocumentLocation` and `IsAtLocation`, `RequestTarget` compared ordinally with the location's path and query): another query, a missing query or another spelling is not that document and the chain walks on, answered 404 where nothing else matches, and two identifiers differing in their query alone have two documents. Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoDpopChallengeNamesUserInfosResourceMetadataAsync`, `Oid4VciDpopCredentialEndpointTests.CredentialEndpointDpopChallengeNamesItsResourceMetadataAsync` and `Oid4VciDeferredAndNotificationTests.ProtectedEndpointDpopChallengeNamesItsResourceMetadataAsync` (`oid4vci-deferred-credential`, `oid4vci-notification`), `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoBearerChallengesCarryTheRealmAndTheResourceMetadataAsync` and `Oid4VciDpopCredentialEndpointTests.CredentialEndpointBearerChallengesCarryTheResourceMetadataAsync` (the `Bearer` challenges, the realm bullet below), `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoChallengeNamesNoResourceMetadataUnderAListenerAliasAsync` (the listener's authority `https://as-alias.test`), `UserInfoChallengeNamesNoResourceMetadataForARequestCarryingAQueryAsync` (`GET <UserInfo>?x=1`, an identifier without a query), `UserInfoChallengeNamesTheDocumentOfAnIdentifierCarryingAQueryAsync` (an identifier ending in `?tenant=t1`, addressed exactly: the pointer written, and a GET of the location answered 200 with the URL the client used as `resource`), `UserInfoIdentifierCarryingAQueryIsNotNamedOrServedUnderAnotherQueryAsync` (`other-query`, `no-query`: no pointer, and 404 at the location), `UserInfoChallengeNamesNoResourceMetadataForAPercentEncodedSpellingOfItsPathAsync` (the request line spells the `u` of `userinfo` as `%75`) and `Oid4VciDeferredAndNotificationTests.ResourcesWhoseIdentifiersDifferInTheirQueryAreServedTheirOwnDocumentsAsync` (`userinfo`, `oid4vci-credential`: each location answered 200 with its own resource's identifier).
  - **A location two documents share.** [RFC 9728 Section 3.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-3.3) (PRM-037): "The resource value returned MUST be identical to the protected resource's resource identifier value into which the well-known URI path suffix was inserted to create the URL used to retrieve the metadata." and [Section 3.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-3.2) (PRM-036): "An error response uses the applicable HTTP status code value." A document located where another document is located names no one resource it could state, the server's own wiring at fault: two library resources whose locations are identical in path and query, and a library resource whose location is the issuer's document's, are answered 500 `server_error` with `WellKnownOAuthErrorDescriptions.ServerNotReady` at that location, and no challenge of the resources involved names it (`ProtectedResourceMetadataEndpoints.IsDocumentLocationShared`, read by `TryLocateLibraryResourceDocument`). The issuer's document is matched by its path alone, so a library location shares it where the location carries no query and its path is that document's. Two identifiers differing in their query alone have two locations and two documents (the previous bullet). Proven over the listener by `Oid4VciDeferredAndNotificationTests.DocumentLocationServingTwoResourcesIsAServerErrorAsync` (`trailing-slash-locations`: UserInfo at `/connect/<t>/resource` and the Credential Endpoint at `/connect/<t>/resource/`, one location under the terminating-slash rule of the well-known location bullet below, UserInfo's challenge then naming none; `issuer-document-location`: UserInfo at `/<t>`, the location of the issuer's own document) and, for the two documents, `ResourcesWhoseIdentifiersDifferInTheirQueryAreServedTheirOwnDocumentsAsync`.
  - **The Bearer challenge's realm.** [RFC 6750 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3): "A "realm" attribute MAY be included to indicate the scope of protection in the manner described in HTTP/1.1 [RFC2617]. The "realm" attribute MUST NOT appear more than once." and "Other auth-param attributes MAY be used as well."; [RFC 9110 Section 11.5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-11.5): "A "protection space" is defined by the origin (see Section 4.3.1) of the server being accessed, in combination with the realm value if present." Every `Bearer` challenge of UserInfo and the OID4VCI Credential, Deferred Credential and Notification endpoints carries the issuer the request is served under as `realm`, as the `DPoP` challenge does, so each resource is one protection space under both schemes, and the `resource_metadata` where the pointer bullet writes it. One composition writes them: `CredentialEndpointDpopValidation.BuildBearerChallenge` writes `realm`, `error`, `scope` and `resource_metadata` in that order through `BearerTokenChallenge.BuildChallenge`, for the refusal of a token (`invalid_token`, a DPoP-bound token presented under `Bearer` included), UserInfo's 403 `insufficient_scope` and its refusal of a token without a subject, the answer to a request without credentials, and the answer to a repeated `Authorization` field, which `RefuseRepeatedField` answers per scheme through the same function (the `Bearer` challenge alone for lines that all name `Bearer`, both challenges for lines of two schemes). The realm is absent where no issuer resolves, as the `DPoP` challenge's is. Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoBearerChallengesCarryTheRealmAndTheResourceMetadataAsync` (`refused-token`, `without-openid-scope`, `no-credentials`, `dpop-bound-token`), `Oid4VciDpopCredentialEndpointTests.CredentialEndpointBearerChallengesCarryTheResourceMetadataAsync` (`no-credentials`, `tampered-token`), `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoAnswersARepeatedAuthorizationFieldNamingUserInfosResourceMetadataAsync` (`two-bearer-lines`, `bearer-and-dpop-lines`) and the refusal rows of `UserInfoEndpointTests`, `Oid4VciDpopCredentialEndpointTests` and `Oid4VciDeferredAndNotificationTests` whose `Bearer` literals carry the realm (40 existing cases).
  - **The requirement stated per resource, and the issuer's contribution as given.** [RFC 9728 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-2) (PRM-005, PRM-007): "dpop_bound_access_tokens_required OPTIONAL. Boolean value specifying whether the protected resource always requires the use of DPoP-bound access tokens [RFC9449]. If omitted, the default value is false." and (PRM-002): "The empty array [] can be used to indicate that no bearer methods are supported."; (PRM-020), of `tls_client_certificate_bound_access_tokens`: "If omitted, the default value is false." `AuthorizationServerIntegration.IsDpopBoundAccessTokenRequiredAtResources` governs UserInfo and the OID4VCI Credential, Deferred Credential and Notification endpoints, so their documents state it: with the flag set each document states `"dpop_bound_access_tokens_required":true` and `"bearer_methods_supported":[]`; with it unset, `"bearer_methods_supported":["header"]` and no `dpop_bound_access_tokens_required`. The issuer's document describes the resource the issuer identifies, which the flag does not govern: it states its contribution as given, `dpop_bound_access_tokens_required` and `bearer_methods_supported` whatever the flag, in the document and in the claims handed to the signer. A library resource's document never states `tls_client_certificate_bound_access_tokens`: the contribution asked for a library resource (the contribution bullet below) supplies the human-readable members alone, and a contribution stating any other member, that one included, is answered 500 `server_error` with `WellKnownOAuthErrorDescriptions.ServerNotReady` and no document (`ProtectedResourceMetadataEndpoints.IsComposedMemberContributed`); the issuer's document relays the application's statement about its own resource as given. Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoProtectedResourceMetadataDocumentNamesUserInfoAsync` (`requirement-unset`, `requirement-set`), `UserInfoProtectedResourceMetadataDocumentContributedAComposedMemberIsAServerErrorAsync` (`names-the-resource`, `names-the-scopes`), `UserInfoProtectedResourceMetadataDocumentCarriesTheNamesContributedForItAsync` (the names contributed for UserInfo written into its document and the issuer's own name and scope into the issuer's, neither the other's) and, for the issuer's document at a server whose resources require DPoP-bound access tokens, `ProtectedResourceMetadataTests.SignedMetadataCarriesTheContributedBearerMethodsUnderTheResourcesRequirementAsync` (a contributed empty `bearer_methods_supported` in the document and the signed claims, no `dpop_bound_access_tokens_required`), `IssuerDocumentStatesOnlyItsContributionUnderTheResourcesRequirementAsync` (a contribution stating neither member: the document states neither) and `IssuerDocumentServesItsContributionUnderTheResourcesRequirementAsync` (`states-not-required`, `names-a-bearer-method`: `"dpop_bound_access_tokens_required":false` and `"bearer_methods_supported":["header"]` written as stated).
  - **The well-known location's derivation.** [RFC 9728 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-3.1) (PRM-033): "If the resource identifier value contains a path or query component, any terminating slash (/) following the host component MUST be removed before inserting /.well-known/ and the well-known URI path suffix between the host component and the path and/or query components." and [Section 1.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-1.2): "As specified in Section 2 of [RFC8707], it also SHOULD NOT include a query component, but it is recognized that there are cases that make a query component a useful and necessary part of a resource identifier." `WellKnownPaths.OAuthProtectedResource` parses the identifier, reads it in its URI form, keeps the path with its terminating slashes dropped (a path of `/` alone written as none) and appends the query as written, never edited: `https://example.com/protected?tenant=t1` gives `https://example.com/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/protected?tenant=t1`, `https://example.com/protected?x=/` gives `https://example.com/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/protected?x=/` and `https://example.com/protected/?v=1` gives `https://example.com/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/protected?v=1`; an identifier with no query gives what the path-insertion rule of the other well-known locations gives. `WellKnownPaths.OAuthProtectedResource.ComputeUri` keeps its signature and answers the IDNA form for an identifier with an internationalized host, a query ending in a slash whole, and a path's terminating slash before a query dropped. Proven over the listener, at an application's resource of the test tree whose challenge names the location and whose document is fetched there, by `ResourceServerChallengeTests.ResourceIdentifierWithAQueryKeepsItAfterTheInsertedSuffixAsync` (`?tenant=t1`) and `ResourceIdentifierEndingInASlashKeepsItsQueryWholeAfterTheInsertedSuffixAsync` (`?x=/`, `/?v=1`).
  - **The audience.** [RFC 9068 Section 4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9068#section-4): "The resource server MUST validate that the "aud" claim contains a resource indicator value corresponding to an identifier the resource server expects for itself. The JWT access token MUST be rejected if "aud" does not contain a resource indicator of the current resource server as a valid audience."; [RFC 9728 Section 7.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-7.4) (PRM-050): "If a client expects to interact with multiple resource servers, the client SHOULD request audience-restricted access tokens using [RFC8707], and the authorization server SHOULD support audience-restricted access tokens."; [RFC 8707 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2): "The authorization server SHOULD audience-restrict issued access tokens to the resource(s) indicated by the "resource" parameter." Each library resource expects two identifiers for itself: the issuer, which its `iss` comparison also resolves, and its own identifier, the text its document names as `resource` (`BearerTokenValidation.ValidateAtLibraryResourceAsync` takes the endpoint name and passes the identifier of `ProtectedResourceMetadataEndpoints.TryResolveLibraryResourceIdentifier` as the second acceptable audience, compared ordinally; the validation an application's resource calls, `BearerTokenValidation.ValidateAsync`, passes none). A token audienced at another resource's identifier, the library's other resources' included, is refused 401 `invalid_token`. Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoServesATokenAudiencedAtItsOwnIdentifierAsync` (`configured-issuer`, `internationalized-issuer`: the pushed request and the token request sent over the wire, the token's `aud` UserInfo's URL alone, UserInfo answered 200 with the subject), `Oid4VciDpopCredentialEndpointTests.CredentialEndpointServesATokenAudiencedAtItsOwnIdentifierAsync`, `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesATokenAudiencedAtTheCredentialEndpointsIdentifierAsync` and the issuer-audience rows `UserInfoEndpointTests.ATokenRequestedWithoutAResourceIsAudiencedAtTheIssuerAndServedAtUserInfoAsync` and `Oid4VciDpopCredentialEndpointTests.ATokenRequestedForTheCredentialIssuerIsServedAtTheCredentialEndpointAsync`.
  - **The contribution per resource and the algorithms.** [RFC 9728 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-2) (PRM-019), of `resource_name`: "The value of this field MAY be internationalized, as described in Section 2.1." and (PRM-017): "No default algorithms are implied if this entry is omitted. The value none MUST NOT be used."; [Section 2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-2.1) (PRM-022): "To specify the languages and scripts, language tags [BCP47] are added to resource metadata parameter names, delimited by a # character." and "it is RECOMMENDED that language tag values used in Claim Names be spelled using the character case with which they are registered in the "Language Subtag Registry" [IANA.Language]."; [RFC 5646 Section 2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5646#section-2.1): "Subtags, in turn, are a sequence of alphanumeric characters (letters and digits), distinguished and separated from other subtags in a tag by a hyphen" and "All subtags have a maximum length of eight characters. Whitespace is not permitted in a language tag."; [RFC 9728 Section 3.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-3.2) (PRM-036): "An error response uses the applicable HTTP status code value."; [RFC 9449 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.1) (DPOP-073): "An algs parameter SHOULD be included to signal to the client the JWS algorithms that are acceptable for the DPoP proof JWT." and [Section 11.6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-11.6) (DPOP-123): "In particular, the algorithm none MUST NOT be allowed." The contribution delegate (`ContributeProtectedResourceMetadataDelegate`) is asked per resource, with the registration, the endpoint's name as its `endpointName`, the context and the token: `WellKnownEndpointNames.ProtectedResourceMetadata` for the issuer's document, the endpoint's name for a library resource's. For a library resource it supplies the human-readable and documentation members alone (`resource_name`, `resource_documentation`, `resource_policy_uri`, `resource_tos_uri` and their language-tagged forms), the tag after the `#` held to the lexical form of a language tag (`ProtectedResourceMetadataEndpoints.IsLanguageTagForm`: one or more subtags of one to eight ASCII letters or digits joined by `-`; the registry is not read); a contribution stating any other member, a tag outside that form among them, is answered 500 `server_error` with `ServerNotReady` and no document. `dpop_signing_alg_values_supported` of both documents carries only the algorithms a proof can be accepted under (`DpopProofValidator.AcceptableProofAlgorithms`) of the list the server holds every proof to, by the rule the resources' `algs` follow (`CredentialEndpointDpopValidation.ResolveChallengeAlgorithmsAsync` and `AcceptableChallengeAlgorithms`: a list holding a name that is no token is read as no list), the member absent when none remains; a library resource's document reads that rule, and the issuer's document reads it where the server validates DPoP proofs and writes the contribution's list as given where it validates none. One answer, 500 `server_error` with `ServerNotReady` and no document, is the server's own wiring at fault: an issuer that does not resolve (the default resolver refusing, an application resolver answering nothing or raising `InvalidOperationException`) or has no URI form, at either document, and a contribution naming `none` among `resource_signing_alg_values_supported`, at the issuer's document. Proven over the listener by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoProtectedResourceMetadataDocumentContributedAMalformedLanguageTagIsAServerErrorAsync` (`resource_name#en#x`, `resource_name#` followed by a space), `UserInfoProtectedResourceMetadataDocumentCarriesARegionTaggedNameAsync` (`resource_name#en-US`), `ProtectedResourceMetadataTests.DocumentOmitsTheDpopAlgorithmsWhereTheHostListsANameThatIsNoTokenAsync` (the host lists `ES256` and `ES 384`), `DocumentPublishesOnlyTheAsymmetricDpopAlgorithmsTheHostListsAsync` (`ES256` and `HS256` listed: `["ES256"]`), `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoProtectedResourceMetadataDocumentNamesUserInfoAsync` (`symmetric-algorithm-listed`: the library resource's document and its challenge name `ES256` alone), `ProtectedResourceMetadataTests.DocumentNamingNoneAmongTheResourceSigningAlgorithmsIsNotServedAsync` (`RS256` and `none`), `IssuerDocumentWhereNoIssuerResolvesIsAServerErrorAsync` (`issuer-unresolvable`, `issuer-resolver-answers-nothing`) and `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoProtectedResourceMetadataDocumentWhereNoIssuerResolvesIsAServerErrorAsync`.
  - **Public surface.** Added: `IncomingRequest.RequestTarget` (`public required string RequestTarget { get; init; }`), set by every skin; code that builds an `IncomingRequest` without it does not compile, and the library builds none under `src`. Re-signed: `ContributeProtectedResourceMetadataDelegate` takes the `endpointName` parameter between the registration and the context (`AuthorizationServerIntegration.ContributeProtectedResourceMetadataAsync`'s type follows). Nothing else is added, withdrawn or re-signed. Behaviour under unchanged signatures: `WellKnownPaths.OAuthProtectedResource.ComputeUri`'s answer for an identifier with an internationalized host, with a query ending in a slash or with a terminating slash before the query; the realm and the `resource_metadata` of the `Bearer` and `DPoP` challenges of the library's resources; the `dpop_signing_alg_values_supported` of the issuer's document; the issuer's document stating its contribution as given; and the answer to an issuer that does not resolve at the protected resource metadata document, 500 `server_error`.
  - **Open.** The issuer's document writes the contribution's `LocalizedParameters` verbatim, so a key naming a typed member (`resource`, `jwks_uri`) writes that member twice ([RFC 8259 Section 4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8259#section-4): "The names within an object SHOULD be unique."), where the documents of the library's resources refuse such a key. An issuer that does not resolve is answered 400 `invalid_request` at the Credential Issuer Metadata (`Oid4VciEndpoints`), the authorization server discovery document (`MetadataEndpoints`), the shared signals transmitter (`SsfTransmitterEndpoints`) and the AuthZen endpoints (`AuthZenEndpoints`), where the protected resource metadata documents answer 500 `server_error`; one rule for the class is the owner's. `CredentialEndpointDpopValidation.TryResolveIssuerAsync` reads an `InvalidOperationException` of the issuer resolver as no issuer, its subclass `ObjectDisposedException` included; the owner decides whether that reading stands. An application contribution that ignores `endpointName` and states a composed member leaves the documents of the library's resources answered 500 while their challenges keep naming them, the pointer reading no contribution. An absolute-form request-target reaching a resource directly is named no document, and no row drives it. The issuer's own document is matched by its path alone, so a library identifier that is the issuer's URL with a query has its own document at its location with the query. The issuer's document writes its `authorization_servers` as the application contributes it, so a Unicode issuer URL contributed there is written in that form. No row contributes `tls_client_certificate_bound_access_tokens`: the refusal for a library resource is the check the rows that contribute `resource` and `scopes_supported` drive, and the issuer's relay of the member is held by the code alone. The OID4VCI `authorization_details_types_supported` member of the per-resource documents is the planned change D2c-a2; the client's reading of a protected resource metadata document is the planned change D2c-b.

- **The DPoP clauses the library does not implement or deviates from, as of `bd54b3f7`.** The clause list holds 134 clauses (124 of RFC 9449 and 10 of RFC 9728 that bear on DPoP): 130 are implemented and tested, 1 is absent, none deviates and 3 are no library clause. The identifiers are those of the clause list. The planned DPoP changes are D2c-b (the client's reading of a protected resource metadata document), D2c-a2 (the OID4VCI `authorization_details_types_supported` member of the per-resource documents) and D2i (the introspection client and the resource server's local binding check, DPOP-057); D2c-a (`bd54b3f7`), D2r (the open clauses, the entry above), D2b (`7ae07edc`), D2a (`c59d4e1d`), D1c (`4bf56546`) and D3 (`5fa3296e` and `9214b8eb`) are landed. Sections 11.4, 11.7 to 11.11 and 12 of RFC 9449, other than Section 12.7.1, hold no RFC 2119 keyword and no conditional that binds a behavior.
  - **Absent, 1.** Each is named with its reason and the change it waits on.
    - (DPOP-057, Section 6.2) "Note that the resource server does not send a DPoP proof with the introspection request, and the authorization server does not validate an access token's DPoP binding at the introspection endpoint. Rather, the resource server uses the data of the introspection response to validate the access token binding itself locally." The library has no introspection client and no local check of a conveyed binding at a resource (the planned change D2i).
  - **No library clause, 3.** DPOP-001 ([RFC 9449 Section 1.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-1.1), the key words of BCP 14: the document's conventions), DPOP-103 ([Section 9](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-9): developers are to take care not to confuse DPoP nonces with the OpenID Connect ID Token nonce: advice that binds no behavior) and DPOP-124 ([Section 12.7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-12.7.1): the registry text of the `nonce` claim).

---

## 9. Token producer selection & identity issuance

The token endpoint issues one JWT per applicable `TokenProducer` per request. Which producers run, and what identity claims they're allowed to carry, is decided across three independent layers rather than one central switch. See `documents/ADRs/token-producer-selection-and-openid-anchoring.md` for the decision record; this section describes the resulting shape.

### 9.1 Endpoint match gates the grant capability

Each of the six token-issuing grants — `authorization_code`, `refresh_token`, `client_credentials`, `token_exchange`, `jwt_bearer`, `pre_authorized_code` — is its own `EndpointCandidate` carrying a `Capability` (`WellKnownCapabilityIdentifiers.OAuthAuthorizationCode` / `OAuthClientCredentials` / `OAuthTokenExchange` / `OAuthJwtBearer` / `Oid4VciPreAuthorizedCodeGrant`). `EndpointChain.MatchAsync` (§2.2) only reaches a grant's `BuildInputAsync` when the registration's resolved capability set allows that capability, so by the time an `IssuanceContext` is constructed the request has already proven the tenant may run this grant at all — the coarsest gate in the pipeline runs once, before any producer is considered.

`IssuanceContext.GrantType` (the wire `grant_type`, from `WellKnownGrantTypes`) carries that grant identity forward into the producer walk. It is not a re-check of what the endpoint match already gated — it's an independent signal producers read for decisions the endpoint match cannot express, because the endpoint match only knows "this tenant may run this grant", not "this grant establishes an authenticated End-User" or any other per-producer semantic the grant identity implies.

### 9.2 Producer selection: optional feature gate + grant-aware `IsApplicable`

`TokenProducer.RequiredCapability` is `CapabilityIdentifier?` — a coarse tenant-*feature* gate, orthogonal to the grant capability the endpoint match already checked. `null` means "not feature-gated at all"; the producer's applicability then rests entirely on `IsApplicable`.

- `Rfc9068AccessTokenProducer` sets `RequiredCapability = null`. An RFC 9068 access token is the default response of every token-issuing grant, and the grant's own endpoint match already gated the request before the producer walk begins — there is nothing left for a second capability check to gate. Its `IsApplicable` is unconditionally `true`.
- `Oidc10IdTokenProducer` keeps `RequiredCapability = WellKnownCapabilityIdentifiers.OidcOpenIdConnect`. Whether a tenant offers ID Tokens at all is a genuine opt-in feature, independent of which grant capability the request matched — a tenant can be `client_credentials`-only and never have OIDC wired at all. Its `IsApplicable` (`IsApplicableAsync`) is `openid ∈ scope AND GrantType ∈ {authorization_code, refresh_token}` — grant-aware, and checked independently of the capability gate (see §9.4).

The walk itself — `IssueTokensAsync` in `AuthCodeEndpoints.cs` — applies both filters per producer, in order: skip when `RequiredCapability` is set and absent from `context.ResolvedCapabilities`; skip when `IsApplicable` returns `false`; otherwise resolve the signing key for the producer's `KeyUsage` via `SigningKeySelection.ResolveSigningKeyIdAsync`, call `BuildAsync`, merge claim-contributor output, and sign. A producer that needs a coarser tenant switch than its own `IsApplicable` logic can express reaches for `RequiredCapability`; a producer whose applicability depends on values already on `IssuanceContext` (scope, grant type, or anything else on the record) expresses it in `IsApplicable` instead. The two are deliberately different axes, not a fallback chain.

### 9.3 One shared issuer

`IssueTokensAsync` is the one producer-walk loop of the six token-issuing grant sites: it owns the `RequiredCapability`/`IsApplicable` filter, the signing material's resolution, the algorithm derivation (`CryptoFormatConversions.DefaultTagToJwaConverter` from the resolved material's `Tag`), `BuildAsync`, the claim-contributor merge, `UnsignedJwt.SignAsync` and the compact serialization, and it returns a `TokenIssuanceResult` (the compact JWS and an `IssuedTokenAudit` of `jti`, `keyid`, `iat` and `exp` per response field, plus the latest `exp` across every token issued in the walk) or a `server_error` `ServerHttpResponse`, either when a producer's signing material could not be resolved, or, with `WellKnownOAuthErrorDescriptions.ServerNotReady` and no token of the walk returned, when a payload's `sub` after the claim-contributor merge names no subject (a blank string for any producer, and for the ID Token and the access token a `sub` that is absent or is not a string; see the section 8 entry "A blank subject is no subject, at every seam that supplies one.").

Each grant site keeps only what is genuinely grant-specific: constructing its `IssuanceContext` before the call, and shaping the response after it. `authorization_code` and `refresh_token` fold the audit dict into PDA state for `BuildResponse`; `client_credentials`, `token_exchange`, `jwt_bearer`, and `pre_authorized_code` write a scalar `expires_in` (read from `IssuedAudits[WellKnownTokenTypes.AccessToken]` rather than tracked separately) plus inline JSON. There is exactly one signing path in the library; a producer added for a new token type is correct at all six sites the moment it is added to `AuthorizationServerIntegration.TokenProducers`.

### 9.4 The `openid` ⇒ end-user invariant

`openid` in a token's scope is supposed to mean "this token identifies an authenticated End-User" — every ID Token and every UserInfo response makes that promise. The library defines its **end-user-authenticating grants** as `{authorization_code, refresh_token}`: the two grants where the library itself established an End-User authentication (refresh continues a prior one). `client_credentials` authenticates a client, not a person — its `sub` is the `client_id`. `pre_authorized_code` establishes no session at all. `token_exchange` and `jwt_bearer` are neither — whether the exchanged or asserted subject is an End-User is a decision the app's own authorization seam makes, not something the library can infer from the grant shape alone.

The invariant is enforced at three independent layers so that a defect in any one of them cannot by itself mint an identity assertion for a non-End-User subject:

1. **Source — scope narrowing.** `DropIdentityScopesForNonEndUserGrant` (`AuthCodeEndpoints.cs`) strips `openid` and the OIDC Core §5.4 identity scopes (`profile`/`email`/`address`/`phone`) from the granted scope before it reaches `IssuanceContext.Scope`, per the RFC 6749 §3.3 narrowing allowance. It runs at exactly the two grant sites with no authenticated End-User by construction — `client_credentials` and `pre_authorized_code` — and emits `OAuthEventNames.IdentityScopesDroppedForNonEndUserGrant` (an `ActivityEvent` tagged with the dropped scopes) when narrowing actually occurs, so a deployment can observe the drop rather than have it happen silently. `token_exchange` and `jwt_bearer` do **not** call it: their authorization seams (`AuthorizationServerIntegration.AuthorizeTokenExchangeAsync`, `ValidateJwtBearerAssertionAsync`) own whether the exchanged/asserted subject is an End-User, and the app opts into identity claims by granting `openid` itself.
2. **Consumer — the ID Token producer.** `Oidc10IdTokenProducer.IsApplicableAsync`'s grant-type check (§9.2) is independent of the scope check — it reads `IssuanceContext.GrantType` directly. Even if a defect ever left `openid` on a `client_credentials` or `token_exchange` token's scope, this producer still declines to synthesize an ID Token unless the grant is one the library itself recognizes as end-user-authenticating.
3. **Consumer — UserInfo.** `UserInfoEndpoints` keeps its pre-existing `openid`-in-token-scope check (OIDC Core §5.3.1), and that check is correct *because* layer 1 guarantees `client_credentials` and `pre_authorized_code` tokens never carry `openid`. A `token_exchange` or `jwt_bearer` token that legitimately carries `openid` is correctly served — the app vouched for the subject by granting it. Absent the source-side guarantee, this check alone would be exactly the failure mode `WellKnownScopes` warns against in its own documentation: an access token issued without `openid` authorizes actions but does not identify a user, and its presence must never substitute for proof of identity.

No single layer is trusted alone. The source layer is the one that actually prevents the leak; the two consumer layers are defense-in-depth against a defect that reaches them anyway.
